# Inside Story: Autoimmune Food & Health App (Project Brief)

> **STOP AND READ FIRST: this project has TWO separate SQLite databases. Before touching either one, especially for any bulk edit or translation work, confirm which one is meant.**
> 1. `assets/data/foods_reference.db` is the LIVE database the shipped app reads from. Any edit here goes live immediately and forces a slow re-import on the user's own phone.
> 2. `unified-database/unified_foods.sqlite` is the separate, newer successor project (see its own section below). Not wired into the app at all.
>
> A session once spent hours hand-translating the LIVE database while the user believed the *unified* one was being worked on, costing roughly 8 hours in one day. Whenever the user says "the new database," "the other database," "the 2nd database," or asks for translation or bulk-edit work without naming a file, **stop and ask which one** before proceeding.

---

## How this document works

This file is the standing brief a new session reads automatically: current status, product direction, architecture decisions, and standing rules. It is deliberately kept small so it fits comfortably in context.

**The full history lives elsewhere and is not auto-loaded:**

- **`docs/CLAUDE-ARCHIVE.md`** holds the complete, unmodified 2.05M-char original of this file: the dated build journal from 2026-07-18 through 2026-08-19, plus the long-form reasoning behind every decision summarized here. Read or grep it when you need the history behind a specific decision. It is not loaded into context automatically.
- **[Inside Story Notion hub](https://app.notion.com/p/3a853652f2728144bde2d78ead5227ea)** has day-by-day decisions and an explicit built-vs-planned reconciliation. New findings get written up there first.
- **[App Development Log](https://app.notion.com/p/3a853652f27281ccb21aeaa9bec8c2ae)** has the full reasoning per change.
- **`unified-database/README.md`** is the authoritative, dated history for that track (1,700+ lines, newest entry at top).

**Keep this file lean.** When a session finishes work, update the Status snapshot below in place and put the long-form account in the Notion log. Do not append to this file. That append-only habit is what grew the original to 2.05M characters and stopped Claude Code from responding at all.

## Status snapshot (2026-08-30)

The app is under active development and substantially built. Current state:

**Most recent (2026-08-30, 1.0.31.1): the `outside_eating_window` flag wired into Trends, closing the one item named as open at the end of the previous session.** Direct request: "Now wire the outside_eating_window flag into Trends."

**What the flag actually records was established first, because it determines what may honestly be claimed from it.** Read directly rather than assumed: it is set in exactly one place, the Meals lens's own add-a-meal form, and only when someone was shown their declared eating window and chose "Add Meal Anyway". It is therefore a count of **deliberate exceptions**, not of meals that merely happen to land outside a window. Confirmed by direct check that the bulk meal-plan paths never set it, which is correct rather than a gap: `resolveMealPlanTimes` moves a generated meal to fit the window instead of booking one outside it, so those paths have no exception to record. Also confirmed: the `meals` table carries no equivalent column, so a meal logged directly on Food is genuinely invisible to this.

**New "Eating Window" lens on Trends.** `getOutsideEatingWindowCountsByDateRange` (`lib/db.ts`) returns per-day `outsideCount` plus that day's `totalMeals`, so a day reads as "1 of 4" rather than a bare number; `getEatingWindowTrendForRange`/`getEatingWindowTrend` (`lib/trendAnalysis.ts`) wrap it into the same `TrendPoint[]` shape every other chart on that screen already uses, plus range totals. Skipped meals are excluded in SQL: a meal deliberately not eaten would otherwise inflate both the exception count and the day's own total.

**Three honesty decisions, each deliberate rather than incidental.** A day with meals scheduled and none outside the window is a real zero and is plotted; a day with no meals at all is left out entirely, matching the rule `getNutrientTrendSeriesForRange` already follows for days with nothing logged. With fasting off, or either window time unset, the lens says so in plain text instead of drawing a chart, since a flat zero line would be claiming compliance with a rule that was never set. And the lens states its own blind spots on screen rather than only in help text: meals logged directly on Food are not counted, and meal-plan meals never appear.

**Framing named directly in the help text:** this is not a compliance score. Eating outside the window for a missed meal or illness is a normal thing to do, and the app already chose to record it rather than refuse the meal; the count exists to be looked at, not kept at zero.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on all three touched files, the bare-text audit still at 0. **Not yet confirmed on-device**, and the specific check that matters: with fasting on, add a meal outside the window via "Add Meal Anyway", then open Trends > Eating Window and confirm that day shows a count of 1.

**Most recent (2026-08-29, versions 1.0.30.21 through 1.0.30.26, every one confirmed on-device): a standing "no text on the tab background" rule enforced app-wide with its own audit script, plus five cards and tiles that showed a number without saying what it meant.**

**1.0.30.21, say capsaicin rather than "spicy".** Direct question: "do we have a list of what spices can trigger a flare for someone with a prostate health problem?" The honest answer is no, and checking showed the app's own label was vaguer than the evidence. The Herati survey names items with measured scores on its -2 to +2 scale (spicy foods -0.61, coffee -0.51, hot peppers -0.44, tea -0.38, chili -0.38); cinnamon, turmeric, cumin, black pepper and garlic were all on the same 176-item questionnaire and none were flagged. Concern relabelled to "Hot peppers and chili (capsaicin)". Also added what that survey found SOOTHING, which most summaries drop: water, herbal non-caffeinated teas, psyllium. The ordinary-tea-aggravates/herbal-tea-soothes split points at caffeine, worth knowing when testing either.

**1.0.30.22, "Worth a look" led nowhere.** It called `router.navigate('/insights')` with no destination, and Insights resets to its lens picker on focus. The count is two different things summed (nutrient gaps, and flagged sub-criteria for tracked conditions), which is why one fixed destination could never be right: it now resolves against whichever the number is made of, and asks with counts named when both contributed. Insights gained `openInsightsLens`, copied from schedule.tsx's own `openScheduleLens` rather than a second mechanism, validated against LENSES so a stale link falls through to the picker.

**1.0.30.23, the standing rule (see Standing rules).** Direct instruction: "No font should ever be directly on the tab background without its own background anywhere in the app... Some of them are headers that should just be a part of the informational box it represents." The check was built before any fix, because a grep cannot answer it: legibility depends on a Text's ANCESTORS. `scripts/audit_bare_text_on_background.js` walks the TypeScript AST and resolves render helpers and components ACROSS files (a chart's labels are bare in its own file and fine because every screen renders it inside a card). It found **449 across 43 files** and now reports **0**. Three remedies in preference order: fold a card's heading inside that card; give standalone text its own surface; fill an outline-only control. Where text belongs together the surface went on the CONTAINER, so a heading and subheading read as one block. Two supporting scripts exist because the same style name is routinely used both inside and outside cards in one file (`add_style_background.js`, `wrap_bare_text_lines.js`).

**1.0.30.24, four cards that showed a number without saying what it meant.** A slow Insights lens now says what it is about to show (reusing the lens's own first help section rather than a second copy that could drift). Home's "Meals logged today" stopped landing on the My Foods menu. The fuel gauge percentages now state what they are: confirmed by reading `analyzeNutrientIntake` rather than assumed, they are food plus supplements logged **so far**, against the whole day's target, so they climb through the day and nothing is projected. The gauge colours became a real gradient (a cool slate at nothing logged blending to green at target, `GAUGE_EMPTY`/`GAUGE_OPTIMAL`/`mixHex` in `constants/colors.ts`), and the over-limit warning keys off `excess_risk` (a published upper limit actually passed), not "over 100%", per "only if it is a real problem". "How You're Feeling" now says what it is: the worst flare or reaction in the last two days, kept on the first screen so an ongoing one is visible without going looking.

**1.0.30.25, two follow-ups.** The lens explanation was tied to the loading state, so only people who waited out a slow lens ever read it and it vanished exactly when the thing it describes appeared: it is now a permanent collapsible header. And `DimensionChart` listed every dimension twice for any condition owning fewer than three (Prostate Health, Gout, PCOS, both diabetes types): below three there is no radar, the plain white rows already print each full name, and the legend reprinted the identical string in muted grey with an empty abbreviation prefix. The legend now renders only alongside a radar, which is the only case it was earning.

**1.0.30.26, Today's Meals.** Direct request: "there needs to be a Today's Meals, and when it opens, each meal will have the recipe available to follow so they can use them to cook the recipe." No existing lens answered it (Meals plans, Past Meals corrects, Home counts, all about the record). New Schedules lens listing the whole day in time order, each expandable to its components' ingredients and steps; the whole day rather than only what was eaten, since you cook before you log. `listTodaysMealsWithRecipes` (`lib/db.ts`) is the data path. **A real bug found on the way, and the reason it would not have worked:** `resolveMealComponent` read `instructions` only for a `'side'`, on stale grounds. `instructions_json` is on all eleven saved-record tables, all eleven get functions map it, and since the 2026-08-26 curated-recipe backfill every builder's "Build This Recipe" fills it in, so salads, soups and handhelds inside a meal were silently dropping steps they had saved. Now generic, like `depthData` beside it. That fix applies anywhere a meal component is resolved, not just the new lens.

**Verification, every version:** `tsc` clean; `eslint` compared against the stashed pre-change tree each time and confirmed at an identical problem count, so none are new; the bare-text audit at 0; and every user-facing string grepped in the real exported bundle before saying it shipped. One verification lesson worth keeping: **a bundle string containing a non-ASCII character (an ellipsis) is stored UTF-16, so a plain ASCII grep reports a false negative** -- check both encodings.

**Most recent (2026-08-29): two standing app-wide text rules enforced at the source, after a morning lost to fixing the same reported problem one screen at a time.** Direct instruction, after the third failed attempt: "I don't want bold font anywhere in the app, I don't care where it is located. And I believe I told you to begin with that all text, no matter where it is located in the app, is to have drop shadows... continuity throughout the app is important."

**Rule 1, no bold anywhere.** All 8 `typography` tiers set to `fontWeight: '400'` (`constants/typography.ts`), so hierarchy is carried by SIZE alone now, plus 27 explicit bold overrides removed across `app/` and `components/`. Verified: zero occurrences of any bold weight remain anywhere in `app/`, `components/`, or `constants/`.

**Rule 2, drop shadow on all text.** `...textShadow` added to 700 text styles that had none, across 63 files, with the typography import added where missing (54 files, plus `timeline.tsx` and `Dropdown.tsx`, which had no typography import at all). Verified by re-sweep: 766 text styles checked, 0 without a shadow. **The one exception, named by the person himself:** TabHub/LensHub `itemLabel` keep `menuLabelShadow`, since that heavier shadow exists precisely because a normal one did not show against that menu's background.

**Why it took three attempts, worth recording so it does not repeat.** The reported symptom was Digest search pills looking "bold and drop shadowed... blurred." Those pills were never bold in code (caption tier, weight 400), which is why every search for a `fontWeight` came up empty and why the fix was wrongly reported as already done, twice. The actual cause was `menuLabelShadow`'s own 4px blur radius applied to 11px text, which smears the glyphs enough to read as thick and blurry. Two lessons, both now in memory: bold is often inherited from a typography preset spread rather than written in the screen's own style (so resolve the EFFECTIVE weight, never grep for `fontWeight`), and when the person says "blurred," suspect the shadow rather than insisting the weight is fine.

**One consequence raised as a question rather than shipped silently:** removing bold flattened Digest's own `**emphasis**` spans to look identical to surrounding prose. Asked directly (leave flat / color it / italic); the answer was to color it. `detailTextBold` renamed to `detailTextEmphasis` and switched to the Digest accent at regular weight, contrast measured at 6.68:1 against the card rather than eyeballed.

**Also this session (1.0.30.8 to 1.0.30.10): the fasting window stopped being a dead end, and midnight stopped being rejected.** Direct request: "what if they missed a meal, or they were sick and need to eat something? Instead of that response being just plain OK and no way to add the meal, have the choice be OK or Add Meal Anyway... It needs to be logged as being outside of the eating window, but kept for the trend info." The eating window was a hard block with one OK button. A fasting window is a goal someone set, not a lock on their own food, and refusing to record a meal they actually ate makes trend data less accurate, not more. Now a real two-option prompt ("Pick Another Time" / "Add Meal Anyway"), backed by a new `outside_eating_window` column on `schedule_items` (conditional `ALTER TABLE`, the established migration pattern) and shown as a plain "Outside eating window" label on the row, not a repeated warning. `updateScheduledMeal` always writes the flag rather than only when true, so rescheduling back inside the window clears it instead of letting one exception permanently corrupt any trend built on it. **Closed 2026-08-30 (1.0.31.1):** the flag was stored and visible but nothing in Trends read it, which was named at the time as real analysis work still to do. Now a dedicated Eating Window lens (`getOutsideEatingWindowCountsByDateRange` in `lib/db.ts`, `getEatingWindowTrendForRange` in `lib/trendAnalysis.ts`).

Separately, `buildTime24` rejected hour `0`, so entering midnight as 0 or 00 failed before any other check could run. Fixed (0 and 12 name the same hour on a 12-hour clock). **A correction worth recording:** that fix was then wrongly presented as the cause of a midnight failure the person had actually hit with 12/00/AM, which genuinely does build 00:00. The real cause of that one is still unknown, so rather than guess a third time, `describeTimeInputProblem` now names the specific field at fault ("AM or PM has not been picked", "the hour reads 25") and says so plainly when every field is valid and it still refuses, rather than blaming the entry.

**Three standing rules saved to memory this session**, all from direct instruction: ask about anything unclear at the moment the request is made rather than guessing and shipping something partial; treat a stated rule as app-wide and enforce it at the source with a verifying sweep, never on just the screen reported; and verify a change is in the exported bundle before telling anyone it exists or where to find it.

**Most recent (2026-08-29): the "apply my meal times to meals already scheduled" action, and four corrections in a row caused by describing where it was instead of verifying where it was.** The action itself (`realignPlannedMealTimes`) was right from 1.0.30.3 onward. Everything that followed was placement and wording, and every round was a correction the person had to make:

1. **1.0.30.3:** built into Schedule's own Meal Plan lens, then described in the handoff message as being in "the Meal Plan area." Profile has a card literally called **Meal Plan**, so that description pointed squarely at Profile while the button sat in Schedule. Direct response: "You said it was in Profile in the Meal Plans section, and now you're telling me you actually put it in one of two completely different places that are not part of Profile." Fair, and entirely self-inflicted.
2. **1.0.30.4:** moved to Profile, but into the **Meal Timing** card. Wrong card. Direct correction: "You were supposed to move it to Profile > Meal Plan, and not where you put it."
3. **1.0.30.5:** moved to **Profile > Meal Plan**, under "Generate My Meal Plan," where it now lives. Moved, never duplicated, so exactly one copy exists.
4. **1.0.30.6:** renamed to "Apply My Meal Timing Changes to Existing Meals," since "Apply These Times" no longer had a "these" to refer to once it left the Meal Timing card. Two stale result messages still saying "the times set above" were caught and fixed in the same pass.
5. **1.0.30.7:** a literal line break after "Changes" plus a `textAlign: 'center'` variant style, since the button centered the text BOX but not the lines inside it. **Confirmed on-device: "I updated and it looks right now."**

**The process change that came out of this, now standing practice:** before telling someone a change exists or where to find it, export the real bundle (`npx expo export --platform android`) and grep the compiled output for the exact user-facing string, plus the expected `APP_VERSION`. That check is what was missing at step 1, and it is what caught, at step 5, that the only remaining hits on an old label were historical changelog entries rather than a stale button. Verifying "is it in the file" is not the same as verifying "is it in what shipped."

**CONFIRMED WORKING on-device 2026-08-29: "I tapped it and the times updated correctly."** This closes the original bug end to end. The chain that started with Lisa's first real test ("the meals aren't following the time settings in profile") is now genuinely resolved: new plans pick up Profile's meal times, and an already-scheduled plan can be corrected in place without regenerating or losing anything.

**One part of this remains untested rather than confirmed**, and it is worth naming instead of folding into the win: the eating-window adjustment inside `resolveMealPlanTimes` (moving a meal whose usual time falls outside a declared fasting window) only fires when fasting is actually enabled with both window times set, and whether that path ran during this test is unknown. The times half is confirmed; the fasting half is confirmed as code and unconfirmed as behavior.

**Most recent (2026-08-29): the meal-time fix was correct and had literally never run, plus the fasting window was the identical oversight one field over.** Direct report after updating Lisa's phone: "I updated my wife's phone and then regenerated her 6 week meal plan and it still isn't picking up on any of the meal information from Profile for meal timing and intermittent fasting." Two genuinely separate causes, both confirmed by reading the source rather than guessed.

**Cause 1, and the reason "regenerate" changed nothing:** `setUpMealPlan` (`lib/db.ts`) skips any date/slot that already has a `status = 'planned'` meal, deliberately, so a half-finished run can resume without double-booking. Pressing "Set Up My 6-Week Plan" again over an existing plan is therefore a genuine no-op for every day already scheduled: the old rows keep their old wrong times. The 2026-08-28 fix was correct and simply never executed for her. Worse, the advice given alongside it ("clear and regenerate") was not real advice, since **no clear action exists in that lens at all** -- that was never verified before being suggested.

**Cause 2, an incomplete fix rather than a new bug:** the fasting eating window was the same oversight one Profile field over. Schedule's own manual add-a-meal form hard-blocks any time outside the declared window, while both bulk paths ignored it entirely, so a generated plan could schedule breakfast hours before someone's window even opened. Fixing the usual meal times last pass without checking the fasting fields sitting in the same Profile card was half a fix.

**Fixed both.** `resolveMealPlanTimes` now returns `{ times, adjustedForFasting }` and, when fasting is on, moves only the meals that genuinely fall outside the window (breakfast to the window's open, dinner just inside its close, lunch at the midpoint), leaving already-compliant usual times exactly as set rather than silently rewriting them. It reuses this file's own already-exported `isWithinEatingWindow` rather than a second copy, caught by `tsc` flagging the duplicate implementation before it shipped. New `realignPlannedMealTimes(fromDate)` fixes a plan that already exists, surfaced as "Update Times on Meals Already Scheduled" in the Meal Plan lens.

**Deliberately an UPDATE of the time, not a delete-and-recreate**, and this mattered: `scheduleMealPlanSlot` creates real saved component dishes plus a meal favorite per slot, so deleting those schedule rows would orphan every one of those favorites (and risk discarding a meal since customized) to fix nothing but a timestamp. The meals are already right; only the clock time is wrong. Only `status = 'planned'` rows are touched, since rewriting when an already-logged meal happened would falsify a record rather than fix a setting.

`tsc` clean project-wide. `eslint` verified against the pre-change tree too: an identical 15 problems before and after, so none are new. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.30.3. **Not yet confirmed on-device**, and the specific check that matters: tap the new Update Times button, then look at Schedule and confirm the existing 6-week plan's meals actually moved to Profile's times.

**Most recent (2026-08-29): OTA updating CONFIRMED WORKING on-device for the first time, and the silent-restart experience it exposed fixed on both ends.** Direct confirmation, the thing the whole prior investigation existed to prove: "I ran the check for updates, and the version number did change." The fingerprint fix below is now genuinely verified end to end, not just in a terminal, and the strictest possible test was the one that passed (the update crossed a deliberate version bump, 1.0.29.9 to 1.0.30.1, the exact thing that used to make an update unreachable). A real supporting confirmation was captured before shipping it too: the fresh build's own fingerprint (`621e8bfc...`) and the update published against it came back byte-for-byte identical, where two updates published minutes apart the day before had not.

**The new report, in the same message:** "When the user checks for updates, the app just restarts. It doesn't give any warning about what is going to happen, or what to do when it starts again, or if an update was applied or if there was any update at all... Please make sure the user is aware of what will happen, or has happened, and provide a informational thing after the update is applied to tell that an update was actually applied, and what did that update include for changes." Correct on every count: the button worked mechanically and communicated nothing.

**Before, in Profile:** picking Check for Updates now hides the busy overlay and asks first, via the same `useConfirmSheet` Backup & Restore's own confirm already uses rather than a second pattern: what will happen (download, then the app closes and reopens on its own), roughly how long, that anything part-way through being entered would be lost, and that a summary of what changed appears when it reopens. Deliberately NOT marked `destructive` (that styling is for data loss, and overstating a restart would be dishonest). "Not Now" cancels cleanly with nothing downloaded. The "Up to Date" path now also names the version actually running and says directly that nothing downloaded and no restart is coming, since "nothing appeared to happen" was itself part of the confusion. The card's own help text now sets that expectation before the tap, not after.

**After, on Home:** a new `announceAppliedUpdate` shows an "Updated to X" summary listing what actually changed, once, on the first load after a new version runs. Backed by a new `constants/releaseNotes.ts` (real per-version, plain-language change lists plus `compareAppVersions`/`getReleaseNotesSince`/`formatReleaseNotesMessage`) and a new `last_seen_app_version` row in the existing `app_meta` key/value table (`getLastSeenAppVersion`/`setLastSeenAppVersion`, `lib/db.ts`). Deliberately compares the running `APP_VERSION` rather than tracking expo-updates' own update ID: that catches BOTH ways a new version actually arrives (the manual button and the automatic check-on-launch), and survives a full native reinstall, which an update ID would not. Someone who skips several updates sees everything that changed in between, grouped by version, not just the newest line. Placed inside Home's own first-load branch right before `markHomeDataReady()`, the exact moment the startup overlay is about to clear, so the popup lands over a ready screen instead of racing the loading gate.

**Three honest calls named rather than papered over:** `constants/releaseNotes.ts` starts at these versions, not at 1.0.0, since writing a plausible-sounding changelog for the roughly 100 earlier versions after the fact would be inventing history, against this project's own evidence-honesty rule; a version with no entry (a purely internal bump, like the 1.0.30.1 test publish) correctly shows nothing and silently moves the marker forward rather than re-triggering every launch. A null `last_seen_app_version` genuinely cannot distinguish a brand-new install from an existing one upgrading into the first version that records this at all, so it shows just the current version's notes in both cases, accurate for the upgrade and harmless for a new install. `showInfoAlert` being stable by construction (`useCallback` with `[]`, documented in `InfoAlert.tsx` for exactly this reason) is what makes adding this to Home's focus-effect dependency array safe rather than a reintroduction of the 2026-08-28 refetch loop.

**A new standing maintenance rule**, written into `constants/releaseNotes.ts` itself: whenever a version bump ships something a person would actually notice, add its entry there in the same pass, the same discipline `APP_VERSION` already carries.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` verified by running it against the pre-change tree too and confirming the identical 6 pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe errors (only line numbers shifted), so none are new. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.30.2. **Confirmed on-device same day: "It works as expected."** The confirmation is unusually strong here because applying this update was itself the test: the new confirm-before-restart dialog and the "Updated to 1.0.30.2" summary both had to work correctly for this version to arrive the way it did.

**Most recent (2026-08-28/29): the actual reason NO EAS Update has ever reached a phone since the fingerprint runtimeVersion policy was set up, found and fixed, plus why a native rebuild is unavoidably still needed once.** Direct on-device report, after the Check for Updates button above shipped: "I have closed and force stopped and reopened 3 times and have no App Updates card." Investigated with real evidence rather than re-guessed, per this project's own standing "no more guesses" rule from earlier this same saga: the two `eas update` publishes minutes apart for the meal-plan-time fix and the Check for Updates button (no native-relevant change between them) came back with two genuinely different runtime-version fingerprints in their own CLI output, `3c9e82c3...` and `d065e58d...`. Neither matched the actual installed build's own fingerprint (`53ddf596...`, confirmed directly via `eas build:list`) either.

Reproduced and isolated locally with `npx @expo/fingerprint fingerprint:generate .`: bumping nothing but app.json's own `version` string (from "1.0.29.8" to "1.0.29.9," no other file touched) changed the computed fingerprint hash. Confirmed twice, both directions. **The real consequence, unnoticed since 2026-08-28 when both were set up the same day:** this project bumps app.json's `version` field on every single request as a standing convention, and the fingerprint policy's default options include that field in the hash -- meaning the runtimeVersion was silently drifting on every request too, so an EAS Update published after ANY version bump could never be compatible with whatever was already installed, and two updates published on two different version-bumped commits could never match each other either. Every JS-only OTA update published since the fingerprint policy went live has very likely never actually reached a phone, the entire premise of "JS changes don't need a full reinstall" was quietly broken from day one of trying to rely on it.

**Fixed the documented, standard way:** a new `fingerprint.config.js` at the project root sets `sourceSkips: ['ExpoConfigVersions']` (`@expo/fingerprint`'s own built-in option, existing specifically to exclude app.json's `version`/Android versionCode/iOS buildNumber from the fingerprint, since a real version string changing on every release is expected and shouldn't alone force a new native build). Verified locally after adding it: the identical "1.0.29.8 vs 1.0.29.9" test that used to change the hash no longer does, confirmed both directions again.

**Named directly, the one unavoidable cost:** the runtimeVersion baked into the two phones' own already-installed APKs was computed before this config existed, so it's permanently whatever it already is. No config change reaching those phones through an update can rewrite what's already compiled into them -- only a new native build, installed once more, actually picks up `fingerprint.config.js` and gives both phones a runtimeVersion that will finally stay stable across ordinary version bumps going forward. This is the same "one more reinstall to switch the mechanism on" pattern as the 2026-08-28 EAS Update setup itself, not a new kind of cost, just a second occurrence of it, this time to fix EAS Update's own foundation rather than to enable it initially.

`node --check` clean on the new config file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.9. A fresh `preview` build was started immediately given how directly this blocks the exact feature being tested. **Confirmed on-device 2026-08-29: "I ran the check for updates, and the version number did change."** Verified under the strictest available conditions rather than an easy case: the update that landed crossed a deliberate version bump (1.0.29.9 to 1.0.30.1), the exact thing that used to make an update silently unreachable. EAS-side evidence matched before it ever reached the phone too, the new build's own fingerprint (`621e8bfc...`) and the update published against it came back byte-for-byte identical, where two updates published minutes apart the day before had not. **OTA updating can now be relied on: a JS-only change reaches both phones without a reinstall.**

**Most recent (2026-08-28): a manual "Check for Updates" button in Profile, direct follow-up question after the meal-plan-time fix shipped.** "If the app is already open, does it fetch the update in the background? If not, is there a way to make that a selection in profile?" The honest answer to the first half: no. EAS Update's default `checkAutomatically: ON_LOAD` only checks for a new JS bundle when the app first launches, never while it's already sitting open in the foreground -- confirmed against the app's own `expo-updates` config, not guessed. That's the whole reason the standing "open once to fetch, close and reopen to apply" two-step exists at all; there was never a background poll to rely on.

New "App Updates" card in Profile (right after Backup & Restore), a single `Updates.checkForUpdateAsync()` -> `Updates.fetchUpdateAsync()` -> `Updates.reloadAsync()` chain behind one button, reusing the same `showBusy`/`showBackupAlert` overlay machinery the ground-theme picker and the Backup card already established rather than a new pattern. Guards for two real, already-encountered cases rather than assuming success: `Updates.isEnabled` is false in Expo Go and in a development-client build with no update channel wired up (both cases this project has actually run into this same week), shown as an honest "not available on this build" message instead of a silent no-op; a genuine check failure (no connection) shows its own distinct message rather than the same generic one. `reloadAsync()`'s own success path tears the JS context down and relaunches on the fresh bundle, matching `handleSelectGroundTheme`'s already-established precedent for the same API.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the touched file (5 pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe errors elsewhere in `profile.tsx`, confirmed via `git diff` to sit outside every line this pass touched). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.8. **Confirmed on-device 2026-08-29**, once the fingerprint fix below actually let an update reach a phone: the button really does check, download, and relaunch. Its own communication gaps (no warning before, no confirmation after) were reported the same day and fixed in 1.0.30.2 above.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): the 6-Week Meal Plan's own bulk scheduler never actually read Profile's meal-time settings, direct report from Lisa's first real on-device test.** "After setting the meal times and everything else in profile and then having it create a 6 week meal plan, she then looked at the schedule and the meals aren't following the time settings in profile." Confirmed directly in the source, not assumed: `setUpMealPlan` and `addMealPlanDayToSchedule` (`lib/db.ts`) had always hardcoded `08:00`/`12:30`/`18:30` for every breakfast/lunch/dinner slot, for every person, regardless of Profile's own `usualBreakfastTime`/`usualLunchTime`/`usualDinnerTime` fields -- the exact fields Schedule's own manual "add a meal" form already reads correctly via `usualTimeForMealType` (`app/(tabs)/schedule.tsx`), just never threaded into either of the meal-plan bulk-scheduling paths. Since the Daily/Weekly Meal Plan's own "Add to Schedule" already reuses `setUpMealPlan` directly (per its own 2026-08-26 entry above), this one fix covers both generators, not just the 6-Week Plan Lisa was testing.

Fixed with a new shared `resolveMealPlanTimes(profile)`, fetched once per call rather than per day/slot (the profile doesn't change mid-loop): uses the person's own stated usual time for each meal, falling back to the same `08:00`/`12:30`/`18:30` defaults these functions always used for anyone who hasn't set a usual time, matching this app's own standing "absence means default" contract rather than forcing a decision nobody's made yet.

**Named directly, not silently glossed over:** this fixes every meal plan generated from now on; it does not retroactively correct a plan already scheduled before this fix landed. Lisa's already-generated 6-week plan keeps its old, wrong times on the days already written to `schedule_items` -- clearing and regenerating that plan is what picks up the correct times on those existing days.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. Published as a plain EAS Update (pure logic fix, no native change) rather than a rebuild. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.7. **CONFIRMED WORKING on-device 2026-08-29**, though not by the route originally expected: this fix only ever applied to newly scheduled meals, and setting a plan up again deliberately skips days already scheduled, so it could never correct an existing plan. What actually verified it was the `realignPlannedMealTimes` action built on 2026-08-29 (Profile > Meal Plan), confirmed directly: "I tapped it and the times updated correctly." The retroactive-correction gap named here originally is therefore closed too, and it was closed by an in-place time update rather than the clear-and-regenerate suggested at the time, which was never real advice since no clear action existed in that lens at all.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): the ACTUAL cause of the multi-minute cold-start stall found and fixed -- a genuine infinite refetch loop in Home's own load effect, not the database, not compression, not the network.** Direct on-device report right after the `noCompress` fix shipped, evidence in hand this time (`Stored 160391168 0%`, confirmed uncompressed): the app still took the same ~2-4 minutes to open. Rather than propose a third theory, added millisecond-precision timing instrumentation to both `getReferenceDatabase()` (`lib/db.ts`) and Home's own load effect (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`), published it as a plain EAS Update (no rebuild needed), and read the actual live device log during a live launch.

**The instrumented log was decisive.** `getReferenceDatabase()` itself resolved in 1ms (`alreadyImported=true`, import genuinely skipped) -- conclusively ruling out the reference database as this launch's bottleneck. Home's own load effect told a different story: `loadSkyData`/`loadDigestConditionScope` resolved dozens of times each, back to back, each one slower than the last (15ms up to 17,000ms+), while `load`/`loadWeekTrend` hit the exact old `NativeDatabase.prepareAsync has been rejected` SQLite-race error within 4ms. Read directly in the source: `load`'s own `useCallback` depended on `userConditionCodes` state -- but `loadDigestConditionScope`, called in the very same `Promise.all` as `load`, is what SETS that state, and `getUserConditions()` returns a freshly-built array every call, a new reference even when the actual condition codes never changed. Every resolution of `loadDigestConditionScope` therefore recreated `load`, which recreated the outer `useFocusEffect` callback, which refired the whole effect, which called `loadDigestConditionScope` again, which set the state again -- a self-sustaining infinite loop, not a slow query. The escalating timings were dozens of overlapping calls piling onto the same SQLite connection, and this is very likely the same pressure behind at least some of the `NativeDatabase.prepareAsync` errors chased across the two days before this was found.

**Fixed at the actual source, in `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`:** a new `userConditionCodesRef` mirrors the state via a plain `useEffect`; `load()` now reads `userConditionCodesRef.current` instead of closing over the state directly, and its own dependency array dropped to `[]` -- stable, like the other 3 functions in the same effect, so `useFocusEffect` now only fires on a genuine focus event, never on the render churn this state update used to cause. The temporary timing instrumentation in both files was removed once the real cause was confirmed and fixed, per its own "remove once found" comment.

Published as a plain EAS Update (`eas update --branch preview --platform android`), no rebuild needed -- a pure JS fix. `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on both touched files (one pre-existing, confirmed-unrelated unescaped-apostrophe error in `index.tsx` left alone, matching this project's own standing precedent for that file). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.6. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: the diagnosis and fix are both directly evidenced from a live device log, but the actual proof is a cold launch completing in seconds with no error, not yet observed after this specific fix.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): the standalone build's own multi-minute "95%" stall, still happening after moving off the dev server, root-caused by pulling the actual installed APK apart and inspecting it directly -- not another theory.** Direct report: after installing the fresh EAS Update-capable build, launching it still took roughly 2 minutes to reach 95% and another minute to complete, with the on-screen version never actually updating either. Direct instruction, after two days of unproven guesses: "I don't want a bunch of guesses."

Confirmed with direct evidence before proposing anything: `adb logcat` during a live launch showed the app's own `DatabaseSetupScreen: hard startup timeout fired after 180000ms` line firing -- the 2026-08-27 safety net was needed again, meaning whatever was slow was still taking the full 3 minutes on THIS build too. System-level `io_stats` log lines showed the app's process steadily reading from disk that whole time with zero application-level log output -- alive and working, not hung. `adb pull`ing the installed APK and inspecting it with `unzip -v` found the actual cause: the reference database is stored **DEFLATE-compressed** inside the app package (`Defl:N`, 160MB down to 34MB, 79% ratio), not stored raw. Every launch needing an import has to decompress the whole 160MB on the phone's own CPU before it's usable -- a slow, entirely local operation, explaining why moving off the live dev server didn't fix this: it was never a network problem for this specific build, nobody had actually looked inside the compiled APK to check.

**Fixed with a standard Android packaging mechanism, not a workaround:** a new config plugin, `plugins/withNoCompressDb.js` (`withAppBuildGradle`, matching this project's own established plugin pattern), injects `noCompress 'db'` into the generated `android/app/build.gradle`'s `androidResources` block, telling Android's packager to store that one file uncompressed so a copy is a plain byte copy instead of CPU-bound DEFLATE decompression. Verified locally before spending a cloud build on it: `npx expo prebuild --platform android --clean` regenerated the local `android/` folder (untracked, disposable, matching this project's own established note about that folder) and confirmed `noCompress 'db'` actually lands in the generated file.

`node --check` clean on the new plugin, `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `node -e` JSON-validated `app.json`. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.5 -- a native config change, so this needs a rebuild (already running), not an EAS Update push; the `runtimeVersion: fingerprint` policy wired up the same day would correctly refuse to serve this as an OTA update to an already-installed, differently-fingerprinted build anyway. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: the diagnosis is directly evidenced, but the actual proof is a fresh install completing in seconds rather than minutes, not yet observed.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): a deliberate architecture shift, direct follow-up to the two-day launch-crash saga: the app now also ships as a standalone EAS build (no live Metro dependency), and EAS Update is wired up so a plain JS/content change no longer needs a full reinstall.** Direct request, after the recurring startup-gate/reimport crashes: install onto both the person's own phone and their wife's for testing. Confirmed technically straightforward: `eas.json` already had a `preview` profile (`distribution: "internal"`, a standalone APK, no `developmentClient` flag) from earlier project setup, never actually used until now. `eas build --platform android --profile preview` (already logged in, project already linked, `4df4360a-6556-4989-8ad3-dcae28961d17`) produced an installable APK link with zero code changes needed -- installed clean on both phones (the second phone's own install recognized as an in-place update of the same signed package, standard Android behavior, not anything built for this). This build embeds every asset (the reference database included) directly, so the class of problem the whole prior 2-day saga was about -- a live database fetch stalling mid-launch -- cannot happen on this build at all; there's no dev server in the loop anymore.

**The named tradeoff, asked about directly and answered honestly:** a standalone build has no live-reload at all -- every change, even a one-line text fix, needs a brand-new EAS build and a fresh manual reinstall, a step back from the dev-client's own instant-JS-reload convenience. Closed most of that gap the same day with EAS Update: `app.json` gained `runtimeVersion: { policy: "fingerprint" }` (Expo's own current-recommended policy, matching the "project fingerprint" step already visible in every `eas build` run) and `updates.url` pointing at this project's own EAS Update endpoint; `eas.json`'s `preview` and `production` build profiles each gained a `channel` (`preview`/`production`); `development` (the dev-client/live-Metro profile) deliberately left without one, since that path is already live by definition and doesn't need OTA. Running `eas build` with this config live also auto-created the `preview` update channel/branch on the EAS project the first time, confirmed in that build's own log output.

**One limitation stated directly, not glossed over:** the builds already installed on both phones predate this config entirely, so neither can currently receive an OTA update -- a new build (already re-running) is required once, specifically to "switch on" update-checking at all; every build after that one should pick up a plain `eas update --branch preview --message "..."` push automatically on next launch, no reinstall. A first build attempt failed on an incidental, unrelated race (this session's own `package.json` edit landing mid-archive, caught as a transient `ENOENT` on a `.tmp` file EAS's own tarball step happened to stat right then) -- not a config problem, confirmed by the retry running clean.

`node -e` JSON-validated both `app.json` and `eas.json` after editing, `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.3. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: this pass's own value only proves out once a future JS-only change actually reaches both phones via `eas update` with no reinstall involved -- worth a deliberate first test of that specific flow once this build lands, not just trusting the config read correctly on paper.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` deliberately re-bumped, this time with the USB fast path actually live.** Direct follow-up, same morning: "It is connected via USB now." `adb devices` confirmed the phone visible over USB; `adb reverse tcp:8081 tcp:8081` was run by hand against the already-running dev server (started 10:31am, already carrying this same morning's console.warn/adb-reverse-in-start-with-qr.js fix, committed 10:12am) rather than waiting for a full server restart. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` restored from the prior entry's temporary revert back to `"20260827080000"`, the same value batch3 originally set -- the fermentation-strain catalog content was never actually removed from the bundled `.db` file, only the version marker that triggers a phone to pull it down, so this is a re-bump of already-ready content, not new work.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.2. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- whether the phone's own dev-client actually connects via the new `127.0.0.1:8081` USB path (fast) or falls back to its existing WiFi connection (slower, but still protected by the prior session's 180-second hard-timeout safety net either way) isn't verifiable from this side; worth confirming directly once the next reimport actually completes.

**Most recent (2026-08-28): the two startup-gate timeout fallbacks from the night before were themselves showing up as a scary, full-screen error, plus a permanent fix for the underlying slow-reimport-over-WiFi problem.** Direct on-device report the next morning, right after the app finally opened: "It opens, but has an error," a LogBox screen reading "DatabaseSetupScreen: hard startup timeout fired after 180000ms; forcing the loading overlay closed regardless of Home's own load state" -- literally this session's own diagnostic message from a few hours earlier, working exactly as designed, but shown via `console.error`, which triggers a full-screen red LogBox overlay in a dev-client build. A safety net firing successfully isn't a crash; both timeout messages (the 120s reference-db gate and the 180s hard fallback, `app/_layout.tsx`) switched to `console.warn`, which still logs for a developer to notice without alarming whoever's holding the phone.

**The console message firing at all confirms the reimport took the full ~180 seconds even after the same-night `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` revert** -- meaning that revert likely didn't actually skip anything, most plausibly because this phone never held a clean, successfully-completed import at the reverted-to version to begin with, given how many launches in a row failed across the same evening. Rather than guessing at another version number, the underlying environmental cause got a permanent fix instead: `scripts/start-with-qr.js` now runs `adb reverse tcp:8081 tcp:8081` automatically whenever a device is already connected when the dev server starts, tunneling Metro's own port over USB. Purely additive (no adb, no connected device, or the reverse call itself failing all skip silently; the existing WiFi QR code is untouched) -- but when a reimport is ever needed again, opening the app via `http://127.0.0.1:8081` while connected by USB now gets the same transfer that measured "100+ seconds" over WiFi back on 2026-08-11 finished in under a second, instead of continuing to hope a future reimport lands within whatever timeout window happens to be configured.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `node --check` clean on the new script logic. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.29.1 (day segment advanced: this is the first request of a new calendar day). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-27): `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` temporarily reverted to unblock a phone that was still stuck at 95%, direct follow-up to the timeout fix a few minutes earlier.** Direct, urgent report: "I reopened it and it's still stuck at 95%," then "This has happened before" -- recognized by the person as a repeat of an already-bad pattern, not a new problem to layer another guessed fix onto while they were actively locked out of the app entirely.

Rather than a third timeout-based workaround, `lib/referenceDbVersion.ts`'s `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` was reverted from `"20260827080000"` back to `"20260827060000"` -- its own value immediately before this session's two same-day bumps (the fermentation-strain catalog work). `getReferenceDatabase()`'s own already-correct "skip the reimport once it's genuinely not needed" check (see this file's own 2026-08-11 entry) now finds a version match against whatever this phone already successfully imported earlier the same day, before this session's fermentation-strain work began, and skips the slow network fetch entirely -- no timeout to wait out, no reimport to hope finishes.

**The cost, named directly rather than hidden:** the fermentation_strains catalog upgrades (batch2: 7 to 18 strains, searchability; batch3: ferment guidance, time-to-effect) are still fully present in the bundled `.db` file itself -- only the version marker that would trigger this specific phone to pull them down is reverted. They will not show up on this device until a later, deliberate re-bump, ideally attempted over USB or a strong connection, or once the underlying slow-reimport-over-Metro-on-WiFi problem itself has a real fix, not just another downstream timeout worked around. The hard 180-second safety-net timeout added minutes earlier stays in place regardless, as protection against any future genuine hang, not undone by this revert.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.28.7. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- the person needs to force-close and relaunch once more to pick this up.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): a second gap in the startup gate's own "fails open" design closed, direct follow-up to the SQLite-race fix a few hours earlier.** Direct on-device report: "It loads to 95% and stalls forever wihtout moving."

Ruled out the SQLite-race fix itself as the cause before touching anything: `DatabaseSetupScreen`'s own progress ring visibly climbing to and holding at 95% means React is rendering and re-rendering fine, which could not happen if `getGroundThemeSync()`'s new synchronous file read (shipped a few hours earlier) were genuinely hanging the JS thread at module-eval time -- that would block before any component ever renders at all, not produce a live, updating progress screen.

**The cause, traced by reading `app/_layout.tsx` directly rather than guessed:** this session's own two `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumps earlier today (the fermentation-strain catalog work) force a reimport of the ~130MB+ reference database on the next launch -- already a known slow path over a live Metro dev-client connection on WiFi (a 2026-08-11 entry in this same file measured "100+ seconds" that way versus "under a second" over USB). The startup gate's own existing 120-second timeout (built for exactly this) DOES force `referenceImportResolved` true if the import is still pending -- but that only lets the app tree, including Home, start MOUNTING; it does not free Home's own first-load effects from the SAME underlying, still-pending `getReferenceDatabase()` promise. `DatabaseSetupScreen`'s own overlay only clears once `referenceImportResolved && homeDataReady` are BOTH true, so if Home's own load stays blocked on that same import (which it will, since Home genuinely needs reference data), the overlay never clears no matter how long someone waits -- the "fails open" promise this gate exists for was only ever kept for half of the actual wait.

**Fixed with a second, longer safety-net timeout** (`app/_layout.tsx`, `REFERENCE_DB_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS + 60_000` from mount, so 180 seconds total): if the whole combined wait still isn't done a full 60 seconds after the first gate already gave up, the loading overlay is forced closed regardless of whether Home's own load ever reports success. Home may show its own honest empty/still-loading state underneath rather than real data yet, but that's a genuinely usable app someone can back out of and retry, not a screen with no way forward at all. This closes a pre-existing design gap (not something introduced by today's SQLite fix), triggered today by the double version bump but not caused by it.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.28.6. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- the person needs to force-close and relaunch the app to pick up this fix at all, since the currently-stuck instance is running an already-loaded JS bundle from before this shipped.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): the recurring launch-crash SQLite race fixed at the source, not retried around a third time.** Direct on-device report, screenshot attached: "I don't know what you did, but now this error happens when the app loads," showing an uncaught promise rejection -- "Call to function 'NativeStatement.finalizeAsync' has been rejected... Cannot use shared object that was already released" -- hours after this same session's own earlier "retry once" fix (commit `c1e8699`) for the identically-worded `NativeDatabase.prepareAsync` crash from that same morning.

Confirmed directly rather than assumed fixed: the retry added that morning wraps `initializeDatabase()`'s own first statement, but this new crash is an unhandled promise rejection with no app code anywhere in its own call stack (`_construct`/`wrapNativeSuper`/`callSuper`/`CodedError.ts`, all inside `node_modules`) -- it was never going to be caught by that retry regardless of which native function threw it. Both crashes trace to the same design: `getGroundThemeSync()` (`lib/visualPreferences.ts`) opening a SECOND, synchronous SQLite connection to the same `inside_story.db` file `getDatabase()` already holds asynchronously, needed because `constants/colors.ts` has to resolve a ground-theme value synchronously at module-load time, before expo-router's own route-table build reads it. This has now been patched three times across two calendar days (2026-08-19: don't hold the sync connection open; 2026-08-27 morning: retry once if it still races) without actually eliminating the two-connections-to-one-file design that causes it.

**The actual fix: stop opening that second SQLite connection at all.** `groundTheme` is now mirrored into a small plain text file (`ground_theme_mirror.txt`, `Paths.document`) every time it's genuinely known -- both when `getVisualPreferences()` loads it and when `setVisualPreferences()` saves it -- via expo-file-system's synchronous `File.write()`/`textSync()`, which touches no SQLite state at all. `getGroundThemeSync()` now reads that file instead of opening a database connection, so there is no second connection left to race against `getDatabase()`'s own. The one accepted tradeoff, named directly: a device upgrading into this fix, or a fresh install before the mirror has ever been written, shows the default ground color for a single launch before self-correcting the moment `getVisualPreferences()` runs its own load and writes the mirror for every launch after -- a visible but minor, temporary, non-crashing cost, not a repeat of the native race it replaces. The `initializeDatabase()` retry from that morning is left in place as general resilience against any other, rarer trigger of the same native error class, its own comment updated to say so rather than still claiming to be the primary fix.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.28.5. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- given this is the third attempt at the same underlying bug, worth a real, deliberate check across several cold launches, including at least one right after a fresh install/reimport, before trusting this closed.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): the Cultures & Probiotics picker rebuilt from pills to a plain checkbox list, direct on-device follow-up to the same day's own info-icon fix.** Direct report, right after the (i)-icon-on-a-pill version shipped: "I see it, but it isn't intuitive at all." Correct: cramming two independent tap targets (the pill's own text to select, a tiny 15px (i) icon to view detail) into one 8px-tall chip was the actual problem, not a missing feature -- nothing about that layout signaled which zone did what.

Rebuilt as a plain list, one row per strain, reusing the exact checkbox-row shape this same file's own `renderFavoriteToggle` ("Also save as a Favorite") already established rather than inventing a new pattern: a checkbox on the left is the ONLY thing that toggles selection, the row's own name/subtitle area opens that strain's full detail (`buildStrainInfoMessage`, unchanged from the same-day info-icon fix) via `showInfoAlert`, and a trailing chevron visually marks that area as "tap for more," matching an already-familiar mobile convention (an email client's own multi-select mode, an iOS Reminders list) rather than a bespoke one. Each row also shows a one-line `use_cases` subtitle so the row itself hints at what a strain is for before ever tapping in. The search box, the "Can these be combined?" link, and the selected-strain recap block below are all unchanged -- only the pill grid itself was replaced.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.28.4. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-27): the Cultures & Probiotics picker gives an informed answer to "how do I actually use this," not just a name and a citation, plus a fix for the premade-recipe menu's own slow, silent load.** Direct report, quoting it back exactly what the problem was: "there are a bunch of pill selectors with probiotic names on them as if a person knows right off the bat what each one is good for, how long it needs to ferment, whether it can be fermented with other probiotics, and at what temp to ferment it... The user should be provided with the ability to understand everything they need to in order to make an informed decision," alongside a separate, same-message report that the 2 yogurt curated recipes above the Fermentation Name field took "a good 30 seconds" to populate with nothing shown on screen in the meantime.

**3 new columns on `fermentation_strains`** (`scripts/add_fermentation_strains_batch3.py`, following the same conditional-`ALTER TABLE` pattern the two prior strain-catalog patches already established): `ferment_role` (`'starter'` only for S. thermophilus and L. bulgaricus, the two Codex Alimentarius cultures that actually set milk into yogurt together; `'supplement'` for every other strain, none of which reliably do that alone), `ferment_guidance` (the temperature/duration for the 2 starters, confirmed via WebSearch against yogurt-making sources: about 108-115F/42-46C for 6 to 12 hours; a shared note for supplement strains that the standard yogurt temperature is gentle enough for most live cultures, with the risk being milk scalded hotter than that before culturing; and two strain-specific exceptions named directly rather than forced into guidance that doesn't apply -- L. mesenteroides, whose role per its existing description is starting sauerkraut, not dairy, and S. boulardii, a yeast that doesn't ferment milk into yogurt texture at all), and `time_to_effect` (how long the strain's own already-verified citation actually ran -- 4 weeks for L. rhamnosus GG, 2-3 weeks for L. reuteri, 8 weeks for B. coagulans and B. longum 35624, 3-6 weeks for B. lactis, 12 weeks for L. casei Shirota/L. gasseri/L. paracasei, 30 days for L. helveticus, 14 days per phase for L. salivarius, prenatal-plus-6-months for B. breve -- left blank rather than guessed for the handful of strains whose citation doesn't report a trial duration).

**A new shared fact, not per-strain data:** `COMBINING_STRAINS_NOTE`, confirmed via direct fetch of a 2024 randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial (Open Forum Infectious Diseases) that combines 7 Lactobacillus species, 5 Bifidobacterium species, Bacillus coagulans, and Saccharomyces boulardii in one product -- the same genera this catalog covers, direct evidence that combining several of these strains is studied, not an invented reassurance.

**The picker itself decouples "learn about a strain" from "select it."** Every pill is now a plain `View` wrapping two independent tap targets: the label still toggles selection exactly as before, and a new (i) icon opens that strain's full detail (description, ferment guidance, time to effect, evidence tier, and a tappable citation link, one shared `buildStrainInfoMessage()` function so the inline recap and the info popup can never disagree) via the same `showInfoAlert`/`linkifyText` pattern Digest's own citation links already use, so reading about a strain no longer requires picking it first. A new "Can these be combined? Tap to learn more." link sits above the pill list for the shared combining-strains fact.

**The premade-recipe menu's own gaps, addressed directly rather than glossed over.** A minor inefficiency was fixed: the menu used to call `listCuratedRecipes('fermentation')` (all ~49 fermentation recipes) and filter down to the 1-4 a given subtype needs on the JS side; a new `getCuratedRecipeSummariesByIds()` (`lib/db.ts`) fetches only the rows actually wanted via `WHERE id IN (...)`, in the caller's own declared order. Named directly: a ~49-row scan was never really what a 30-second wait looks like, so the more likely cause is the one-time reference-database reimport this session's own recent `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumps would force on the next launch, an already-documented, self-resolving cost, not something guessed away without evidence. What WAS a confirmed, fixable bug regardless of root cause: nothing at all was shown on screen while the menu loaded, a blank-card gap that reads as broken rather than loading -- a new `loadingRecipeMenu` state now shows a spinner plus "Loading recipes…" for that whole wait.

`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped. `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.28.3. **Not yet confirmed on-device**, and the premade-recipe wait specifically is worth a real re-check after this update actually lands, since the true cause was reasoned about rather than measured.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): every remaining bold-plus-drop-shadow text style in the Digest fixed, not just the search-result pills that prompted the report.** Direct report: "The search result pills again have bold and drop shadowed font instead of just drop shadowed. Go through all of the Digest and make sure that there is not any font anywhere that is both bold and drop shadowed. If you find any anywhere on any information screen or anywhere, remove the bold so there is ONLY drop shadowed font."

A full sweep of every `...menuLabelShadow` use in `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx` (14 total) found the 3 real pill-text styles (`matchTermPillText`, `crossConditionPillText`, `searchResultCategory`) were already correctly caption-weight with no bold, matching the rule a 2026-08-23 pass already established for pills specifically -- but 12 other styles, never covered by that earlier, pill-scoped pass, were spreading a bold typography preset (`typography.label`/`bodyEmphasis`/`eyebrow`/`screenTitle`) together with the same shadow: `categoryHeaderText`, `digestTopicMenuItemLabel`, `shelfHeading`, `shelfCardTitle`, `cardTitle` (the actual title text `SearchResultCard` renders for each search result, the closest literal match to what was reported), `demoHeading`, `demoSubheading`, `detailLabel`, `dynamicActionButtonText`, `recipeNutritionLabel`, `citationsLabel`, `relatedLabel`. Each now carries an explicit `fontWeight: '400'` override placed after the typography spread, so the preset's own fontSize/letterSpacing survive but its weight doesn't, leaving the shadow alone to carry legibility against the photo background.

Checked and confirmed clean elsewhere, not assumed: `components/DigestChart.tsx` (the one other component Digest renders text through) has a bold heading but no shadow anywhere, so no combo exists there; `components/TabHub.tsx`/`components/LensHub.tsx` (the shared hub-menu labels, reachable from Digest's own lens picker but not Digest-specific code) were both already caption-weight with a shadow, no bold, needing no change.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): the Cultures & Probiotics catalog in Fermentation Builder expanded from 7 strains to 18, and made searchable by purpose, not just by name.** Direct request: "they need to be able to have a full list of the possible choices for which probiotic to add to the yogurt culture, and they need to be able to search for the probiotic they need to use for whatever it is they are trying to accomplish."

Confirmed the LIVE database (`assets/data/foods_reference.db`) was the right one to touch before starting, since this is a shipped app feature, not the unspecified bulk-edit/translation case the standing two-database warning exists for. `scripts/add_fermentation_strains_batch2.py` (new, idempotent, following `scripts/add_interaction_rule_mechanisms.py`'s own conditional-`ALTER TABLE` pattern for adding columns to an already-populated reference table) adds 4 new nullable columns to `fermentation_strains` (`use_cases`, `evidence_tier`, `citation_source`, `citation_url`) and upserts all 18 rows: the original 7 backfilled with those 4 fields (reusing the exact citations their own dedicated `lib/digest/fermentedFoods.ts` entries already carry, no new research needed there), plus 11 new strains chosen for being the most commonly used, best-documented probiotic strains beyond the original 7, the ones someone browsing by purpose (immune support, IBS, infant colic, oral health, mood/stress, weight management, cold prevention) is likely to be looking for.

**Every one of the 11 new citations was independently verified by direct fetch of the paper's own page** (title, journal, year, and key finding cross-checked, not just trusted from a search-tool summary), the same discipline this app's Digest content already holds itself to. Evidence tiers assigned honestly per strain rather than uniformly: `strong` only for L. reuteri DSM 17938 (a 6-RCT meta-analysis on infant colic) and B. lactis HN019 (a systematic review and meta-analysis on immune function in older adults); `moderate` for a single RCT (L. rhamnosus GG, B. coagulans, B. longum 35624/formerly B. infantis 35624, L. casei Shirota, L. gasseri SBT2055, L. helveticus R0052, L. salivarius WB21, L. paracasei 8700:2); `weak` for B. breve M-16V, whose own cited eczema-prevention study is open-label, not blinded or placebo-controlled, named directly in its own description rather than smoothed over. Three strains tested in the cited trial as part of a two-strain combination, not alone (L. helveticus + B. longum R0175, L. paracasei + L. plantarum HEAL9, B. breve + B. longum BB536), say so directly in their own description.

`lib/db.ts`'s `FermentationStrain` type and both `listFermentationStrains`/`getFermentationStrain` widened to carry the 4 new fields (nullable, `evidenceTier` narrowed to the same `'strong' | 'moderate' | 'weak'` vocabulary `lib/digest/types.ts`'s `EvidenceTier` already uses, not a second invented scale). `components/FermentationBuilder.tsx`'s Cultures & Probiotics section gained a search box (matches strain name OR the new `use_cases` field, so typing "ibs" or "infant colic" now works, not just typing a scientific name); an already-selected strain never disappears from the filtered list just because a later search narrows past it, so a pick can never get silently stranded with no visible pill left to un-tap. The selected-strain detail block now also shows its evidence tier and a tappable citation link (`Linking.openURL`, matching Digest's own `CitationsBlock` convention), surfacing evidence that existed as structured data for the first time rather than leaving it invisible.

A writing-style check caught 3 filler-word/dash violations in the original 7 strains' own descriptions (carried over unedited since 2026-08-14, before this app's writing-style rules existed) while this same pass was already touching those rows, fixed here rather than left standing, matching this app's own established practice of cleaning up a stale violation whenever a session is already there rather than walking past it.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): a launch crash, the morning after a version-bump-heavy day, root-caused to a recurrence of an already-documented native SQLite race.** Direct on-device report: "I restarted the dev server and downloaded a new copy v1.0.27.25. I got the error above without opening anything," with a screenshot reading "Call to function 'NativeDatabase.prepareAsync' has been rejected... Cannot use shared object that was already released."

**Confirmed directly, not re-guessed, by reading `lib/visualPreferences.ts`'s own comment:** this is the exact same error text a 2026-08-19 fix already diagnosed and partially fixed. `getGroundThemeSync()` (`constants/colors.ts` needs a real ground-theme value before first render, which can only be read synchronously at module-load time) opens its own real, synchronous connection to the same `inside_story.db` file `getDatabase()` also opens asynchronously moments later, then closes it immediately -- but expo-sqlite's own native layer can apparently still hand the next async open a connection tied to that already-released native resource, since a JS-level `closeSync()` finishing doesn't guarantee the native side has fully torn the object down first. The 2026-08-19 fix narrowed this race without eliminating it; it recurred here most likely because yesterday's own multiple `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumps forced a real, slow database reimport on this exact launch, shifting the relative timing of everything else at startup enough to reopen the window.

**Fixed as a real, pragmatic resilience measure, since perfectly synchronizing expo-sqlite's own sync and async native bridges isn't something fixable from this app's own JS layer.** `initializeDatabase()` (`lib/db.ts`) now catches this specific, unambiguous native error signature on the very first real statement this app issues against the main database, discards the corrupted connection, and retries the whole sequence exactly once with a genuinely fresh native connection, rather than continuing to reuse the one that already broke. Since `getReferenceDatabase()` already calls `initializeDatabase()` first, this protects that downstream path too, not just the main database's own callers.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION`/`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.26. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is exactly the kind of fix that most needs a real check, and specifically needs confirming across a few cold launches rather than just one, since the original race was itself intermittent (present since 2026-08-19, only now actually hit).

**A real efficiency confirmed before writing a single recipe, not assumed.** Checked directly: every AIP and Mediterranean breakfast recipe from the two phase-1 batches was already safe (green or yellow, never red) for IBD and Chronic Kidney Disease too, since neither condition's own real relevant sub-criteria (IBD: Additives, Excess Fiber or Anti-Nutrients, Oxalate x3, Processing; CKD: Protein Density, Oxalate x3) ever fire on whole meat, egg, dairy, or vegetable ingredients. Since every AIP recipe also auto-earns the Paleo tag, closing the GENERAL AIP-breakfast gap (17 of 30, uniform across all 19 conditions) directly closes Paleo-breakfast for Hashimoto's/IBD/CKD (21 of 30 each) in the same motion, rather than three separate targeted batches.

The audit's own full "every diet tag x condition x meal" sweep, all 570 combinations, now reads **"None -- every (diet, condition, meal) combination already has 30+."** This closes Open Next Steps item 20 in full, the last remaining item from the same-day cross-condition equity audit.
