**Most recent (2026-08-27): Hashimoto's healing stages extended to actually account for progress, throughout the entire stock of system recipes.** Direct question: "Hashimoto's has a lot of difficulties, but many are also overcome as the user gets through the different stages of healing. Are we accounting for that throughout the entire stock of system recipes?"

**The honest answer, investigated directly, not assumed.** Checked `lib/healingStage.ts`'s own `FOOD_RELEVANT_HEALING_STAGES`: only Digging and Gut Repair ever produced any food advisory at all. Rebalancing and Maintenance -- the two later stages this app's own already-published stage descriptions describe as "less about strict food rules" and "maintaining what's already working" -- produced zero food-stage output whatsoever, meaning someone who had fully healed and reintroduced everything saw the exact same red-flagged recipe list as someone just starting Digging. A second, real, separate finding along the way: this app already has a genuine per-food reintroduction tracker (`food_trials`, statuses waiting/trialing/cleared/flagged, reached from Log), but it has never been connected to condition scoring, recipe severity, or "Meals You Can Eat" anywhere -- a real, substantial, separate feature (turning one person's own specific cleared trial into a broader "this trigger is resolved for me" signal) named directly as a genuine follow-up, not attempted in this same pass, since automatically loosening a caution based on personal trial history is a real, health-safety-adjacent design decision deserving its own scoping, not a quiet side effect of a stage-coverage fix.

**Fixed at the source, applying automatically to the whole recipe corpus.** `FOOD_RELEVANT_HEALING_STAGES` extended to include `rebalancing`/`maintenance` (Triage stays excluded, correctly -- it's about initial symptom relief and hormone stabilization, not diet, per the model's own real framing). `lib/healingStageAdvisory.ts`'s `getHealingStageAdvisory` (the one shared dispatcher every builder's live advisory, `RecipeDepthReport`, and the offline recipe-condition compute script's own faithful mirror all route through) now gives Gluten/Dairy/Nightshade -- the genuine elimination-diet reintroduction triggers Digging/Gut Repair exist to test -- a real, softened, stage-appropriate message for these two later stages: naming that food choices matter less at this point, and that something already reintroduced without a reaction doesn't need to keep being avoided, while still naming the food as an active concern if it was never tested or did cause a reaction. Goitrogenic(Raw)/Additives/Processing deliberately keep firing with the identical message at every stage, since they're not reintroduction-dependent sensitivities: raw goitrogens are a biochemical iodine-uptake mechanism cooking resolves regardless of healing progress, and food-additive/processing quality doesn't improve just because someone reached remission.

**A real, deliberate architecture choice, not an oversight, once investigated:** severity (`conditionCautions`/`safeForConditions`, the actual green/yellow/red classification and sort order) stays completely unchanged by declared stage, matching this feature's own standing "advisory and reordering only, never gating" rule from day one -- only the advisory TEXT and its own within-tier reordering vary by stage, exactly as they already did for Digging/Gut Repair.

**Wired through the entire stock, verified, not assumed.** Since `compute_recipe_condition_data.js`'s own stage-note loop is already generic over whichever stages a condition's own `STAGED_CONDITIONS` entry lists, adding the two new stage codes to Hashimoto's entry there was the only change needed to generate real notes for all 372 recipes at once -- confirmed directly: stage-advisory notes generated rose from 997 to 1,301. A new, genuinely idempotent `scripts/apply_new_stage_advisory_notes.js` (the existing `apply_recipe_condition_data.js` is already documented as unsafe to re-run, since it unconditionally appends every computed note rather than checking for one already present) appended 438 real new notes across 211 recipes, confirmed idempotent by running it a second time and getting zero. A self-caught writing-style slip ("genuinely unresolved," a banned filler word) was found and fixed in both the new source text and the 152 instances already written into `recipes.ts`, via a precise, verified string replacement rather than a second, unsafe re-run of the apply script.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file except the confirmed pre-existing `scripts/*.js` pattern. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped; no reference-database schema or data changed, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump needed this time. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth a real check: declare Rebalancing or Maintenance for Hashimoto's in Profile, open a gluten- or dairy-flagged recipe under "Meals You Can Eat," and confirm the new, softer stage note shows instead of the Digging/Gut Repair one.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): a real, previously-unknown live-resolution bug found and fixed while answering a direct architecture question, plus a first, real batch closing part of the AIP/Paleo breakfast shortage.** Direct question: "Can the way of how foods will be consumed, meaning how it will be cooked before consuming it, be determined in the app by the way the user has chosen for them to be cooked, or as they are meant to be cooked in the system recipes?"

**The honest answer, investigated directly rather than assumed.** For a food someone picks by hand in any of the 11 direct-ingredient builders' own `FoodLookup` picker, yes -- this reference database stores real, distinct rows per prep method (e.g. "Broccoli, boiled" and "Broccoli, baked" are different rows), and `FoodLookup` already lets a person pick the specific one, feeding straight into scoring. For a curated ("system") recipe, the honest answer was no, confirmed by reading `getCuratedRecipe`/`resolveCuratedRecipeIngredient` directly: `curated_recipe_ingredients.cooking_method` was already stored and already shown in a recipe's own customer-facing ingredient text ("broccoli, baked"), but the actual function resolving which real food row to use for nutrient and condition-score data ignored it completely and always tried 'Raw' first -- meaning the ACTUAL data used every time anyone tapped "Build This Recipe" for ANY curated recipe with a cooked ingredient was silently the raw version, not the cooked one the recipe's own real instructions describe. A different, more fundamental bug than the already-known `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` mechanism (`scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js`) fixes -- that script only ever patches the offline-computed Digest text, never what the live app actually resolves and saves into a person's own dish record.

**Fixed at the source** (`lib/db.ts`): `resolveCuratedRecipeIngredient`/`resolveCuratedRecipeIngredientUncached` now accept the real, stored `cooking_method` and, when it's set, prefer any visible real non-Raw `prep_method` row for that ingredient (a case-insensitive substring match against the real prep_method vocabulary first, since a recipe's own free-text wording like "braised" or "sauteed" never exactly matches this database's own short, capitalized vocabulary; falling back to any non-Raw row otherwise) -- this app's own already-confirmed finding (2026-08-25/27) that every checked ingredient scores identically across its own real non-Raw variants makes this a correct, general rule rather than a per-recipe guess. A null `cooking_method` (the dish genuinely serves it raw) is untouched, falling through to the exact same Raw-first behavior as before. The cache key was widened to include cooking method, since the same (category, base_name) pair can now legitimately resolve differently between a recipe that cooks it and one that doesn't. Named honestly: the separate offline `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` script still needs its own hand-maintained entries going forward (it's a plain Node script that can't import this new TS logic), and reconciling the two into one mechanism is a real, scoped, not-yet-done follow-up, not attempted in this same pass.

**Then, moving to the next backlog item: Open Next Steps item 20, phase 1.** 15 new AIP-compliant breakfast recipes (`scripts/add_hashimotos_aip_breakfast_batch.py`), reusing the same verified-clean protein/vegetable palette confirmed earlier the same day for the lunch/dinner batch (salmon, halibut, cod, shrimp, beef top sirloin, pork tenderloin; broccoli, cabbage, kale, carrot, fennel, zucchini; avocado), plus 3 fruit-only no-cook bowls. Sweet potato was considered and directly ruled out by database query before writing anything: it carries a real "Mineral Binding Risk: High" flag for Hashimoto's across every prep method (Raw/Baked/Boiled/Canned/Fried alike), the same still-open, named data question from this app's own 2026-08-26 history, not resolved here. 9 of the 15 needed a matching `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` entry each for the same cooked-vegetable pattern found in the lunch/dinner batch, added before shipping rather than after. The known `apply_recipe_diet_tags.js` duplication bug recurred a fourth time (372 -> 744 dietTags lines) and was fixed the same established way.

**The real, honest result, verified by `scripts/audit_meal_plan_recipe_coverage.js`, not assumed:** AIP breakfast improved from 2 to 17 recipes, uniformly across all 19 tracked conditions (confirming this really is a content gap, not a per-condition safety gap); Paleo breakfast improved to 21 (Hashimoto's/IBD/CKD) or 25 (every other condition), since every AIP recipe also auto-earns the Paleo tag. Named directly, not oversold: this is a real, phased first batch, not a full closure -- AIP breakfast still needs roughly 13 more recipes to clear 30, Paleo needs 5 to 9 more, and Mediterranean breakfast (4-16, needing its own separate olive-oil/no-red-meat ingredient palette entirely untouched by this batch) hasn't been started at all yet.

**Most recent (2026-08-27): checked whether every other tracked condition got the same depth of attention Hashimoto's just did, found the honest answer is more nuanced than a plain yes/no, and closed a real, verified ingredient-reference bug found along the way.** Direct request: "I want to make sure that all of the other conditions have received the same amount of focus as Hashimoto's did. If they have, then let's tackle the next item on the list, if they haven't then they deserve it so that should be next."

**The real audit, not assumed from the breakfast work alone.** The prior pass only ever checked ONE diet tag (Vegan) combined with each condition. `scripts/audit_meal_plan_recipe_coverage.js` extended to check EVERY real diet tag (Vegan, Vegetarian, Omnivore, Plant-Based/Flexitarian, Mediterranean, Gluten-Free, Dairy-Free, Paleo, AIP, High-Protein) against every condition and every meal -- 570 combinations total. A real bug was caught and fixed in this new check before trusting its own first result: the base-tier diet hierarchy (Vegan/Vegetarian/Omnivore, where a Vegan recipe also satisfies a Vegetarian or Omnivore preference, and literally everything satisfies Omnivore) wasn't modeled, making "Omnivore breakfast: 0 for every condition" and several "Vegetarian" numbers look like real gaps when they were actually just the check counting wrong -- fixed by mirroring `lib/digest/types.ts`'s own real `BASE_DIET_TIER_RANK`/`recipeMatchesDietPreference` logic exactly.

**The honest, corrected finding: other conditions have NOT been under-served -- if anything, Hashimoto's itself was still the one lagging.** Every condition already clears 30+ for the condition-alone check and for vegan+condition+breakfast (the specific combination just fixed). But checking every diet dimension surfaced two genuinely different things: (1) Hashimoto's itself still had real gaps below 30 for Vegan/Vegetarian/Paleo/AIP **lunch and dinner** (never addressed by the breakfast-only work) and for High-Protein breakfast specifically; (2) a real, structural, condition-agnostic shortage of AIP (2), Mediterranean (4-16), and Paleo (6-10) breakfast recipes affecting **all 19 conditions equally** -- not favoritism toward or against any one condition, a genuine corpus-wide content gap for those three strict diets specifically. Presented both findings directly and asked which to prioritize; direct answer: close Hashimoto's own remaining gaps first.

**A real, genuine bug found and fixed while investigating, not just more recipes added.** Of the ~100 vegan/vegetarian/paleo/AIP lunch-and-dinner recipes not safe for Hashimoto's, bucketing by actual flagged sub-criterion found most are correctly flagged (soy, gluten, genuinely raw goitrogenic salads/slaws/wraps where raw greens are the whole point of the dish, real high-oxalate foods, turmeric/pepper/cumin's real iron-contextual effect) -- but 9 recipes (7 lentil dishes, a Mediterranean chickpea salad, a lima bean salad) were flagged "Lectins (Legumes): High Risk" for a genuinely fixable reason: each referenced an under-specified legume `base_name` ("Lentil, green/red, hulled, dry", "Chickpea", "Lima Bean") that has **no real cooked or raw variant to redirect to at all** in this database -- confirmed directly, not assumed, by querying every prep_method row for that exact base_name. A richer base_name for the identical real food ("Lentils", "Chickpeas (garbanzo beans, bengal gram)", "Lima beans, large") already exists elsewhere in this app's own recipes and does carry a real Boiled/Canned variant, confirmed to score Lectins as Mild Risk (yellow, safe) instead of High Risk (red) once resolved that way. Fixed at the source: `scripts/fix_legume_ingredient_refs.sql` switches the ingredient reference itself (not just a prep-method override, since the old base_name genuinely had nothing to override to), paired with matching `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` entries in `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js` selecting the real prep method each dish actually uses (Boiled for a simmered soup/stew/meatball/meatloaf, Canned for a salad).

**The result, verified, not assumed:** Vegan+Hashimoto's lunch and dinner improved from 24 to 26 each; Vegetarian+Hashimoto's dinner improved from 26 to 28. Named honestly, not oversold: this did not fully close the gap to 30 -- the remaining shortfall (Vegan/Paleo/AIP lunch+dinner still below 30, Vegetarian dinner still 2 short) is now attributable to genuinely, correctly flagged content (soy, gluten, raw-salad goitrogens, real oxalate/mineral-binding foods), not further bugs to hunt. Fully closing it would need a genuinely new lunch/dinner recipe-authoring batch built from a similarly narrow, verified-clean palette (matching the breakfast work's own scope and effort), a real, separate next step, not assumed done here.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (the standing pre-existing `__dirname`/`no-undef`/unused-var pattern on `compute_recipe_condition_data.js` and the two `scripts/*.js` files, unchanged, confirmed pre-existing). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` and `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped (9 recipes' own ingredient references and condition-safety data both changed). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.21. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26/27): the Hashimoto's+vegan+breakfast gap closed, 5 to 32 recipes, via a genuinely narrow, verified-clean ingredient palette.** Direct request: "Let's tackle the Hashimoto's-vegan-breakfast batch next," with a shared Google AI Mode conversation of generic vegan breakfast ideas for creative reference. The AI Mode conversation's own ideas (tofu scrambles, nut-butter toasts, bean hashes, chia pudding, nutritional-yeast scrambles) were checked directly against Hashimoto's real 24 relevant sub-criteria before writing anything, not assumed usable just because they're vegan -- every single one turned out unusable, for reasons well beyond the already-known oxalate/phytate issue: **every real cooking oil row in this database (olive, coconut, avocado) is flagged Omega-3-vs-6 Imbalanced or Oxidation Risk Imbalanced for Hashimoto's specifically** (whole avocado itself scores clean; pressed oils don't), and **turmeric, black pepper, and nutritional yeast** (a very common savory-breakfast trio, already used in several of this app's own existing vegan breakfast recipes) are independently flagged under Iron (contextual) and Zinc. Confirmed by direct query, not assumed.

**The real, verified-clean palette this left standing, checked ingredient by ingredient against the live database:** whole fruit (roughly 20 kinds), oats/rice/sorghum/cornmeal/tapioca as the only clean whole-grain-family bases (millet, quinoa, buckwheat, and amaranth are all independently oxalate- and/or mineral-binding-flagged), coconut milk and whole avocado as the only clean fat/creaminess sources, maple syrup as the only clean sweetener, and a handful of clean vegetables (zucchini, bell pepper, tomato, leek, fennel, cilantro, fresh ginger) for genuinely savory options. A real, honest tradeoff follows directly: with every common plant-protein source (legumes, nuts, seeds, soy) independently flagged, these recipes run genuinely lower in protein than a typical vegan breakfast -- named directly, not hidden, a real property of what the narrowest possible reading of "genuinely clean" actually allows.

**26 new curated recipes** (`scripts/add_hashimotos_safe_vegan_breakfasts.py`, 18 first, then 8 more the same day closing the remaining gap): oat/rice/sorghum/polenta/tapioca porridges and puddings across a real variety of fruit pairings, three no-cook fruit bowls and a smoothie, and four genuinely savory rice/polenta bowls cooked entirely without oil (a splash of water standing in for a saute, whole avocado standing in for richness where wanted) -- a real, workable technique this pass confirmed works, not a compromise. Every one of the 35 distinct ingredients used was individually verified against the live database before being written in, and again after, matching this app's own standing discipline.

**A real, previously-latent bug in `curated_recipes.instructions` found and fixed while wiring these in**, not just written around: `getCuratedRecipe()` never selected the new instructions column at all in this exact codepath's own newest builder-integration surface, though this was because the column had only existed since a few hours earlier the same day (the "steps aren't showing up" fix below) -- confirmed already correct once checked, no further fix needed there. A real, separate, second bug WAS found and fixed here: `apply_recipe_diet_tags.js` duplicated `dietTags` across all 342 recipes on this run, the exact same pre-existing bug this project has hit and fixed twice before -- caught immediately via an exact-count check (342 expected, 684 found) before trusting anything, fixed the same way each prior time, by keeping only the first `dietTags` line per `recipeCard` block. A third, smaller but real bug was found and fixed in `scripts/audit_meal_plan_recipe_coverage.js` itself: its own `BREAKFAST_ELIGIBLE_RECIPE_IDS` extraction used a naive quote-matching regex that silently swallowed real ids whenever an ordinary apostrophe (e.g. "Hashimoto's") appeared in a `//` comment inside that same array, hit twice in this same pass before being hardened to strip comment lines before matching -- a real, permanent fix, not just a one-off dodge.

**The honest, verified final result**, confirmed by `scripts/audit_meal_plan_recipe_coverage.js` (not assumed from this batch's own reasoning): Hashimoto's+vegan+breakfast coverage is now 32 of the confirmed 30-minimum bar (was 5); Hashimoto's breakfast overall (any diet) is now 43 (was 17, already past 30 after just the first 18). Every one of the 19 tracked conditions now clears 30+ recipes for every meal type, both alone and combined with vegan -- the audit script's own closing line: "None -- every condition already has 30+ for every meal type." Every new recipe's own `linkedCuratedRecipeId` was also added to `lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`'s `BREAKFAST_ELIGIBLE_RECIPE_IDS`, the real, separate hand-maintained list the actual meal generator reads (a genuinely easy step to forget, caught directly by the audit numbers not moving on the first attempt rather than assumed done).

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two `scripts/*.js` files carry the same pre-existing `__dirname`/`no-undef` pattern every other one in this project already has). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` and `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped (the live database and the Digest's own recipe text both changed). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.20. Closes Open Next Steps item 15 in full. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth a real walkthrough: generate a Hashimoto's, vegan, breakfast-only Daily Meal Plan a few times and confirm real variety shows up across these 26 new recipes, not just the same one or two repeating.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): the real, deeper bug behind "the steps aren't showing up" found and fixed for real, across every Food builder, not just Fermentation.** Direct, on-device report after the fix below shipped: "I restarted the server and downloaded the newest version. The steps aren't showing up for a fermentation called Wild-Fermented Blueberry, Ginger & Turmeric Tonic. I assume they wouldn't for any of them. It says none added yet." Correct on both counts, and a real, deeper bug than the one actually fixed below.

**The real root cause, confirmed by reading the function itself, not re-guessed.** `getCuratedRecipe()` (`lib/db.ts`), the one shared function every Food builder's own "Build This Recipe"/curated-recipe-pick flow calls, never selected or returned `instructions` at all -- its SQL only ever asked for `name, flavor_profile, health_benefit, servings, serving_size_amount, serving_size_unit`. The `curated_recipes` table itself had no `instructions` column to select in the first place: the real, hand-written, non-plagiarized steps for all 316 curated recipes have only ever existed as TypeScript content in `lib/digest/recipes.ts`, never migrated into the database the builders actually read from. This meant the earlier same-day fix (`setSteps(recipe.instructions ?? [])` in Fermentation Builder) was reading a field that was always `undefined` -- a real fix in intent, applied to data that was never there, so it could never have worked. It also meant `handleStartCuratedRecipeToday` ("Start Today," which saves `instructions: recipe.instructions ?? []` directly to a new fermentation) had been silently saving an empty step list this whole time, a genuinely pre-existing bug, not something this session introduced. And confirmed directly, not assumed: `SideBuilder.tsx` had the identical pattern already named in its own code comment ("A curated recipe has no real hand-authored steps of its own to start from... reset to empty") -- a prior session had already discovered and worked around the same root gap, just never closed it at the source.

**Fixed at the source, benefiting every builder at once.** A real `instructions TEXT` column added to `curated_recipes` (`ALTER TABLE`), backfilled for all 316 rows from `lib/digest/recipes.ts`'s own real `RecipeCard.instructions`, matched by `linkedCuratedRecipeId` via the real TypeScript compiler API (`scripts/backfill_curated_recipe_instructions_extract.js`, walking the actual AST rather than a fragile regex over 15,000+ lines of prose full of every kind of quote and apostrophe) -- verified as an exact 316-to-316 match in both directions (every database recipe had a matching Digest entry, every extracted entry had a matching database row) before writing anything. `getCuratedRecipe()` now selects and JSON-parses this column, with a real `RecipeDepthResult`-style graceful fallback (`try/catch`, falling back to "no steps" rather than throwing) if a row's own JSON is ever malformed. `handlePickCuratedRecipe` across all 11 direct-ingredient builders (Fermentation already had the line from the earlier, ineffective fix; the other 10, including Side Builder's own stale one, were updated the same mechanical way) now correctly carries `recipe.instructions` into `StepsEditor` the moment a curated recipe is picked.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two new one-off scripts carry the same pre-existing `__dirname`/`no-undef` pattern every other `scripts/*.js` file in this project already has). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped (the live database's own schema and 316 rows of real content both changed); `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` unchanged (no Digest content itself changed, only where it's already-stored data now also lives). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.19. **Confirmed on-device**: "Confirmed working on my phone, the steps show up now" -- the same Wild-Fermented Blueberry, Ginger & Turmeric Tonic that originally surfaced the bug.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): filtered water pushed corpus-wide with a real tap-water advisory, the Fermentation Builder's own pointless "no oil" nudge removed, a curated fermentation's real steps now actually carry over, and "Worth Knowing If You Have..." made tappable across all 11 direct-ingredient builders.** Three direct reports in one message.

**(1) Filtered water, everywhere, with a real reason why.** "When using water for system recipes it should always be filtered water, and a warning about using tap water based on their area possibly having included chloramine and/or chlorine that could be in it and unknown levels of other things that could easily kill beneficial bacteria. However, this app promotes the use of filtered water and should always continue to push that." A handful of fermented-drink recipes already said "filtered, unchlorinated water" from an earlier session, real, existing precedent this pass generalized rather than reinvented. A scripted, builder-type-aware pass (tracking each recipe's own `linkedBuilderType` line-by-line) brought every other plain-water ingredient line across the corpus (42 lines: soups, baked goods, sauces, snacks, beverages, plus a few fermentation outliers that had drifted from their own siblings) up to the same standard: "filtered, unchlorinated water" for fermentation recipes specifically (the chlorine/chloramine-kills-bacteria mechanism), plain "filtered water" everywhere else (a real quality practice worth promoting even where survival of a live culture isn't the question). Coconut water, water kefir grains, and watermelon were correctly left untouched, real, different ingredients the word "water" just happens to also appear in. A new Digest entry, `fermented-filtered-water` (Fermented Foods, Basic Health), covers the actual mechanism with real citations (US EPA on chloramine's own multi-day persistence and resistance to ordinary filters, a practical fermentation source on chlorinated water's effect on cultures), cross-linked into the category's existing tying-together synthesis. A new `TAP_WATER_RULE` in the shared `lib/generalHealthRules.ts` registry (the same extensible mechanism already covering alcohol/coffee/juice/raw-meat) fires a real, cited advisory the moment anyone manually adds a genuine tap-water ingredient (Water, tap / tap, drinking / tap, municipal / tap, well -- not bottled or coconut water) to ANY builder, not just when reading a curated recipe's own already-fixed text.

**(2) The Fermentation Builder's own "No cooking oil or fat logged for this fermentation yet" nudge, removed.** Direct report: "I don't understand why this screen is here for a fermentation. It should just go to the final steps screen." Confirmed as a real design mismatch, not a bug: this nudge (checking for a Fats/Herbs ingredient before letting someone finish) was copy-pasted from the other 10 direct-ingredient builders, where "did you forget the cooking oil" is a genuinely common silent-error source for a cooked dish -- but most real fermentations use neither, so the check could never answer anything meaningful for this builder specifically. Removed outright for Fermentation Builder alone (the other 10 builders' own identical nudge is unaffected and still correct for what they're checking): picking "Done adding ingredients" now goes straight to the real final review screen, matching the direct request exactly.

**A second, related, genuinely separate bug found in the same investigation.** "But for system recipes, for all system recipes, there is supposed to be a premade set of steps written so they are not plagiarized but convey the correct steps." Checked directly: `handleStartCuratedRecipeToday` ("Start Today") already saved a curated recipe's own real, hand-written instructions correctly. `handlePickCuratedRecipe` ("Customize the ingredients first") never did -- it loaded the recipe's name, servings, and ingredients, but silently dropped the real instructions, leaving `StepsEditor` blank and forcing the person to write their own steps from scratch for a recipe that already had real ones. Fixed with one added line (`setSteps(recipe.instructions ?? [])`), still fully editable from there like any other field.

**(3) "Worth Knowing If You Have..." made genuinely tappable, without losing your place mid-build.** "They are in the middle of creating their hydration through the fermentation, and they might want to read the warning without losing their place in the build. No user should ever be enticed by information and not be provided a way to read it and then to get back to what they were doing." Traced to `getConditionNotesForIngredients` (`lib/db.ts`), the always-visible live preview shown on every direct-ingredient builder's own final review screen (distinct from the richer, optional "Preview Full Report" -- this simpler one already used `getFoodScoresForCondition` internally but discarded its real `relevanceNote`/`citation` fields, the same enrichment data Insights' own Condition Scores lens already surfaces, keeping only a generic templated sentence. `ComponentConditionNote` widened to carry both through when a real one exists for whichever sub-criterion actually triggered the flag. New shared `components/ConditionNoteRow.tsx` replaces 11 near-identical hand-copied render blocks (one per direct-ingredient builder): a note with real relevance data becomes tappable ("Tap to learn more"), opening the same real, cited explanation via the in-place `showInfoAlert` overlay every other "tap to learn more" affordance in this app already uses (DimensionFlags, GeneralHealthAdvisories, the healing-stage row) -- never navigation, so reading it can never risk losing whatever's mid-build on screen. A note with no real relevance data yet renders as plain text, no tap affordance offered where there's genuinely nothing more to show. Named directly, not built: a fuller version linking to the SPECIFIC Digest article a claim traces back to, rather than the same relevance/citation text Insights already shows, would need mapping every sub-criterion to a long-form entry -- a real, separate, larger project.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (14 files: `lib/db.ts`, `lib/digest/recipes.ts`, `lib/digest/fermentedFoods.ts`, `lib/generalHealthRules.ts`, `components/ConditionNoteRow.tsx` new, plus all 11 direct-ingredient builders). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` unchanged -- the live database's own ingredient rows weren't touched, only `lib/digest/recipes.ts`'s customer-facing text; `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped, since that text plus the new `fermentedFoods.ts` entry are real Digest content changes. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.18. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth a real walkthrough: pick a curated fermentation recipe via "Customize the ingredients," confirm its real steps show up pre-filled and the finish flow skips straight to the final review; manually add a tap-water ingredient anywhere and confirm the new advisory fires; and open a builder's own "Worth Knowing If You Have..." box and confirm a note with real relevance data is tappable and returns you to exactly where you were.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): a real standing Hydration Routine, bridged directly from Meal Builder.** Direct follow-up to the hydration-reminder work below: "This should be tied to their full day as well, however, it requires a presence in Food > Meal Builder... attribute it automatically somehow to their self created scheduled hydration, which probably could be on a repeating daily schedule, with choices that include plain water, water with electrolyte related additives, or from fermentations or other infused drinks." Investigated first, not assumed: the underlying repeat/rolling-window machinery (`RepeatConfig`, `insertScheduleSeries`, `ensureScheduleSeriesGenerated`) already fully supports a genuinely indefinite daily-repeating `schedule_items` entry, the exact mechanism Supplements and Prescriptions already use -- nothing new needed there. The real, missing piece was the bridge itself: no path existed from "I just built a real drink" to "this is now part of my standing schedule" without leaving Meal Builder, finding the same drink again in Schedule's own Hydration lens, and manually setting a repeat.

**New "Add to My Hydration Routine" action in Meal Builder** (`components/MealBuilder.tsx`), shown only for a beverage-type meal, alongside the existing "Log This Now"/"Save & Schedule for Later" actions. Saves the same real favorite those other two paths already save, then calls `scheduleMeal` with `repeat: {type:'daily', endType:'indefinite'}` at a chosen time -- a real, permanent daily series starting today, not a one-off. This is the direct answer to "plain water, water with electrolyte additives, or from fermentations or other infused drinks": any real drink built through Meal Builder (combining a Beverage Builder component, a Fermentation Builder tonic, plain water, or any mix) can become a standing routine slot this same way, carrying its own real nutrient content (electrolytes, probiotics, and so on) once logged, not a flat water-only estimate.

**Two smaller, real ties to "their full day."** First, the Hydration lens's own "+ Schedule a drink" form (already fully capable of a repeating daily entry via its existing `RepeatPicker`, just never defaulted that way) now defaults a FRESH add to Daily/Indefinite rather than a one-off, on the reasoning that a hydration entry is almost always meant to recur -- still fully changeable in the picker, and editing an existing occurrence is untouched. Second, the Daily Meal Plan generator's own auto-water-reminder feature (built earlier the same day) now checks for a real standing routine first (`hasStandingHydrationRoutine`, `lib/db.ts`) and skips generating its generic "Drink about Xml of water" filler when one already exists, rather than piling a second, generic reminder on top of drinks the person already scheduled for real, by name, on purpose. No routine yet still gets the original generic filler as a safety net.

**Named directly, not built this pass:** the batch-remake-cadence idea ("a homemade specific bacteria yogurt every so many days based on how much the batch makes and how often it is intended to be eaten and by however many people") is a real, valuable, and genuinely separate feature -- prep-ahead planning for any batch/make-ahead food, not just hydration -- that would extend the already-shipped Fermentation Tracker's own batch/harvest/inventory model (`fermentation_batches`/`fermentation_harvests`) with a real consumption-rate and household-size concept it doesn't have yet. Scoped out as its own follow-up rather than folded in here. Multi-user/partner sharing (both phones seeing the same scheduled meals and drinks) is unchanged, already-known deferred architecture (see the Partner tier and the ordered security-work prerequisite list above) -- not started, and this pass's own work was deliberately built on the existing single-device schema so it needs no rework once that architecture lands.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file, matching the confirmed pre-existing baseline on `schedule.tsx` exactly. No Digest content or reference-database changes. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.17. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth a real walkthrough: build a drink in Meal Builder, tap "Add to My Hydration Routine," confirm it shows up repeating on the Hydration lens every day going forward, and confirm a fresh Daily Meal Plan's "Add to Schedule" correctly skips its own generic water reminders once that routine exists.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): a UI overflow fix, full per-day viewability for a multi-day Daily Meal Plan, and real hydration-reminder scheduling plus a genuine cooking-water data gap closed.** Direct report, three parts in one message, after the pairing-requirement/kidney-stone work below shipped: the nutrient-coverage figures ran off the right edge of the screen; a multi-week plan only ever showed a condensed one-line-per-meal summary with no way to see or swap a specific day's full report; and "more hydration will have been scheduled throughout each day than just one helping... why isn't the water in the food tracking with hydration... the same applies for soups and anything else we can track the amount of water are used with a recipe that is kept."

**(1) The overflow.** The new "Mostly from: X (Y%), Z (W%)" contributor text (added earlier the same day) had no width constraint. Fixed with `flex: 1` on both the label and value columns of each nutrient row plus right-aligned text, so long contributor lists wrap instead of running off-screen.

**(2) Every day of a multi-day plan is now individually viewable and swappable.** The full single-day report (health rating, meals, Hydration callout, nutrient coverage) was pulled into a new shared `DailyPlanFullReport` component, reused both for a single generated day and, now, for any day in a multi-day list once expanded via a new "View full day"/"Hide full day" toggle per row. A "Regenerate This Day" button calls `generateDailyMealPlan` fresh for just that one day and replaces it in place -- a named, accepted limitation: this loses that day's place in the week's own shared rotation/frequency-rule state (fish-twice-a-week, red-meat-cap), the honest tradeoff for a real per-day swap rather than a full week regeneration.

**(3) Hydration, investigated end to end rather than patched blind.** The "why isn't food's water tracking with hydration" question turned out to have a real answer already built (`getDailyNutrientAnalysis`'s water entry already combines food-derived and logged-beverage water into one combined total, and the report's own Hydration callout already showed the real gap to target as a sentence) -- but two genuine, separate gaps sat underneath that sentence, confirmed by direct query rather than assumed. **First:** nothing ever turned that computed gap into anything real; it was always just text. New `scheduleHydrationRemindersForDay` (`lib/db.ts`) schedules real `beverage`-type `schedule_items` (the exact mechanism the Hydration lens's own "Something unplanned" form already uses, so these show up there like any manually-added drink), spread across a handful of realistic times through the day (355ml/12oz per slot, capped at 6 slots, the last slot sized to the true remainder), wired into "Add to Schedule" for the Daily Meal Plan lens so both a single day and a multi-day plan now schedule real timed water reminders alongside real meals, not just meals. **Second, a genuine pre-existing data gap:** a direct query found 14 of this app's 19 soup/stew/chowder/chili/broth recipes carry a real "vegetable stock" seasoning concentrate (2.5-7.5g, clearly just flavoring) but never the actual cooking water it dissolves into, unlike the other 5 soups, which already do this correctly -- meaning these 14 dishes' real water content, and therefore their contribution to a day's hydration total, was silently undercounted. The same gap was also found in 6 of this session's own new savory vegan porridge/grain breakfasts (amaranth, oats, millet, buckwheat, quinoa, polenta), which simmer a dry grain in plain water that was never added as an ingredient row at all. Fixed directly at the database (a real `Water, tap` ingredient row added to all 20, quantity calibrated per dish against this app's own 5 already-correct soups as a reference point) and mirrored into `lib/digest/recipes.ts`'s customer-facing ingredient text for the same 20 recipes; one of the 14 (`curated_soup_green_lentil_vegetable_stew`) turned out to already have a text-only "400ml water" line that was never backed by real database data at all, corrected to the single, real, database-matching amount rather than left as a duplicate. Named honestly, not audited: whether this same gap exists elsewhere across the wider 300+ recipe corpus (any other grain bowl or braised dish that simmers in water without listing it) was not checked -- a real, scoped-out follow-up, not assumed clean.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (`schedule.tsx`'s own pre-existing baseline of unescaped-apostrophe/quote errors, unchanged in count, confirmed via `git diff` to sit outside every line this pass touched). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`/`PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped (the live database and the Digest's own recipe text both changed). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.16. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is exactly the kind of fix (real scheduled reminders, a multi-day UI restructure, calibrated data added to 20 recipes) that most needs a real walkthrough: generate a multi-day plan, expand and regenerate one day, add it to schedule, and confirm real water reminders actually appear on the Hydration lens at the expected times.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): a third, real performance bottleneck found and fixed in the meal generator, plus a real "which foods caused this" breakdown added to the nutrient-coverage report.** Direct report, after the two earlier same-day performance fixes: "It still took quite a while for it to build a one day meal plan... I think it would be beneficial to tell the user what it is that is putting something way over on RDA being identified. Can we include the foods in the meal plan that are giving them the high amount of whatever?"

**The real, still-remaining performance cause, confirmed by reading this app's own prior fix for the identical pattern.** `getCuratedRecipeNutrientTotals` was still resolving every distinct ingredient's nutrient totals through `getFoodNutrients`, a genuinely heavy per-call query (a real sibling-fallback CTE: a CROSS JOIN against the whole foods table, a GROUP BY, a window function), one call per distinct (foodId, source) pair, all serialized through this app's one shared SQLite connection -- the exact same root cause a 2026-08-16 fix already solved for `getDailyNutrientBreakdown` (confirmed via that fix's own header comment: "37-41 SECONDS on top of it" from this identical pattern), just never carried over to the meal generator. A second, compounding cost found in the same pass: `getCuratedRecipe`'s own per-ingredient `resolveCuratedRecipeIngredient` call was never cached at all, even within one shared run, so the same real ingredient (Veg\|Garlic, Fats\|Olive Oil (Extra Virgin), Herbs\|Common salt/table salt) repeating across dozens of different recipes was resolving fresh via its own real query every single time.

**Both fixed by reusing already-proven infrastructure, not new code.** `getPrimaryNutrientAmountsBulk` (the exact bulk resolver the 2026-08-16 fix already built and verified) is now exported and reused here: every unique recipe's own ingredients are resolved once, every distinct (foodId, source) pair across the whole candidate pool is collected, and their nutrient amounts are fetched in one real bulk call instead of one call per pair, then used to pre-populate the shared nutrient cache before any candidate is actually loaded. `IngredientResolutionCaches` gained a fourth field, `ingredientChoice` (keyed by `${category}|${baseName}`), shared the same way the other three caches already are, so a repeated ingredient's own food-choice resolution happens once per run instead of once per recipe that uses it.

**The nutrient-attribution feature.** `DailyMealPlanPick` now carries its own real `nutrientTotals` (already computed, no new query), and `DailyMealPlanNutrientCoverage` gained a `topContributors` field: for any nutrient whose percentage is genuinely notable (150%+ of a floor target, or 80%+ of a real ceiling, matching the report's own existing near-or-over-limit threshold), the report now ranks the day's own real picks by how much each one actually contributed and names them, "Mostly from: Lentil & Spinach Bowl (62%), Chickpea Curry (28%)," rather than only naming the nutrient in the abstract. Deliberately per-dish, not per-ingredient -- the generator only ever tracks a whole dish's own resolved totals, not a per-ingredient breakdown within it, so this is the real, honest level of detail actually available, not a fabricated finer-grained claim.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file except confirmed pre-existing, unrelated errors in `schedule.tsx`. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.15. No Digest content or reference-database schema changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is the third performance pass on the same generator in one day, and the one most in need of a real timed re-run before trusting it.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): a real, generalized "pairing requirement" checker shipped, "The Rule Engine" named as a standing architecture concept, and a shared kidney-stone research conversation wired through conditions, Basic Health, recipes, and the meal generator all at once.** Direct request: "if we are telling them to pair something with a calcium source, it should trigger a look for a source of calcium in the meal, and if there is no source, the app should say so and suggest something else... The same would need to apply to any other thing that is like that," plus "we need a word for this part of the app so we both know what we are talking about... it has to always apply to all of the ways the information needs to be wired up, so nothing ever gets left out," plus a shared Google AI Mode conversation on kidney stone causes and oxalate-reducing cooking methods.

**"The Rule Engine," a new standing rule (see Standing rules below).** A real name for every mechanism in this app that encodes one evidence-based fact as a reusable "if X then Y" rule, and an 8-point checklist (reference-database scoring, condition relevance, recipe condition-cautions, the meal generator's own rule sets, the interaction-rules engine, Digest condition content, Digest Basic Health content, healing-stage advisories) to run through deliberately whenever a new fact enters the app, so nothing gets left half-wired the way earlier passes sometimes did.

**A real, working pairing-requirement checker** (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`): `PAIRING_REQUIREMENT_RULES` matches a recipe-caution phrase already written by the compute pipeline (starting with "pair with a calcium source," the oxalate/calcium case at hand) against the day's own real, computed nutrient totals, and surfaces a specific warning naming the real shortfall and a real suggestion when the pairing genuinely isn't met, not merely assumed satisfied because the caution text mentioned it. Built as a short, named rule list specifically so a future instance of "this caution says pair with X" is a data addition, not new code.

**Kidney-stone research, verified independently (not carried over from the source conversation as-is) and wired through every real point The Rule Engine names.** Chai & Liebman 2005 (DOI 10.1021/jf048128d) for the real 30-87% boiling-reduces-oxalate figure (steaming only 5-53%, baking near zero); Curhan et al. 1993 (NEJM, PMID 8441427) for the real, inverse dietary-calcium/kidney-stone-risk finding, plus a genuinely important nuance the same body of research draws out: the protective effect is specific to dietary calcium eaten as food, not calcium supplements, which several studies found carry the opposite association. Two new condition-relevance rows added to the live database (`scripts/add_kidney_stone_condition_relevance.js`): Oxalate Level/Load Rank/Tolerance Note extended to Chronic Kidney Disease (the direct kidney-stone connection) and IBD (enteric hyperoxaluria, a real, documented Crohn's/ulcerative-colitis complication from fat malabsorption), each with its own real citation, not a blind copy of Hashimoto's own framing. Deliberately NOT mapped to Gout, whose own real kidney-stone connection runs through uric acid, a different mechanism already well covered in this app's existing Gout research. 5 new Digest entries: two Basic Health entries (what actually raises kidney stone risk; why boiling, not baking or steaming, is what lowers oxalate), one Essential Nutrients entry (the dietary-calcium-vs-supplement nuance), and one each for Chronic Kidney Disease and IBD, all cross-linked to each other and to the existing Gout/produce-profile kidney-stone content rather than duplicating it.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. Every new `relatedIds` reference reconfirmed to resolve against the full, real corpus (3,251 references checked, zero unresolved). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`/`PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.14. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- the pairing-requirement checker specifically needs a real run against a plan that actually trips it.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): a real, confirmed severity-scoring bug behind several of the new vegan breakfast recipes' own "red" Hashimoto's flags, found and fixed following a direct challenge.** Direct pushback on the batch above: "high in oxalates doesn't equal they can't eat it. It means treat with care and make sure they are cooked... Aren't they flagged for a rule like that?... You aren't limiting them or giving them undue emergency status, are you?" Correct, and a real, confirmed bug, not a difference of philosophy.

**The actual database structure, checked directly rather than assumed.** Hashimoto's own D6 dimension carries three separate real sub-criteria for the identical underlying oxalate fact: `Oxalate Level` (a raw measurement: "High"/"Very High"), `Oxalate Load Rank` (the real, calibrated risk verdict: "Use Carefully"/"High Risk"), and `Oxalate Tolerance Note` (a real, cited, per-food action note already sitting in the database: "Discard cooking water where applicable; pair with a calcium source"). `Oxalate Level`'s own tier words happen to be the literal strings `tierSeverity`'s generic `RED_TIERS` set already treats as red for any sub-criterion using that vocabulary -- correct for a sub-criterion whose tier IS the verdict, wrong here, since `Oxalate Load Rank` already translates that same measurement into the real risk tier. Checking `Oxalate Level` as an independent hit meant a food the calibrated rank itself only called "Use Carefully" (amaranth, buckwheat, sweet potato) could still get captioned red purely from its own raw measurement label -- confirmed directly: spinach and chia genuinely agree red across all three real measures (a real, still-serious caution, correctly still red), while amaranth/buckwheat/sweet potato disagreed, exactly the false-emergency pattern described.

**The fix** (`scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js`): `Oxalate Level` and `Oxalate Tolerance Note` both excluded from independently driving severity (the same `NEAR_UNIVERSAL_SUB_CRITERIA`-style exclusion mechanism, for a related but distinct reason: not near-universal noise, but a raw measurement and a plain-text annotation both duplicating a properly-calibrated sibling sub-criterion). A new `TOLERANCE_NOTE_SUB_CRITERION_BY_RANK` links `Oxalate Load Rank` to its real tolerance-note text, now preferred over the generic templated sentence whenever both exist for the same ingredient -- "Spinach: High oxalate load. Boil and discard the cooking water where applicable; pair with a calcium source at the same meal." replaces the old "Spinach: rated Very High for oxalate level. The highest tier used for this measure." A real, separate database-content bug caught while this text was made visible for the first time: two Oxalate Tolerance Note strings used a stray " -- " (a pre-existing, previously-invisible violation of this app's own writing-style rule, now fixed directly in `food_scores`).

**A second, real, separate bug caught in the same pass, not just the oxalate one:** the new White Bean & Kale Breakfast Hash recipe's own instructions saute the kale, but no `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` entry existed for it, so the default Raw-first resolution was scoring its real raw-goitrogenic flag as if never cooked -- the identical class of bug already fixed for legumes in the same batch, now confirmed for a cruciferous vegetable too. Fixed with one more override entry.

**A related, genuinely open question, named directly rather than silently decided either way:** `Mineral Binding Risk` (the sub-criterion behind amaranth/sweet potato/buckwheat's remaining red flags) has no calibrated rank or tolerance-note sibling the way Oxalate does -- it is the ONLY real sub-criterion for that fact, so there is no clearly "correct" calibrated tier to defer to the way there was for oxalate, and downgrading its own High/Very-High-to-red mapping would be a real, judgment-call change to Hashimoto's own scoring philosophy, not a demonstrated bug fix the way the oxalate one was. Left unchanged pending a direct answer, not assumed.

**Honest result:** across the whole 316-recipe corpus, Hashimoto's own red-tier count barely moved (204 to 202) since most already-red recipes carry a separate, genuine red flag (soy, gluten) the oxalate fix was never going to touch -- but for the recipes where oxalate genuinely was the only trigger, this is a real, meaningful correction, not a cosmetic one. Vegan+Hashimoto's+breakfast improved 5 to 6; Hashimoto's breakfast overall (any diet) 16 to 17, still the one gap below 30 across all 57 (condition x meal) combinations, now confirmed to be a mix of legitimate remaining flags (spinach and chia's own real, agreed-upon red oxalate status) and the still-open Mineral Binding Risk question above.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two pre-existing `__dirname`/no-undef warnings in `compute_recipe_condition_data.js`, unchanged, confirmed pre-existing per this project's own standing precedent for every `scripts/*.js` file). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`/`PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped again; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.13. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26): the meal generator regenerated and reported still slow with still-high nutrient percentages, plus a direct real-world example ("why can't a vegan have savory amaranth porridge with garlic and avocado?") -- root-caused further and closed with a real, verified 16-recipe content batch.** Direct report after re-testing the fixes above: generation still took about a minute, and copper/fiber/folate/iron/manganese/potassium/B6/K/A/zinc all still read as implausibly high (256%/197%/144%/182%/133%/181%/309%/178%/133%/111%).

**The remaining performance gap, a real, separate cause from the pool-dedup fix.** Confirmed directly: `buildCandidatePools` still ran the sweetener check and both FREQUENCY_RULES checks once PER RECIPE (up to 3 real queries each) inside `loadCandidate`, across 150-250+ distinct recipes -- a second real contributor the first perf fix never touched. Fixed with two new bulk functions (`getCuratedRecipeIdsWithSweetener`/`getCuratedRecipeIdsContainingIngredient`, `lib/db.ts`), each answering the identical question for every recipe id at once, one query instead of N.

**The nutrient percentages, confirmed genuinely not a bug this time -- checked against every real DRI/UL value directly, not assumed.** All 10 flagged nutrients sit comfortably under their own real upper limit (iron the closest, at roughly 73% of its own 45mg UL; several, like B6 and vitamin K, aren't within striking distance at all) -- a real, expected characteristic of a nut/seed/legume-heavy whole-food vegan day, not a calculation error. The report's own framing needed to say so more clearly than a caveat paragraph could: a new dedicated "Hydration" callout was pulled out of the generic list entirely, and every other row now shows its own real percent-of-upper-limit alongside percent-of-target, visually flagged only when genuinely close to or over that real ceiling (>=80%), muted otherwise.

**The direct example -- "why can't a vegan have savory amaranth porridge with garlic, avocado, and mixed vegetables?" -- led to a real, verified 16-recipe content batch, not just a UI explanation.** Audited first with a new, read-only `scripts/audit_meal_plan_recipe_coverage.js`, mirroring the generator's own real eligibility logic exactly: this corpus's vegan breakfast pool was 17 (of 48 total breakfast-eligible recipes), and every one of the 16 dedicated "_vegan_" breakfast recipes in it is soy-based (tofu or soy milk) -- confirmed as the real, structural reason a whole-food, soy-free savory breakfast like the one described didn't already exist. Every other (condition, meal) combination across all 19 conditions already cleared 30+ recipes; only Hashimoto's breakfast (12) sat below that bar.

`scripts/add_vegan_savory_breakfasts.py` adds 16 new, verified curated recipes: 11 savory whole-grain/legume/vegetable bowls (including the exact amaranth-garlic-avocado dish described) and 5 sweet ones built on coconut milk instead of soy milk, none of them soy-based. A real, second data gap found and worked around, not silently: the originally chosen amaranth, cornmeal, corn-tortilla, and almond-milk rows all turned out to carry `hidden=1` in the live database (every real corn-tortilla row across all 4 sources, and all 3 real almond-milk rows in the whole database, are hidden) -- amaranth and cornmeal got real, non-hidden alternative rows, the tortilla recipe became a bowl, and the three "almond milk" recipes use coconut milk instead, the only other real non-soy, non-dairy milk this database has a usable row for. Diet tags and condition safety/cautions were computed through the same established `compute_recipe_diet_tags.js`/`compute_recipe_condition_data.js`/`apply_recipe_condition_cautions.js` pipeline every prior recipe batch already uses, not hand-guessed -- including 6 new `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` entries for legumes actually simmered/cooked in these recipes, the same real "raw resolution wrongly scores a cooked dish" fix this app's corpus has applied before.

**The honest result, not oversold:** the 30-vegan-breakfast target is met (33 total, up from 17). Hashimoto's breakfast improved (12 to 16 recipes; the vegan-and-Hashimoto's-and-breakfast combination specifically improved from 1 to 5) but is still the one combination across all 57 (19 conditions x 3 meals) below 30. Confirmed directly why: most of the new recipes are genuinely flagged for Hashimoto's on oxalate content (amaranth, spinach, sweet potato, buckwheat, chia seeds are all real, legitimately high-oxalate whole foods, a real Hashimoto's-relevant sub-criterion this app already tracks, not an invented rule) -- avoiding soy alone was not enough to make a whole-grain/legume vegan breakfast clear Hashimoto's own, unusually wide 25-sub-criterion net. Named directly as a real, scoped next step rather than forced further in this same pass: closing the remaining Hashimoto's-breakfast gap needs a further, deliberately low-oxalate (and/or dairy/egg-based, since Hashimoto's breakfast overall isn't diet-restricted) batch, not more of the same whole-grain approach.

**Two real, self-caught near-misses during this batch, fixed before shipping, not after:** `apply_recipe_diet_tags.js` always inserts a fresh `dietTags` line immediately after `recipeCard: {` rather than checking for one already present -- pre-writing a placeholder `dietTags: []` for the new entries (to satisfy the type checker before the real pipeline ran) caused a duplicate-property `tsc` error across all 16 new recipes on the first run, and re-running the same apply script a second time (after renaming 4 recipes for the hidden-ingredient fix) silently duplicated `dietTags` across all 300 PRE-EXISTING recipes too, caught immediately by the resulting `tsc` errors rather than shipped. Both fixed with a precise, scoped script keeping only the first `dietTags` line per `recipeCard` block, confirmed by an exact count (316, one per recipe) before moving on.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`/`PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` both bumped (real database and Digest content changed); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.12. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is the third pass at the same generator in one day, and the one most in need of a real re-run on the same device before trusting any of it: the perf fix, the reassuring nutrient framing, and the new recipes actually showing up as real breakfast options.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): adjustable personal protein/fiber/sodium targets shipped, plus a real, separate bug found and fixed while wiring them in.** Direct follow-up, before re-testing the meal-generator fixes above: "you should complete the adjustable personal protein/fiber/sodium targets with its own small settings feature. Then I'll regenerate the a 1 day plan and check the numbers."

**New `user_nutrient_targets` table (`lib/db.ts`)**: one row per nutrient someone has deliberately overridden, absence meaning "use my real default," the same contract every other personalization table in this file already follows. `target_amount` overrides a floor nutrient's RDA/AI (protein, fiber -- a real person can legitimately need more than the population-average figure); `limit_amount` overrides a ceiling nutrient's real cap (sodium, a CDRR-type row -- someone managing blood pressure or CKD may need stricter than the general population's 2300mg). `getUserNutrientTargets`/`setUserNutrientTargetOverride` are the two new functions; saving a blank field deletes the row outright rather than storing an empty override.

**New Profile card, "Nutrient Targets"** (right after "Meal Plan"), one row per curated nutrient (Protein, Fiber, Sodium -- a short, named first pass, not every DRI-tracked nutrient at once) showing the person's own real default (resolved from their sex/age, or a range when Profile is incomplete) alongside an editable custom value. Wired into the generator via a new `applyNutrientTargetOverrides` (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`), applied on top of the existing `buildConservativeDriByCode` merge, so every downstream consumer (the gap-filling scorer, the exceeds-UL guard, the nutrient-coverage report) automatically respects a personal override with no separate wiring per site. Sodium's own CDRR row was previously excluded from the nutrient-coverage report entirely (the filter only ever matched RDA/AI); widened to include CDRR too, with a new `isCeiling` flag so the UI shows "stay under" framing rather than "reach at least."

**A real, separate, pre-existing bug found and fixed while wiring this in, not assumed already correct:** `wouldExceedUpperLimit`/`scoreNutrientGapFilling` were being called with the raw, un-deduped `driRows` array (straight from `pools.driRows`), not the conservative, override-applied `driByCode` the same day's earlier DRI-collision fix had already built. Confirmed directly by tracing every call site: when Profile is missing a sex or birth date and a nutrient genuinely carries more than one real DRI row, `scoreNutrientGapFilling`'s own loop iterated every colliding row rather than one, double-counting that nutrient's gap-filling credit; and neither the earlier collision fix nor a brand-new personal target override actually reached `wouldExceedUpperLimit`, the one real guard that blocks a genuine overshoot rather than merely reporting one. Fixed by resolving `driRows` from `driByCode`'s own values exactly once at the top of `generateOneDay`, reused everywhere the function used to reach for the raw array directly -- both bugs closed by the same one change, not two separate patches.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`, `lib/db.ts`, `app/profile.tsx`, `schedule.tsx`) except confirmed pre-existing, unrelated errors in `schedule.tsx`/`profile.tsx`. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.11. No Digest content or reference-database schema changes (a new local, on-device table only). **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- next up is the same 1-day vegan/Hashimoto's/low-carb regeneration this whole pass exists to make trustworthy.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): the Daily Meal Plan generator shipped a few hours earlier the same day was reported broken in real use, root-caused, and fixed on four fronts.** Direct, detailed report after a real on-device run (Hashimoto's, vegan, low carb, 1 day): a ~5-minute generation time, "no compliant option found" for breakfast, only one side dish and no real hydration tracking, and nutrient percentages (265% copper, 175% iron, 180% manganese, 178% potassium) that read as implausible. Each traced to a real, distinct cause, not one bug wearing four faces.

**(1) Performance.** Confirmed directly, not guessed: `buildCandidatePools` loaded 6 candidate pools independently, and a recipe belonging to more than one (a 'side' is a real lunch/dinner main AND its own dedicated side candidate, a real 2-3x multiplier for this corpus's most-used builder type), had its own real, several-queries-deep nutrient resolution redone once per pool. A second, compounding cost: `getCuratedRecipeNutrientTotals` built itself a fresh, empty ingredient-resolution cache per recipe, so a common ingredient (olive oil, garlic, salt) used across dozens of recipes had its own data re-queried once per recipe that uses it, even within one run. Fixed by resolving every distinct recipe id exactly once into a shared map, and sharing one `IngredientResolutionCaches` (now exported from `lib/db.ts`) across the whole run.

**(2) "No compliant option found," confirmed as a genuine, verified content gap, not a filtering bug.** Every one of this corpus's 16 vegan-tagged breakfast recipes is red-flagged for Hashimoto's, confirmed by direct query against `lib/digest/recipes.ts` -- overwhelmingly soy (tofu, soy milk). Combined with a real, separate bug (the "no added sugar at breakfast" check excluded any recipe carrying a `Sweets`-category ingredient row even when that row's own `prep_note` is `'optional'`, which the 2026-08-24 chrononutrition pass made true of nearly every fruit-and-oat/porridge/chia-pudding breakfast in the corpus), a vegan Hashimoto's search had zero real candidates left standing. The optional-sweetener bug is fixed (`curatedRecipeContainsSweetenerIngredient` now ignores an optional row); a genuine sweetened-fallback and a specific, honest failure message ("this is a real gap in the recipe library itself, not a setting to adjust") replace the old, unexplained dead end. Authoring new soy-free vegan breakfast recipes to close the underlying content gap itself was deliberately not done in this same pass -- named directly as a real, scoped, separate next step (Open next steps, below), matching this project's own precedent of treating curated-recipe authoring as its own dedicated, verified session rather than folding it into an unrelated bug fix.

**(3) The nutrient percentages, a real, distinct bug, not (only) a surprising-but-normal whole-food-vegan-diet outcome.** `getDietaryReferenceIntakesForCurrentUser()` returns every applicable DRI row when Profile is missing a sex and/or birth date, by design (its own doc comment: "the caller/UI should show side by side, not collapse") -- but the new `driByCode = new Map(driRows.map(...))` silently kept whichever colliding row happened to sort last for a given nutrient, e.g. the male iron row (8mg) even for someone whose real target might be the female 19-50 row (18mg), a genuine, silent demographic mismatch that reads as "175%" for an amount closer to 80% of the actual target. Fixed with a new `buildConservativeDriByCode`: colliding rows merge into one conservative row (the highest real RDA/AI, the lowest real defined upper limit) instead of an arbitrary pick, and a new `profileIncomplete` flag surfaces a direct warning telling the person to add a sex and birth date in Profile for a properly personalized plan. Separately, the nutrient-coverage report itself now distinguishes a nutrient with no real defined upper limit (or comfortably under one) running past 100% of a small RDA (copper and manganese especially -- genuinely normal for a nut/seed/legume-leaning vegan day) from one actually close to or over its real safety ceiling, instead of treating every percentage past 100 as equally alarming.

**(4) Hydration**, confirmed to already exist mechanically (water is a real tracked DRI nutrient, 2700ml female / 3700ml male, matching the user's own quoted target exactly) but buried, unlabeled, inside a generic nutrient list. Pulled into its own dedicated "Hydration" callout on the report, framed the same way the existing Hydration lens already does: amount and percent from this plan's own food and drink, plus a direct "drink about Xml more of plain water today" figure closing the real gap to the target.

**A fifth, direct request answered in the same pass: "there should also be more than just the carb choice... less than a certain amount of sugar."** A real "Added Sugar" toggle now exists alongside Carb level (Schedule's Daily Meal Plan lens), reusing the same structural, ingredient-level "was a real sweetener added" check breakfast already used (deliberately not a raw gram threshold on `sugars_total`, which mixes a food's own natural sugar in with anything added, the same reasoning "no sugar at breakfast" was already built around) -- and applying it, for the first time, to lunch and dinner mains too, not just breakfast. Named directly, not built this pass: adjustable personal protein/fiber/sodium targets (the user's own words, "I'm not sure what else there should be... they might need more of something than the normal person would need"), a real, separate, and valuable follow-up needing its own small schema (a per-nutrient override table) and UI, scoped out here rather than half-built alongside four urgent bug fixes in one pass.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`, `lib/db.ts`, `schedule.tsx`) except confirmed pre-existing, unrelated unescaped-apostrophe/quote errors in `schedule.tsx` (verified via `git diff` to sit outside every line this pass touched). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.10. No Digest content or reference-database schema changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is exactly the kind of fix (live timing, a real demographic edge case, a genuine content gap) that most needs a real re-run on the same device and the same vegan/Hashimoto's/low-carb combination that surfaced the original report, before trusting it fully.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): nutrient-optimized, cross-builder 6-week meal generation, steps 1-4 of the confirmed 5-step plan.** Direct request: build a way, from Profile or Schedule, to auto-generate 6 weeks of REAL, full meals combining across every Food builder (not one recipe standing in for a whole meal), optimized toward RDA targets without exceeding any nutrient's upper limit, aware of nutrient synergies and things that shouldn't be combined -- explicitly required to work identically for vegan, vegetarian, pescatarian, and every other declared diet style, not specific to omnivore or any one way of eating. Researched first (see the Notion App Development Log for the full research writeup, including verified citations): vitamin C/non-heme iron synergy, calcium/iron and zinc/copper antagonism, and the already-established fat/fat-soluble-vitamin rule already in this app's own `interaction_rules` table. Confirmed the real, existing `lib/dailyMealPlan.ts` generator (6-week-capable since 2026-08-25) and `lib/db.ts`'s own `scheduleMealPlanSlot` (already converts curated recipes into real saved dishes wrapped into one real Meal favorite) were both already most of the way there -- this extends them rather than building parallel systems.

**Step 1: `MealPlanSlot` widened** from `{main, side?}` to `{main, side?, salad?, soup?, sauce?, beverage?, dessert?}`, backwards-compatible (every existing `MealPlanDay` literal in `lib/mealPlan.ts`/`lib/mealPlanVegan.ts`/`lib/mealPlanVegetarian.ts` only ever sets main/side and needed zero changes). `scheduleMealPlanSlot`'s own component loop was already generic over however many refs a slot carries; only the type needed widening. New `joinMealTitleParts` gives a real meal name for 3+ combined components ("X, Y, and Z") instead of repeating "with" past where it reads naturally.

**Steps 2-3: real nutrient-gap-filling, upper-limit awareness, and cited synergy/antagonism scoring, then multi-component assembly using it.** New `NUTRIENT_SYNERGY_RULES`/`NUTRIENT_ANTAGONISM_RULES` (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`), a short, cited list matching `FREQUENCY_RULES`'s own discipline: vitamin C + iron (synergy, Cook & Reddy 2001, PMID 11124756), calcium + iron (antagonism, Hallberg et al. 1993, PMID 8490006), zinc + copper (antagonism, named honestly as mainly a supplement-dose concern), and dietary fat + fat-soluble vitamins (reusing, not re-citing, this app's own existing `interaction_rules` rows). Every rule checks real per-candidate nutrient AMOUNTS, never ingredient identity or food type -- diet-style-agnostic by construction, confirmed directly: a vegan iron-rich lentil dish gets the identical vitamin-C bonus a lean beef dish would. "Meaningful source" reuses the FDA's own real 10%-of-target "good source" labeling threshold (21 CFR 101.54) rather than an invented number. `getDietaryReferenceIntakesForCurrentUser`'s own `upperLimit` field (fetched for years, never used) now actually gates selection: a candidate that would push any nutrient's day-total past its own UL is excluded when a real alternative exists. `pickCandidate` ranks the surviving pool by gap-filling-plus-pairing score before the existing carb-bias/rotation pick runs, keeping real day-to-day variety rather than forcing one "best" answer every time. Lunch/dinner can now also gain a real salad and/or beverage component beyond the existing main+side, but only when scoring shows a genuine, meaningful benefit (`BONUS_COMPONENT_MIN_SCORE`, a named first-pass judgment call) -- never bolted on by default. Antagonism is a scoring penalty, not a hard exclusion, named directly as the honest treatment: the science says "measurably reduces absorption when combined," not "unsafe together," and a hard exclusion would make many ordinary combinations (a single leafy green carries both calcium and iron at once) impossible to generate around; genuine "never safe" cases (Celiac + gluten) are already hard-excluded upstream by the existing condition-safety filtering, unchanged.

**Step 4: both entry points.** Schedule already had the full Daily Meal Plan lens; it just needed labels for the two new roles (`DailyMealPlanPickRow`, "Salad:"/"Beverage:" alongside the existing "Side:") and a real deep link (`openScheduleLens`, mirroring `purple-digest.tsx`'s own `openDigestLens` and `garden.tsx`'s own `openGardenLens`). New Profile card ("Meal Plan," right after Diet Preferences) with a real button jumping straight into that same lens -- one real generator, two doors into it, not two implementations.
