**Most recent (2026-08-26): the one named loose end from the 5-phase Condition Scores rebuild closed.** Direct follow-up: "Fix the Digest evidence-tiering string too." Digest's own "A growing set of categories, one evidence standard" help text used to compare its Strong/Moderate/Weak citation-tiering discipline to "this app's own 6 Dimensions scoring" -- a comparison that was never quite accurate even before today's rename (citation-quality tiering and per-food dimension scoring are two different real things), and, after this rebuild, one more place naming "6 Dimensions" as if every tracked condition has it. Fixed by dropping the comparison outright rather than swapping in a new one: the sentence states its own real rule (every entry tiered by its own actual evidence) without reaching for an analogy that doesn't hold up. `tsc`/`eslint` clean on the one touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.8. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26): phase 5 of 5, Pattern Finder's own trigger candidates scoped to tracked conditions -- the condition-scoped 6 Dimensions rebuild is now complete end to end.** Direct follow-up: "Yes, go ahead and start on phase 5."

**The real, named trade-off from the start of this rebuild, now actually built in:** `lib/patternFinder.ts`'s own dimension candidates ("this flagged factor showed up before N of your flares") used to check every sub-criterion this app currently scores at all, regardless of relevance to anything the person actually tracks. Now it only ever checks sub-criteria relevant to a tracked condition, using the same phase-1 bulk condition-scoring engine every other phase already relies on. Tracking no conditions yields no dimension candidates at all, an honest empty state named directly in the UI ("Set your tracked conditions in Profile to check for this"), not a fallback to the old generic, noisier behavior -- unlike the phase-4 flag-count fix, there's no real "your own condition" concept left to fall back to once nothing is tracked, and reverting to the generic list would just reintroduce the near-universal noise this whole rebuild exists to remove.

**A real, pre-existing bug fixed in the same pass, not just the scoping:** the old version deduped and counted candidates by `${dimension}::${tier}` alone, which could silently merge two genuinely different sub-criteria that happened to share both a dimension and a tier word -- now keyed by `${conditionCode}::${subCriterion}::${tier}`, the precise identity, with each candidate naming which specific tracked condition it's relevant to (a shared sub-criterion can now surface as a distinct candidate under more than one tracked condition at once, correctly, rather than being silently collapsed into one). The same near-universal-sub-criteria exclusion (Selenium & Zn synergy, Iron Presence) the rest of this rebuild already applies everywhere else now applies here too -- the old version never excluded them, so a background signal that fires on roughly half the reference database could have trivially cleared the 2-occurrence bar for almost anything.

**A corpus-wide sweep for stray "6 Dimensions"/"6-DFF" mentions across every app screen** (not just the 5 files this rebuild directly touched), since the rename needed to be consistent everywhere the concept gets described, not just where the lens itself lives: fixed in Profile's own "why personalize" help text, Log's "what this is separate from" help text, Garden's harvest-logging help text, Food's own tab-intro help text, and two spots in Schedule (one help block, one code comment) describing a rotated ingredient's own scoring. Two intentionally left alone, named directly rather than silently skipped: a 2026-08-08 code comment in `insights.tsx` that's a verbatim quote of the original feature request (rewriting a direct quote would misrepresent what was actually said), and a Digest help string (`purple-digest.tsx`) describing the evidence-tiering discipline generally across all 19 conditions -- a real, separate piece of writing that deserves its own accurate rewrite rather than a mechanical find-replace, flagged directly rather than touched in passing.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file except confirmed pre-existing, unrelated unescaped-apostrophe/quote errors in `schedule.tsx` and `profile.tsx` (verified via `git diff` to sit outside every line this pass touched). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.7. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- and this closes out the full 5-phase rebuild (shared engine, Insights' own lens, the saved-dish detail view, the shared flag-count fix, and now Pattern Finder), worth a real, deliberate walkthrough given how much of it touches live, per-condition computation a type-checker can't verify on its own.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): phase 4 of 5, the shared flag-count fix -- Home's "Worth a Look" badge, Trends' own trend line, and the Reports flag summary all condition-scoped from one shared function.** Direct follow-up: "Yes, go ahead and start on phase 4." Confirmed by tracing the actual call chain, not assumed: `getSixDimensionsFlagCountsByDateRange`/`getProjectedSixDimensionsFlagCountsByDateRange` (`lib/db.ts`) were the one remaining generic, condition-agnostic data source in the whole rebuild, reached three separate ways -- directly from Home, via `lib/trendAnalysis.ts`'s thin wrappers from Trends' own Condition Scores lens, and via those same wrappers again from `lib/reportGenerator.ts`'s doctor-facing report.

**The counting logic itself rebuilt**, not just parameterized: a new shared `countFlaggedSubCriteriaByDate` (`lib/db.ts`) takes an optional `conditionCodes` list and, when given one, counts DISTINCT sub-criteria flagged for ANY of those conditions (a sub-criterion relevant to two tracked conditions at once counts once, not twice) via the phase-1 `getConditionScoresForFoodsBulk` machinery, excluding the same near-universal background signal (`NEAR_UNIVERSAL_SUB_CRITERIA`) the rest of this rebuild already excludes -- without that exclusion, Hashimoto's own two near-universal sub-criteria would have silently inflated this exact number for anyone tracking it. An empty `conditionCodes` list (nobody tracks anything yet) keeps the prior generic behavior, the same "absence means no restriction" contract already used everywhere else in this personalization work. Both real and projected functions were also switched from a sequential per-food score-cache loop to one bulk fetch per call, a genuine efficiency gain alongside the correctness fix.

**All three callers wired, none needing a second condition-picker.** Home already loaded `userConditionCodes` for its own Digest flip-card scoping (2026-08-23) -- reused directly, no new fetch. Trends gained the same `getPersonalizationProfile()` state Insights/food-item-detail.tsx already use. Reports' `generateReport` already fetched `userConditionCodes` for its own "TRACKED CONDITIONS" section -- reused for the flag summary directly below it, so the two sections can't disagree with each other. Trends' own lens renamed "6 Dimensions" to "Condition Scores" (its label, both help blocks, and every internal comment naming it), matching phase 2's rename of Insights' identical lens; Reports' own help text and generated section header ("6 DIMENSIONS FLAGS" to "CONDITION SCORE FLAGS") updated the same way. Pattern Finder's own "6 Dimensions flags" section heading (Trends' own Pattern Finder sub-view) is deliberately left for phase 5, since it's tied to that phase's own data-scoping decision, not a bare rename to make in isolation.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside, including `index.tsx`'s own pre-existing, confirmed-unrelated unescaped-apostrophe error), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every other touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.6. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26): phase 3 of 5, `app/food-item-detail.tsx`'s own reuse of the same lens wired the same way.** Direct follow-up: "Yes, go ahead and start on phase 3." This screen (My Kitchen/My Favorites' saved-dish detail view, reached for any of the 11 direct-ingredient builders) literally imports `SixDsView`/`PrepView`/`NutrientsTable` straight from `app/(tabs)/insights.tsx` rather than building its own -- phase 2 left its call deliberately unwired, defaulting to an empty condition list so it kept compiling rather than breaking.

Now loads the person's own tracked conditions the identical way Insights does (`getPersonalizationProfile()`, its own local `personalizationProfile` state), passes it into `SixDsView`, and threads it through `loadSide()` -> all 11 `get*SixDimensionsBreakdown(id, trackedConditions)` calls, one line changed per call, the same mechanical shape every rollout across these 11 functions has followed since phase 1. This screen's own lens picker (`DETAIL_LENSES`) gets the identical rename phase 2 gave Insights': "6 Dimensions" to "Condition Scores," plus one stale code comment corrected in passing.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the one touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.5. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26): phase 2 of 5, Insights' own lens actually rebuilt and renamed.** Direct instruction: "use the condition picked from the profile for all of these. Don't create a new condition picker specific to Insights." Confirmed no such single-condition Profile field exists (only the existing multi-select tracked-conditions list) -- so this reuses that list directly: one section per tracked condition, stacked top to bottom, no new picker UI at all, matching the exact same pattern `RecipeDepthReport.tsx` already established for a single dish's own report.

**The lens itself, renamed from "6 Dimensions" to "Condition Scores"** (`LENSES`' own label, both help-text blocks), since the old name was never accurate for 18 of the 19 conditions and the new naming rule ([[feedback_six_dimensions_hashimotos_only_naming]]) reserves "Six Dimensions of Food Friendliness" for Hashimoto's specifically. `SixDsView` (the exported component name stays, an internal identifier only) now renders one section per tracked condition: `condition.code === 'hashimotos'` titles its own section "Six Dimensions of Food Friendliness" via `DimensionChart`'s own `conditionName` prop; every other condition's section is titled with its own plain name, never compared to Hashimoto's or framed as a smaller version of the same framework. A tracked condition with nothing assessed in the current scope shows an honest "Nothing in this scope is assessed against [condition]" rather than an empty gap; zero tracked conditions shows "Set your tracked conditions in Profile to see this."

**Real, new value surfaced along the way, not just a rename**: `relevanceNote` (the condition-specific "why this matters" text `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` already stored, computed by phase 1's own engine but never shown anywhere before this) now appears in a sub-criterion's own drill-in detail, and its own condition-specific `citation` is preferred over the shared, generic one when present. Neither existed in the old generic view, which had no per-condition concept to attach either to.

**Two other stale, condition-agnostic claims found and fixed in the same file while renaming, not left half-inconsistent**: Safe Foods' own help text still described "the 6 Dimensions"/"D1-D6 scoring" and a flat "24 sub-criteria" figure, both stale since Safe Foods itself became condition-scoped a few hours earlier the same day -- corrected to describe what it actually does now (checked against every tracked condition, diet preference, and allergy at once). `getDailySixDimensionsBreakdown` now receives the person's real tracked conditions from Insights' own load effect, threaded through the same `personalizationProfile` state the rest of today's work already established.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): the condition-scoped replacement for Insights' generic "6 Dimensions" lens, phase 1 of 5 -- the shared engine plus every lib/db.ts function that feeds it.** Direct follow-up to a real gap surfaced the same day: checked directly against the live database, only Hashimoto's actually owns all 6 real, independently-scored dimensions (D1-D6); every other of the 19 tracked conditions has its own, smaller, differently-named real dimension set (1 to 4 dimensions), and the existing "6 Dimensions" lens (and every screen sharing its data) was entirely condition-agnostic, showing the same generic, Hashimoto's-flavored dump to everyone regardless of which conditions they actually track. Full audit of every real consumer, and the 5-phase plan this closes phase 1 of, is in the Notion App Development Log. Two standing rules apply throughout this whole rebuild, saved directly to memory: never use Hashimoto's as the reference point when explaining another condition's own version of this tool ([[feedback_no_hashimotos_primacy_framing]]), and "6 Dimensions"/"D1-D6" as a name only ever appears when Hashimoto's is the condition being viewed ([[feedback_six_dimensions_hashimotos_only_naming]]) -- both apply starting with phase 2, the first phase with any user-facing text.

**New `lib/conditionDimensions.ts`**: `buildConditionDimensionSummary`/`buildPerConditionSummaries`, pure and synchronous, turning a list of already-scored foods into one condition's own worst-case-per-dimension chart data plus full per-sub-criterion drill-in detail (which foods triggered which tier) -- reusable at every scope level (a single ingredient, a side, a meal, a whole day, or one saved dish) with no extra query cost once the underlying scores are fetched. Deliberately added ALONGSIDE the existing generic per-item data, not built by replacing it: the Cooking & Prep lens (`PrepView`) reads that generic data for a genuinely different, condition-independent question (does this food change based on preparation) and needed zero changes as a result.

**New `getConditionScoresForFoodsBulk`/`computeConditionDimensionsForFoods` (`lib/db.ts`)**: a bulk, cached-source equivalent of `getFoodScoresForCondition` for many foods and many conditions at once, reusing the exact same cached full-table fetch (`getAllScoreRowsWithSubCriteria`) the 2026-08-26 Safe Foods personalization work already established, so this doesn't reopen that lens's own documented 18.6-second freeze risk. `sub_criterion_condition_relevance`'s cached row shape widened to carry its own `dimension_label`/`relevance_note`/`citation`, needed to build a faithful, complete equivalent of the per-food function rather than a thinner copy.

**All 12 `get*SixDimensionsBreakdown` functions updated** (the daily one plus one per direct-ingredient builder: Side, Salad, Smoothie, Fermentation, Beverage, Snack, Baked Goods, Soup, Sauce, Handhelds, Dessert): each now accepts an optional `trackedConditions` parameter (defaults to `[]`, so every existing caller keeps working unchanged) and attaches a new `perCondition`/`dayPerCondition` field at every tree level (item, side, meal, day) alongside the existing generic `bySubCriterion` data. The 11 builder-level functions were also refactored from 11 near-identical hand-copied bodies into one shared `buildDishSixDimensionsBreakdown` helper plus 11 thin wrappers -- confirmed identical in shape before refactoring, not assumed.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched file. No user-facing change yet: `trackedConditions` defaults to empty everywhere it's called today, so this phase is purely additive plumbing. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.3. **Phase 2 next: rebuilding Insights' own `SixDsView` to actually use this data** (a condition picker defaulting to the person's tracked conditions, the exact same Whole Day -> Meal -> Side -> Ingredient drill-down already in place). Phases 3-5: `app/food-item-detail.tsx`'s reuse of the same component, the shared flag-count fix (Home's "Worth a Look" badge, Trends' own 6 Dimensions line, Reports' flag summary all trace to one function), and Pattern Finder's `dimensionCandidates` scoping.

**Most recent (2026-08-26): Healing Stage Food Finder given the same diet-preference/allergy filter as the rest of Insights, after first closing the small schema gap that had blocked it.** Direct follow-up to the personalization work below: "Do the healing stage food finder, making sure to first take care of the small schema change." That schema gap, named directly the same day this lens's own personalization work was scoped out: `StageFood` (the row shape both `queryStageGroup` and `foodsWithSubCriterionTag` in `lib/db.ts` already return) carried no `category` field at all, and `foodMatchesDietPreferences` (`lib/foodPersonalization.ts`) needs a real category to run `computeDietTags` against.

Fixed first, as asked: `category` added to `StageFood`, and to both real query functions' own `SELECT` lists -- a plain column addition, not a new join or a new concept, since both functions already filter on the exact category value being added (`group.category`/the `category` parameter). Then wired the same way Safe Foods and Nutrient Ranking already are: the lens's own load effect now waits on `personalizationProfile` (so the very first result shown is already filtered, not a flash of unfiltered foods immediately narrowed out from under the person) and filters every Stage 1 group and Stage 2 round through the same `foodMatchesDietPreferences`/`foodMatchesAllergy` pure functions. `HealingStageView` itself needed no changes at all: an empty, fully-filtered-out group already renders its own existing "None found" message.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on both touched files (`lib/db.ts`, `app/(tabs)/insights.tsx`). No Digest content or reference-database schema changes (the `category` addition is a query-side `SELECT`, not a database migration). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.2. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-26): the person's own tracked conditions, food allergies, and declared diet preference wired into Insights for the first time.** Direct report: "we associated and wired up all of the conditions, food alergies, and general health specific tracking numbers. Those things didn't get wired through to the Insights tools. As well, when a user specifies their style of eating, all of the tools in the app need to follow those settings, just like their conditions, to only report back what is within the filter for their foods."

Checked directly before writing anything, not assumed: `app/(tabs)/insights.tsx` had never once called `getUserConditions`/`getDietPreferences`/`listFoodAllergies` anywhere, and `components/FoodLookup.tsx` (the shared picker every Food builder AND Insights' own Food Lookup lens both use) imported nothing condition-, diet-, or allergy-aware at all -- the only real per-food safety signal anywhere near it was `DimensionFlags`, a plain generic six-dimension flag used inside every builder but never inside FoodLookup itself. Safe Foods (`listSafeFoodCategories`/`listSafeFoods`) turned out to carry a second, real, pre-existing bug on top of that: its own "safe" set was condition-agnostic AND had never picked up the near-universal-sub-criteria exclusion (Selenium & Zn synergy, Iron Presence) the Digest's "Meals You Can Eat" redesign already applied on 2026-08-25, so roughly half of all 22,022 foods were silently marked "not safe" for a background signal that isn't a real concern at all.

**New `lib/foodPersonalization.ts`, the one shared core, not a third scoring engine.** A single food is a real, correct degenerate case of the same "whole dish" computation `lib/recipeDepth.ts` already built 2026-08-25 for the Food builders' own pre-save depth report: `computeRecipeDepth`/`computeDietTags` (now exported) run unchanged against a one-ingredient list. `getPersonalizationProfile()` loads all three real inputs at once (tracked conditions with names, declared diet preferences, food allergies); `evaluateFoodForPerson()` returns one food's real per-condition safety/caution/dimension data plus which declared diet preferences it violates and whether it matches a declared allergy (reusing `app/(tabs)/log.tsx`'s own already-shipped best-effort substring allergy match, not a second way of asking the same question); `foodMatchesDietPreferences()`/`foodMatchesAllergy()` are the pure, DB-free versions cheap enough to run per-row over an already-fetched list.

**Food Lookup lens**: a new "For You" card appears once a food resolves (`FoodLookup.tsx`'s new optional `personalize` prop), showing an allergy warning, a diet-preference-conflict warning, and a real Clean/Mild Caution/Caution/Not Recommended verdict pill per tracked condition (`verdictFor`, now exported from `RecipeDepthReport.tsx` and reused rather than duplicated). Deliberately additive and Insights-scoped only: every Food builder's own embedded `FoodLookup` (and Garden's harvest log, and `log.tsx`'s food-trial picker) leaves this prop unset and renders exactly as before, since builders already carry their own separate live per-ingredient advisory and full pre-save depth report -- a second, differently-shaped personalization block bolted onto their shared picker would have been an unrequested duplication, not a fix.

**Safe Foods**: real, condition-scoped "safe" for the first time, matching the exact standard "Meals You Can Eat" already established for curated recipes -- a food flagged for even one tracked condition no longer counts as safe. New bulk, cached machinery in `lib/db.ts` (`getFlaggedFoodIdsForCondition`/`getPersonalizedSafeFoodIds`) was built specifically to avoid reintroducing this lens's own documented 18.6-second freeze history: one full `food_scores` JOIN `sub_criteria` fetch (cached forever, reusable for any condition) plus one small relevance-table fetch, both computed once regardless of how many conditions are tracked, rather than a query per condition. Named honestly in the code: this new fetch returns more columns than the original ungated query that caused that freeze, so it is very likely at least as slow, not faster, and hasn't been re-profiled on-device yet -- kept behind the exact same "only when the lens is actually opened, once per session, honest loading text" gating that made the original cost acceptable. `getSafeFoodIds()` itself (the `conditionCodes: []` fallback for someone tracking no conditions yet) got the near-universal-exclusion fix in the same pass. Results are filtered a second time, client-side, against declared diet preferences and allergies.

**Nutrient Ranking**: the already-fetched, already-capped (100-row) ranked list is now filtered the same way -- someone declared vegan no longer sees beef leading their own protein ranking, and a declared allergen never appears.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass, named directly rather than silently skipped:** Healing Stage Food Finder (its own `StageFood` rows don't currently carry a `category` field at all, needed for diet-tag classification -- a small, real schema gap, not done here); Today's Advisories (reflects today's actual logged meals against the interaction-rules engine, a different real mechanism from browsing reference foods, not audited this pass); and the builders' own embedded FoodLookup instances, an intentional scoping decision explained above, not an oversight.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched file (`lib/foodPersonalization.ts` new, `lib/recipeDepth.ts`, `lib/db.ts`, `components/FoodLookup.tsx`, `components/RecipeDepthReport.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/insights.tsx`). No Digest content or reference-database schema changes. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.27.1 (day segment advanced: this is the first request of a new calendar day). **Not yet confirmed on-device**, and this one specifically needs a real check given the named, unresolved Safe Foods performance risk: open Safe Foods for the first time with at least one tracked condition and confirm it completes in a reasonable time rather than freezing.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): Meal Builder's version of the depth report, closing the one deliberate exception the 10-builder rollout named a few hours earlier the same day.** Direct request: "Now build Meal Builder's own version of the report."

A real structural difference from all 11 of the other builders, named directly rather than forced into the same shape: Meal Builder assembles a meal FROM several already-saved components (a side, a salad, and so on), each of which may already carry its own real depth for its own full batch, not for whatever share of it actually landed in this specific meal. There is also no `depth_data_json` column on the `meals` table the way the other 11 saved-record tables have one: a meal is a logged event or a reusable favorite template, not a browsable saved dish the way My Kitchen shows a side or salad, so nothing here would ever read a stored value back later. The honest answer for a meal isn't merging several already-computed, differently-scoped results together; it's recomputing depth fresh, live, across the exact combined ingredient list this one meal actually draws from, every time the report is opened. That's what this builds: a report computed on demand, never persisted, purely a look before finishing, the same role the other 11 builders' own reports play even though the underlying computation isn't stored the same way here.

A new `buildDepthIngredientsForMeal()` resolves every currently selected component via `resolveMealComponent` (the same real per-component resolution `getMealComponentsGoitrogenicFlags` already uses for its own cross-component raw-goitrogenic check) and flattens every component's ingredients into one list. `computeRecipeDepth` itself never actually reads quantity, dish servings, or share percentage at all, only food identity and category, so this flattening is correct for the safety/diet-tag/stage verdicts regardless of how much of each component was actually had. The nutrient chart is the one place share percentage still matters, and `getNutrientChartDataForIngredients` already applies it correctly per ingredient through the same share-fraction math every other real meal-nutrition view in this app already relies on, since `resolveMealComponent` carries each ingredient's real `yourSharePercent`/`dishServings` through untouched.

Meal Builder had no tracked-conditions or declared-stage wiring of any kind before this (unlike the 10 direct-ingredient builders, which have carried it since 2026-08-09); both were added here for the first time, the same `declaredStages`/`conditionsWithStagingModel`/in-place `AppActionSheet` stage picker shape as every other builder's report. "Preview Full Report" appears in both real places a meal actually finishes: the create-mode assembling screen (ahead of "Log This Now"; "Save & Schedule for Later" is left as its own separate, unpreviewed action, a deliberate, narrower scope than the primary finish path) and the edit-mode Past Meals screen (ahead of "Save Changes"). The report's "Save As Is" routes to whichever of those two real save functions actually applies, including edit mode's trial-reconciliation prompt chain, which needed its own `AppActionSheet` and `confirmSheet` elements added to this new render branch too, the same real elements every other branch in this file already carries.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the one touched file. No Digest content, reference-database, or schema changes. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.25. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the depth report rolled out to all 10 direct-ingredient builders beyond Side, closing the gap the pilot's status entries had named as a tracked next step.** Direct request: "Roll out the depth report to the other 10 builders next." Confirmed by count before starting, not assumed: 12 Food builders exist in total (the 11 named in this file's Shipped section plus Dessert, added 2026-08-14 after that line was last written), and Meal Builder assembles already-saved components rather than raw ingredients, a different shape `lib/recipeDepth.ts` was never built around. "10 builders" lines up exactly with the 10 that remained once Side and Meal are set aside: Salad, Smoothie, Fermentation, Beverage, Snack, Baked Goods, Soup, Sauces, Handhelds, Dessert. Meal Builder is named directly here as a deliberate, separate follow-up, not silently skipped.

Two layers, both already proven on Side, extended mechanically rather than redesigned. First, `lib/db.ts`: `depth_data_json` had already been added to all 11 remaining tables back when the column was first migrated, but only `sides`' save/update/get functions actually threaded it, confirmed directly by grep before writing anything. All 10 remaining pairs (salads, smoothies, fermentations, beverages, snacks, baked_goods, soups, sauces, handhelds, desserts) now accept, store, and return a real `depthData` field the identical way sides already did, applied via one script performing the same anchored, table-name-scoped text substitution across every occurrence rather than 60 separate hand edits, then verified with a real `tsc` pass before trusting any of it. `resolveMealComponent`'s `detailDepthData` extraction, hardcoded to `'side'` only because no other builder carried this data yet, is now a plain, generic cast across every component type; `lib/digestDynamicEntries.ts` already read `depthData` generically with no per-builder branch at all, so My Kitchen and My Favorites pick up the richer display for all 10 the moment a saved record actually carries it, no separate change needed there.

Second, each of the 10 builder components: the same `buildDepthIngredients`/`handlePreviewReport`/`finishX(ingredients, precomputedDepth?)` shape Side already established, a `'report'` finishStep branch rendering `RecipeDepthReport` with its own in-place `AppActionSheet` stage picker, and a "Preview Full Report" button on both the create-mode review screen and the edit-mode overview screen. Two builders, Salad and Smoothie, carry their own pre-existing raw-goitrogenic confirmation gate (`confirmAndFinishSalad`/`confirmAndFinishSmoothie`) from a much earlier pass; both were threaded with the same `precomputedDepth` parameter rather than bypassed, so the report's "Save As Is" still passes through that same check the direct button already does. The curated-recipe name-collision guard (`loadedFromCuratedRecipe`, "give this a new name") that Side also carries was deliberately not replicated here: it was a Side-specific addition from the same original pilot day, not part of this request's scope, named directly rather than left as an unexplained gap between builders.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings across all 11 touched files (10 builders plus `lib/db.ts`). No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.24. **Not yet confirmed on-device**, and worth a real walkthrough on at least one of the 10 given the sheer mechanical repetition involved: build a dish in each, preview the report, save both with and without previewing, confirm My Kitchen shows the richer depth once a saved record carries it.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a stale, 8-hour-old Metro dev-server process turned out not to be the actual cause of the "missing stage" saga, and a "nothing to report" state was added where the report used to just go quiet.** After the edit-mode fix below still didn't resolve it, direct screenshots confirmed the stage line and dimension chart were, in fact, both rendering correctly on-device: the actual, still-open complaint was a separate card ("Worth Knowing for Your Healing Stage") that had gone from showing a duplicate warning to showing nothing at all for the dish being tested.

Checked directly rather than assumed: every ingredient in that exact dish (chicken breast, beets, orange, olive oil, salt, pepper) was queried against every condition `getHealingStageAdvisory` actually checks (Gluten, raw Goitrogenic Load, Dairy, Nightshade, flagged Additives, heavy Processing) and matched none of them, confirming the dedup fix was working correctly the whole time and this dish never had anything to flag. Restarting the dev server along the way surfaced a real, unrelated finding worth naming: Metro had been running continuously since the very start of this session, still holding its port, exactly the "leftover dev server silently serving a stale bundle" failure mode `scripts/start-with-qr.js` already exists to prevent on every fresh `npm start`, just never triggered mid-session. Restarting it was a warranted fix on its own even though it turned out not to be the cause of this specific report.

**The actual, direct request once both of those were confirmed: "If there is nothing to advise in line with their current stage of healing then it should say that there is nothing to report about it."** Correct, and consistent with how the rest of this report already behaves (the verdict pill always shows something, even "Clean," never goes silent). The "Worth Knowing for Your Healing Stage" card now shows whenever at least one tracked condition has a real declared stage at all (not gated on whether anything was actually flagged), with an explicit "Nothing in this dish is flagged for your current stage" message when `stageNotes` comes back empty, instead of the card disappearing entirely.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.23. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the actual reason the healing-stage report kept "disappearing" found: the whole Nutrition & Health Report was never reachable from edit mode at all.** After the fire-and-forget write fix below didn't resolve a repeated report of the stage information being gone, the phone's database was pulled directly (adb) and checked: the declared stage (`hashimotos: digging`) was present and correct on-device the whole time, and every line of the render code checking it out on review too. A full app reload didn't change anything either, which meant the problem was neither the data nor a stale bundle.

The real cause: "Preview Full Report," the button that opens the entire report, stage information included, was only ever added to the create-mode review screen. Reopening an already-saved side to check it (via Edit, the ordinary way to look at something already built rather than rebuilding it from scratch) lands on a separate edit-mode overview screen that never had this button at all, in any of the prior commits. From the person's side, checking a saved side's report looked exactly like the whole feature had been silently removed, when it had never actually been reachable that way in the first place.

Fixed by adding the identical "Preview Full Report" button to edit mode's overview screen, wired to the same `handlePreviewReport`/`finishStep === 'report'` mechanism the create-mode button already uses: no new logic needed, since neither depends on whether `editSideId` is set. A misleading code comment claiming this was a deliberate create-mode-only scoping decision was corrected in the same pass.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.22. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: needs a real check that opening an already-saved side via Edit now shows "Preview Full Report" and that it correctly displays the declared stage.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a declared healing stage could be silently lost, root-caused to a fire-and-forget database write.** Direct report right after the duplicate-warning fix below shipped: "You seem to have removed what you just implemented. The stage information is missing again." Traced carefully before touching anything, since the two files that actually render the stage line (`SideBuilder.tsx`, `RecipeDepthReport.tsx`) hadn't been touched by that fix at all: the real cause was in the "Set My Stage" picker itself, from the prior pass: its `onPress` handler called `setConditionStage` with `void` (fire-and-forget) while updating the on-screen state immediately regardless of whether that write had actually finished. If anything interrupted the app before the write landed (a Metro reload mid-session, closing the app), the next real read of `user_condition_stages` came back with nothing, and the declared stage was really gone, not just a stale display.

Fixed by awaiting the real write and only updating local state once it's confirmed to have succeeded, with a visible failure alert instead of a silent one if it doesn't. The table's schema was checked directly too, confirming `condition_code TEXT PRIMARY KEY` correctly backs the `ON CONFLICT` upsert, ruling that out as a contributing factor.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.21. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this one specifically needs a real re-test: set a stage, confirm it shows, then background/reopen the app and confirm it's still there.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): duplicate stage-advisory warnings fixed by attributing each one to the ingredients that actually match it.** Direct on-device report after confirming the stage feature above worked: "it listed the same warning twice. Maybe it was for two different ingredients in the side... We don't want to list it twice, we want to list the ingredients that each match it." Root cause confirmed directly: `computeStageNotes` (`lib/recipeDepth.ts`) looped every ingredient and pushed a separate `{title, message}` advisory each time one matched, with no dedup and no ingredient name attached at all, since the underlying per-ingredient advisory functions were built for a single-food tap-to-explain context, never a whole-dish list. Two different ingredients tripping the identical real advisory produced two identical entries.

Fixed by deduping on the exact `{title, message}` pair (the same real advisory text is never coincidentally produced by two unrelated real triggers) and collecting every ingredient name that matched it, appending a plain "Matches: X, Y." to the message rather than repeating the whole warning per ingredient. The shared `ConditionStageAdvisory` type itself, and every other real place that calls the same underlying dispatcher for one food at a time, are untouched; this fix is scoped to the report's whole-dish aggregation.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on the touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.20. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a real in-place way to set a healing stage that's missing, right from the report.** Direct debugging exchange on the stage-context feature below: the user reported "I don't see it," which turned out to be a real Hashimoto's condition with no declared stage at all, confirmed directly rather than assumed to be a code bug. Direct follow-up once that was confirmed: "If the user doesn't have a stage chosen, there needs to be a way to tell them here, and give them a way to set it without losing their work."

A real distinction the report now draws that it couldn't before: `conditionsWithStagingModel` (new, `SideBuilder.tsx`) tells "no real staging model exists for this condition" (most of the 19, correctly silent) apart from "a real model exists but nothing's declared yet" (worth a real prompt). A condition in the second bucket now shows "No healing stage set for [Condition] yet" with a "Set My Stage" link, right inside its own block in the report.

Tapping it opens an in-place stage picker (a new `AppActionSheet`, reusing Profile's `CONDITION_STAGING_MODELS` stage list and stage labels) rather than sending the person to Profile: a named risk avoided rather than assumed away, since this in-progress side's state lives entirely in one component instance, with no route/param mechanism that would restore it if a navigation away ever unmounted it. Picking a stage writes it for real (`setConditionStage`) and updates the same `conditionStages` state the report's stage lookup already reads, so the report reflects the new stage immediately, with nothing lost and no navigation at all.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.19. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the Nutrition & Health Report now shows a person's declared healing stage per condition.** Direct follow-up to the chart-based redesign below: "There should be something about the stage they are in of their healing, such as for Hashimoto's stages of healing." A real, existing gap: the report already showed `stageNotes`, but those only ever appear when a specific ingredient trips a real stage-relevant flag, so someone whose dish trips nothing saw no stage context at all, even though the stage itself matters for reading the whole report (the same dish reads differently in Stage 2: Digging, removing triggers, than in Stage 3: Gut Repair, reintroducing them).

New `resolveDeclaredStage` (`lib/conditionStages.ts`) resolves a condition code plus a raw declared stage code into its real label and short description, reusing the same `CONDITION_STAGING_MODELS` registry Profile's stage picker already draws from, not a second copy. `SideBuilder.tsx` reuses the `conditionStages` state its own live per-ingredient advisory already fetches (no second `getConditionStages()` call) and resolves it per tracked condition via a `useMemo`. Each tracked condition's block in the report now leads with "Your stage: [label]" plus its short description whenever a real staging model and a declared stage both exist for that condition, ahead of its dimension chart; a condition with no staging model (most of them) or no declared stage yet shows nothing extra, an honest gap rather than an invented one.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.18. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the Nutrition & Safety Report rebuilt into a real, chart-based Nutrition & Health Report.** Direct pushback on the pilot's first version: "This looks pretty much like another version of the recipe. It needs to be a report about the nutrients. It does need to use some sort of graph instead of just writing it out. Visuals are far more effective. But to be honest, I was looking more for a PDF style of reporting feature, very professional looking, like a real health report as it applies to their conditions, so for Hashimoto's it should show how it does not cause problems or does cause them for the D1-D6." Scoped by direct follow-up: a styled in-app screen, not an exported PDF file for now (that stays the same deferred capability this app's architecture notes already name for the Reports tab, worth building once for both rather than twice).

**"D1-D6" checked directly, not assumed to generalize.** Querying the live database before building anything found D1-D6 is Hashimoto's real 6-dimension framework specifically: every other tracked condition has its own, differently-named real dimension set (as few as 1 real dimension for Gout/PCOS/Type 1/Type 2 Diabetes, up to 5 for Rheumatoid Arthritis), never called "D1-D6." The new `DimensionSeverity`/`dimensionBreakdown` (`lib/recipeDepth.ts`) is built generically around whichever real dimensions a condition actually owns, computed in the same pass that already queries `getFoodScoresForCondition` for cautions (no second query pass), taking the worst severity per dimension across the whole dish, the same "worst wins" reduction Insights' 6 Dimensions lens already uses.

**Two new chart components, both built on what already existed rather than a new dependency.** `components/DimensionChart.tsx`: a real SVG radar chart (`react-native-svg`, already used for Trends' line chart) for any condition with 3 or more real dimensions, one axis per dimension, a colored dot and filled polygon showing this dish's severity shape at a glance; a condition with fewer than 3 real dimensions (a degenerate radar) falls back to a plain colored row per dimension instead. Hashimoto's dimension labels ("D1 Micronutrient Density...") get their real "D1"-"D6" prefix as the short axis label; every other condition's longer, descriptive labels fall back to a plain position number on the chart with the full name spelled out in a legend underneath. `components/NutrientBarChart.tsx`: a real horizontal bar per nutrient against this person's daily target, mirroring Digest's established `DigestBarChart` bar-chart approach. New `getNutrientChartDataForIngredients` (`lib/db.ts`) is the same real %-of-target math `getNutritionHighlightsForIngredients` already used, generalized to return plain `{nutrient, percent}` pairs for a real chart instead of a short, pre-worded highlights list.

**The report itself dropped everything that just repeated the recipe.** No more ingredient bullet list, no more diet-tag badges, no more the generated caution sentence text (`"X: rated Imbalanced for..."`): the recipe card already shows the ingredients and diet tags, and the chart itself is now what explains a flagged dimension, not a restated sentence. What's left, per tracked condition: a plain verdict pill (Clean/Mild Caution/Caution/Not Recommended) plus its own real dimension chart, and, above that, the nutrient bar chart. Healing-stage notes stay, unchanged. The background-legibility fix and the curated-recipe identity guard from earlier the same day (see the entry directly below) both carry over unchanged.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched/created file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.17. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: worth a real look given how much of "professional, easy to read" is a visual judgment a type-checker can't verify, especially the radar chart's own SVG geometry on an actual phone screen.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the new Nutrition & Safety Report given a real, opaque background, plus a real guard against a modified side quietly keeping a curated recipe's name.** Two direct reports on the pilot shipped a few hours earlier the same day.

**(1) The report's background.** "None of the information in the new report can be read because none of it has any sort of background behind them at all and the food tab background is there so it is very hard to see or read." Confirmed directly: `RecipeDepthReport.tsx`'s own outer wrapper carried no fill at all, so every nested box (which, correctly, relies on an opaque parent rather than its own background, matching every other Food builder card in this app) sat directly on top of the Food tab's photo background instead. Fixed the same way every other builder screen already is: the whole report is now one solid `colors.surface` card (matching `SideBuilder.tsx`'s own `formCard`), with every nested section sitting on that real backdrop instead of the photo behind it.

**(2) A modified side can no longer quietly keep a curated recipe's name.** Direct instruction: "If they made a change to the system recipe, they need to save it as something else. Other than changing the number of servings for the meal, they need to not save over the system recipe." Checked directly before writing anything: there was never a real database-overwrite risk here (`handlePickCuratedRecipe`, the function "Build This Recipe" fires, never sets `editSideId`, so a save has always created a new `sides` row, never touched `curated_recipes` at all, which the app has no write path to anyway). The actual risk was identity confusion: a side loaded from a curated recipe, edited, and saved under the exact same name would look, in My Kitchen or a share, like the system recipe itself.

New `loadedFromCuratedRecipe` state (name plus a stable ingredient snapshot) is set the moment a curated recipe loads in. `finishSide` now blocks the save with a direct alert ("Give this a new name") whenever the dish name still matches the original recipe's name AND the ingredient list has changed since it loaded. Changing only servings, serving size, or the unit never trips this: comparing ingredients specifically, not servings, is what makes "still recognizably the same dish, just a different portion" the one exception named directly in the request. The check lives in `finishSide` itself, the one save function both the direct "Complete & Save This Side" button and the report's "Save As Is" converge on, so it applies uniformly to both.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched file. No Digest content or reference-database changes; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.16. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a shared live "recipe depth" engine, plus an optional pre-save Nutrition & Safety Report, piloted on Side Builder.** Direct question asked and answered honestly first: "Do all of the food builders wire into everything the same way that the recipes do now?" No: a builder-made dish saved through My Kitchen/My Favorites got only a thin, live, ungraded condition note (a hand-picked list of 7 "noteworthy" sub-criteria, no severity, no diet tags, never counted toward "Meals You Can Eat"), while the 300 curated recipes carry the full computed depth (severity-tiered `conditionCautions`, `safeForConditions`, `dietTags`, absolute exclusions, stage advisories) built by the offline compute scripts. Direct instruction: "The builders all absolutely must match the depth as is provided to the recipes, and it should happen every time the user creates anything," plus a proposal to add a pre-save report screen "so the user can see things clearly rather than try to build something into the builder view that crowds the screen." Scoped by direct follow-up: "Pilot it on Side Builder first, choice to create the report or not but both paths route to saving it."

**New `lib/recipeDepth.ts`, the one real engine, not a third copy of the same rules.** Investigated before writing anything: the curated-recipe compute scripts (`scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js`/`compute_recipe_diet_tags.js`) already document themselves as faithful ports of real, live TypeScript this app runs everywhere else: `getFoodScoresForCondition`/`getFoodScores` (`lib/db.ts`), `isFlaggedTier`/`tierSeverity` (`lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts`), and the per-condition stage-advisory dispatcher every Food builder's live preview already calls (`getConditionStageAdvisory`, `lib/conditionStageAdvisory.ts`). `computeRecipeDepth()` is mostly orchestration over those same real primitives, not new scoring logic: for each of a person's tracked conditions, scores every ingredient, applies the same severity tiers and the one real absolute exclusion (Celiac + High-Risk Gluten), and picks the worst hit; runs the same stage-advisory dispatcher across the whole ingredient list; computes diet tags via a faithful port of the diet-tag script's rule set (the one named duplication this introduces, since a plain Node script can't import a `.ts` module without its own build step, flagged directly in the new file's header comment, matching the same accepted duplication this project already carries elsewhere). Returns the exact same shape (`safeForConditions`/`conditionCautions`/`dietTags`/`stageNotes`) `RecipeCard` already uses.

**Computation always runs; viewing a report is the only optional part.** `depth_data_json` (nullable TEXT) added to all 11 real saved-record tables at once via the established `PRAGMA table_info` plus conditional `ALTER TABLE` pattern (the same precedent `instructions_json` already set 2026-08-17): only `sides` actually reads/writes it this pass, every other builder's column is just ready for its own later rollout. `saveSide`/`updateSide`/`getSide` and `resolveMealComponent` all thread a real `depthData` field through (the identical "only ever populated for 'side' so far" pattern `instructions` already established), and `BuilderFavoritePayload` carries it too, so a favorited side gets it "for free" the same way `instructions` already does. `SideBuilder.tsx`'s `finishSide` now always computes real depth data before saving, whether or not a report was ever opened: a new `buildDepthIngredients()` helper builds the one real ingredient shape both `finishSide` and the report's preview share, so the two paths can never compute this two different ways.

**The report itself: `components/RecipeDepthReport.tsx`**, a pure presentational component (no data-fetching of its own) so a future builder's rollout can render it unchanged. A new "Preview Full Report" button on Side Builder's final review screen (create mode only, per the pilot's scoping; edit mode keeps its existing no-confirmation save convention) computes the real depth live and opens a new `finishStep: 'report'` render branch: ingredients, yield, real diet-tag badges, nutrition highlights, then every one of the person's tracked conditions as a real row (green/yellow/red dot, reusing the exact `colors.statusGood`/`statusYellow`/`danger` palette "Meals You Can Eat" already established for this same concept, plus a distinct red "not safe at any amount" case for an absolute exclusion), then any real healing-stage notes. "Go Back and Adjust" returns to the review screen with nothing saved; "Save As Is" calls the same real `finishSide`, passing the already-computed depth through so it's never computed twice. Skipping the report entirely and tapping "Complete & Save This Side" directly still computes and stores the same real depth: both paths converge on one save function and one computation, not just a shared button.

**My Kitchen/My Favorites updated to prefer the new real depth over the old thin check once it exists**, rather than showing both: a saved side with real depth data now gets diet-tag badges and severity-graded condition cautions there too, with the old plain "may be worth a closer look" sentence replaced (not duplicated) the moment real depth data is present; a side saved before this shipped, or any of the other 10 builders, keeps the exact old behavior unchanged, an honest, named gap rather than a silent guess.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass, named directly rather than assumed:** rollout to the other 11 builders (the schema and the report component are both ready for it; only SideBuilder's wiring is pilot-specific); whether a person's clean builder creation should now also surface inside "Meals You Can Eat" alongside curated recipes (a separate decision about mixing personal data into a Digest-wide feature, not decided here); and the existing inline nutrition/condition preview already on Side Builder's review screen was left in place rather than trimmed now that the fuller report exists, a minor, named redundancy rather than something removed without being asked.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean with zero warnings on every touched/created file. No Digest content or reference-database changes, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION`/`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.15. **Not yet confirmed on-device**: this is a genuinely new, multi-part mechanism (a live multi-query computation, a new schema column, a new screen, a save-flow branch) worth a real walkthrough (building a side with a tracked condition and a real flag, previewing the report, saving both with and without previewing, then checking My Kitchen) before trusting it fully.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the remaining 4 hard-to-find ingredients named in the Bison audit swapped out, and the 5th (Cherry) kept with a substitution note.** Direct follow-up to the Bison audit below: "Cherry should stay anyway, with a note for if not found because maybe they aren't in season, then use plum or other options. Go ahead and swap all 5."

**Cherry, kept with a note.** All 3 recipes using tart/sour cherry (the wild-fermented tonic, the chicken-and-cherry side, and its tempeh counterpart) keep cherry as the actual ingredient; each ingredient line now names plum or another tart stone fruit as a stand-in when cherries are out of season, and the tonic's melatonin citation is explicitly flagged as specific to tart cherries, not the substitute, so the evidence claim never silently follows the swap.

**Duck, swapped to Chicken Breast (2 recipes).** An invisible-to-the-dish 1:1 swap, the same shape as Bison to Beef: title, teaser, ingredients, and instructions rewritten for a pan-seared or baked chicken breast instead of duck's skin-rendering technique, and the `nutritionHighlights` line that used to favorably compare duck's iron content against chicken breast was rewritten outright, since it stopped making sense once chicken breast became the dish's protein. A meaningfully different flag came out of this one, not assumed away: Duck (Raw)'s Oxidation Risk flag (Imbalanced) is gone once the ingredient is chicken breast, but chicken breast carries an Omega-3 vs 6 Imbalanced flag instead, relevant to a wider set of conditions (Hashimoto's, RA, cardiovascular disease, psoriasis, MS, lupus, Sjögren's, confirmed directly against the live `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` table, not guessed), so both recipes' `conditionCautions` changed shape, not just their wording, and were regenerated from the compute script rather than hand-edited to match.

**Burdock root, swapped to Chicory root (2 recipes), and Dandelion greens, swapped to Chicory greens (1 of those 2).** Beets, the first candidate considered, was rejected on a second look: Burdock's whole role in both recipes is being a bitter root (one explicitly frames itself as a bitter digestive tonic), and beets are sweet, not bitter, a swap that would have kept the ingredient "available" while breaking the actual reason either recipe exists. Chicory root is a standard-supermarket bitter root already sold as a coffee substitute, preserving the bitter-tonic framing honestly; chicory greens (the same plant's leaf) replaced Dandelion greens in the Ale recipe for the same reason, rather than Kale, which isn't bitter either. Since both replacement ingredients now come from one plant, the Ale recipe was renamed "Chicory Root and Greens Ale," and its dandelion-specific diuretic citation (Clare et al. 2009, about dandelion leaf extract specifically) was removed rather than left attached to an ingredient it was never studied on, replaced with a plain, uncited statement of chicory's well-established inulin content, matching the tonic recipe's own precedent of naming a claim as folk tradition rather than forcing an unearned citation onto it.

**Elderberry, kept as elderberry, not swapped.** Checked directly, not assumed: this recipe's citation (a meta-analysis on Sambucus nigra specifically) and its safety framing (raw elderberries are toxic and fermentation is the traditional way they're prepared at all, not a style choice) are both elderberry-specific and don't transfer to a substitute fruit, so swapping to a different berry would have either misapplied a citation to something it doesn't support, or dropped the recipe's whole point entirely. Asked directly rather than guessed past, since this touches the evidence-honesty rule head-on: the actual availability problem turned out to be that this database only has a fresh/raw elderberry row, and fresh elderberries are uncommon at an ordinary supermarket, while dried elderberries (the usual base for homemade elderberry syrup) are common at health food stores and online. The ingredient line now names dried, reconstituted elderberries as an available alternative to fresh or frozen, keeping the citation and the safety framing intact.

Every ingredient swap was verified against the live database directly, not assumed: `Chicken, broiler, breast, skinless, boneless, meat`, `Chicory roots`, and `Chicory greens` were each confirmed to already resolve cleanly in this app's reference database before being written in. `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js` and `scripts/apply_recipe_condition_cautions.js` (both already-established, already-idempotent machinery from the 2026-08-24/25 "Meals You Can Eat" work) were re-run against the updated database, rather than hand-deriving new `safeForConditions`/`conditionCautions` values, so every affected recipe's condition-safety data reflects the current ingredients rather than a guess. `curated_recipes.name`/`flavor_profile`/`health_benefit` (the separate database-side text shown through the Food builder's "Build This Recipe" flow, not just `recipes.ts`) were updated to match for all 4 swapped recipes, caught by checking rather than assuming `recipes.ts` was the only customer-facing copy. Two cross-references outside these 6 recipes were also caught and fixed: a vegan portobello-and-beets recipe's teaser and summary ("standing in for duck") and `lib/mealPlan.ts`'s header comment listing whole foods this plan deliberately reaches for (which had already dropped "bison" from an earlier pass, but still listed "duck").

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real Digest content changed); `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped (the live database itself changed again); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.14. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the 3 curated recipes using Bison swapped to Beef, plus a corpus-wide audit for similar hard-to-find ingredients.** Direct report: "I noticed 3 recipes that contained Bison. Bison could be something a person wouldn't really be able to get... Can we swap things like Bison out with a standard supermarket items? Please let me know if there is anything else like Bison."

Confirmed by direct query: exactly 3 curated recipes (`curated_side_bison_root_vegetable_bowl`, `curated_handheld_bison_roasted_vegetable_wrap`, `curated_side_bison_meatballs_herb_tomato_sauce`), all part of the 6-Week Meal Plan's weeks 3-6 content. Swapped to Beef Top Sirloin, already a real, used ingredient elsewhere in this same corpus, so the resolution path was already proven, not new. Checked against the reference database's six-dimension scores before trusting the swap wouldn't silently change anything: Bison Top Sirloin and Beef Top Sirloin score identically on every practically relevant sub-criterion (protein density, selenium, zinc, iodine, iron, sodium, saturated-fat-adjacent measures), so every stored `safeForConditions`/`conditionCautions` entry for these 3 recipes stays accurate without needing a fresh compute-script run. Every customer-facing mention (title, teaser, summary, ingredients, instructions, nutrition highlights, flavor notes) was rewritten by hand across all 3, not a blind find-replace, since two sentences specifically compared bison's leanness to beef and no longer made sense once bison, itself, became beef. Three more recipes (the vegan/vegetarian seitan, lentil-walnut, and tempeh counterparts to these same 3 dishes) referenced "instead of bison" in their own teasers and were updated to match. The database ingredient itself was updated too (`curated_recipe_ingredients`), so the swap is real, not just cosmetic text.

**The audit, reported honestly rather than silently acted on**: every one of the 210 distinct ingredients actually used across all 300 curated recipes was pulled by direct query and reviewed by eye for real supermarket availability. Two directly comparable to Bison, not carried by a standard supermarket: Burdock root (2 recipes, both fermented tonics -- an Asian-grocery/herbal-specialty item) and fresh Elderberry (1 recipe, a fermented tonic -- a foraged/farmers-market item, which also carries a real raw-toxicity caution worth naming on its own, separate from availability). Three more named at lower confidence, available at many but not every standard supermarket: Duck (2 recipes, frozen section of larger stores), fresh Sour/Tart Cherry (3 recipes, a short natural season with frozen pitted tart cherries as an easy, likely-already-stocked substitute), and Dandelion greens (1 recipe, the same dish as the burdock root one). Checked and deliberately not flagged: Tempeh (15 recipes), Seitan (7), Nutritional yeast (3), Spelt (2), Millet (3), and canned Hearts of palm (3) are all common enough at most major supermarkets today to not warrant a swap. Unlike Bison -> Beef, none of these 5 candidates has an equally invisible 1:1 substitute already in this database -- picking a real replacement changes each dish's identity, so this was named directly as a real, pending decision rather than guessed at unprompted.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real Digest content changed); `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped (the live database itself changed, forcing the same re-import on next launch every prior reference-database patch already does); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.13. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the Daily Meal Plan generator extended to real multi-day rotation and weekly frequency targets.** Direct follow-up: "we need to also follow specific timing for how often things should be eaten, such as fish twice a week. We are also trying to provide a real rotation of food randomly but also so they don't eat the same thing, it all needs to be wired to the 6 week, or really however many weeks up to 6, of days worth of meals." The generator (shipped a few hours earlier the same day) only ever produced one day at a time, with no memory between calls -- regenerating repeatedly could serve the same breakfast three days running, and had no concept of a weekly eating target at all.

**Two real, cited frequency rules**, deliberately a short list rather than an invented long one: fish/seafood at least twice a week (the American Heart Association's 2018 science advisory, PMID 29773586), red meat no more than 3 times a week (World Cancer Research Fund's standing guideline, with a real 2024 paper questioning that exact threshold's evidence strength, PMID 38492553, named honestly rather than presented as settled). A real content gap found and closed before either rule could work at all: the reference database has no dedicated "Fish" category, and its `subcategory` column (a real candidate, checked first) turned out unreliable -- both real salmon rows carry subcategory 'Meat & Poultry,' not 'Fish & Seafood,' a confirmed data-quality inconsistency that would have silently missed this app's most commonly used fish. Fixed with an explicit, verified base-name allowlist instead (`FISH_SEAFOOD_BASE_NAMES`/`RED_MEAT_BASE_NAMES`, `lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`): all 24 distinct 'Meat'-category ingredients actually used anywhere across the 300 curated recipes were pulled by direct query and classified by hand, not guessed. New `curatedRecipeContainsAnyIngredient` (`lib/db.ts`) checks the allowlist directly; the salmon-detection bug was caught and fixed before shipping, not after, by testing the query against a real salmon recipe and getting the wrong answer first.

**Real rotation**: a new `RotationState` (`usageCount` per curated recipe) threads through an entire multi-day generation run, and every meal slot's pick always prefers whatever's been used fewest times so far, only repeating a recipe once every real option in that pool has already been used at least once, the same "deal from a shuffled deck, don't reshuffle early" pattern a real weekly menu follows. Frequency-rule enforcement is real too, not just a soft suggestion: an at-most rule (red meat) hard-excludes anything that would push the week over its cap; an at-least rule (fish) forces a matching pick once the days left in the week no longer leave room to skip one and still hit the target, checked and updated meal by meal so lunch's pick correctly reduces what dinner still needs to force the same day. Both rules apply to lunch and dinner mains only, not breakfast or sides, a real, named scoping decision (fish and red meat aren't realistic candidates in either of those pools in this corpus).

**Architecture stayed one real implementation, not two.** `generateOneDay` (the actual single-day logic, unchanged in behavior) now optionally accepts rotation state; a plain single-day request passes none, leaving every candidate eligible exactly as before. `generateMealPlanDays` is the new multi-day loop (1 to 42 days, capped at the same real ceiling the existing 6-Week Meal Plan already uses), resolving candidate pools once, then calling `generateOneDay` once per day with one shared state, clearing the weekly frequency counters every 7 days. `generateDailyMealPlan` (single day) is now a thin wrapper around the same function, so the two paths can never quietly drift apart.

**UI**: the "Daily Meal Plan" Schedule lens gained a "How many days" picker (1 Day up to 6 Weeks); a single day still shows the full detailed view (health rating, nutrient coverage), while more than one day shows a condensed, color-dot-coded per-day list, matching this app's standing "tool areas use compact rows" rule. "Add to Schedule" now reuses `setUpMealPlan` directly (the exact same bulk-scheduling function the 6-Week Meal Plan already uses) for the whole generated range at once, not a new scheduling path. New help text names both frequency rules and their real citations directly, built from `FREQUENCY_RULES` itself rather than a second, hand-typed copy that could drift out of sync.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every new/touched file (`schedule.tsx`'s own pre-existing unescaped-entity errors elsewhere in the file, unchanged in count, confirmed to sit outside every line this pass touched). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.12; no Digest content or database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth a real, deliberate walkthrough given how much of this turns on real day-to-day state (rotation, weekly resets) a type-checker can't verify on its own.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): "Hashimoto's Disease" renamed to "Hashimoto's Thyroiditis" throughout the app.** Direct request: it's the name most commonly used clinically. Changed the canonical Digest category label (`lib/digest/index.ts`), the Digest help heading and a topic subgroup label (`purple-digest.tsx`), and the healing-stage framework's condition label (`lib/conditionStages.ts`). The one real risk checked directly before touching anything: `lib/digest/recipes.ts` already stored 312 stage-advisory strings (`condition: 'Hashimoto\'s Disease: Stage 2: Digging'`, and so on) built from that exact label by `compute_recipe_condition_data.js`; renaming the label alone would have silently broken every stage-specific note's own lookup, the same class of bug this project already hit and fixed once before (the 2026-08-24 stage-label-drift entry). All 312 were renamed to match in the same pass, verified by count (312 before, 0 left over, 312 now reading "Thyroiditis"), not assumed safe from the label change alone. `compute_recipe_condition_data.js`'s source-of-truth label updated too, so a future re-run stays consistent.

Deliberately left unchanged, and named directly rather than silently skipped: 4 occurrences that are quoted citation titles (`index.tsx`'s NIDDK source citation, two article titles in `lib/digest/history.ts`) actually titled "Hashimoto's Disease" by their publishers -- rewriting a citation's title to match this app's preferred terminology would misrepresent what the source actually says. Also left unchanged: a code comment in `purple-digest.tsx` that's a verbatim quote of an earlier direct request, and CLAUDE.md's existing status-log entries, which are historical record, not current app description. `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (stored Digest content changed); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.11; no database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a real, working Daily Meal Plan generator shipped end to end.** Direct request: "do we have any way to use the RDA of nutrients to build full day meal plans from the recipes available... without sugars being used in breakfast so no smoothies for breakfast but for lunches along with sides if necessary, and a choice on carbs or no carbs or low carbs with appropriate information about the health rating of the day's ingestion, all based on their eating style and selected conditions." Two architecture questions confirmed before building: a new, standalone, dynamic generator (not an extension of the fixed 6-Week Meal Plan, which isn't aware of a specific declared condition at all); the day's health rating reuses the existing green/yellow/red condition-safety tiers "Meals You Can Eat" already uses, not a new rating scale.

**Real infrastructure confirmed and reused, not rebuilt.** `resolveIngredientNutrientTotals`/`addNutrientTotalsInto` (already live in `lib/db.ts`, the same pipeline Trends already uses for logged and projected meals) compute nutrient totals for any set of ingredients; `getDietaryReferenceIntakesForCurrentUser` already returns age/sex-personalized NASEM DRI data. New `getCuratedRecipeNutrientTotals`/`curatedRecipeContainsSweetenerIngredient` (`lib/db.ts`) are the only two new database-touching functions this needed, both thin wrappers around what already existed.

**Two gaps found and closed, not assumed away.** First: a "Sugar" scoring sub-criterion is documented in `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts`'s citation table but does not actually exist anywhere in the live database (confirmed by direct query), so "no sugar at breakfast" could not be built on it. Fixed with a structural check instead: every curated recipe that actually adds honey, maple syrup, or sugar uses the reference database's "Sweets" ingredient category (confirmed directly), so `curatedRecipeContainsSweetenerIngredient` checks for that category rather than a total-sugar-gram threshold that would also have penalized a recipe's naturally-occurring fruit sugar, matching this app's standing distinction from the chrononutrition work ("a small amount of honey next to a large amount of whole fruit isn't the processed 'hidden sugar' the research is actually warning against").

Second: no per-recipe breakfast/lunch/dinner classification exists anywhere in this app. Builder type alone is not a reliable signal, confirmed directly: the breakfast dishes in this 300-recipe corpus (yogurt bowls, overnight oats, chia pudding, warm porridge, egg/tofu scrambles, breakfast burritos) overwhelmingly sit under the 'snack' builder type, this app's generic single-serving builder, not a dedicated breakfast one. Found via a direct SQL keyword search against every curated recipe's name, individually reviewed by title (not guessed), landing on 48 verified recipes now named directly in `BREAKFAST_ELIGIBLE_RECIPE_IDS` (`lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`), every one confirmed to resolve to a `linkedCuratedRecipeId` in `recipes.ts` before shipping.

**Carb-level thresholds are real, cited numbers, not invented ones.** "No Carbs" (under 50g/day) matches the same very-low-carbohydrate/ketogenic range this app's new Popular Diets comparison entry already cites from StatPearls. "Low Carb" (under 130g/day) matches the commonly-cited low-carbohydrate-diet threshold from a 2023 scoping review of the clinical literature (WebSearch-verified this session, not recalled from memory), applied to the whole day's total.

**The generator itself** (`generateDailyMealPlan`, `lib/dailyMealPlan.ts`): pools every recipe safe (green or yellow, never a red flag, matching the same "actively recommending is a stronger claim than passively listing" reasoning) for every declared condition at once, and compliant with every declared diet preference at once (reusing `recipeMatchesAllDietPreferences`, moved from `purple-digest.tsx` into `lib/digest/types.ts` this same pass so the "Meals You Can Eat" cross-filter and this generator share one implementation instead of two). Breakfast draws only from the verified 48, with a live sweetener check, never a smoothie (smoothies move to a lunch option instead, matching the direct instruction). Lunch and dinner draw from Salads & Bowls/Soups/Handhelds/Sides, plus Smoothies at lunch only, each paired with an optional Side when the main dish reads light (a named, direct judgment call: under roughly 400 kcal). A carb ceiling, when chosen, biases selection toward the lower-carb half of each candidate pool and tracks a running day total. Nutrient-coverage figures against the person's actual DRI targets are shown as informational data, not folded into the health rating itself, matching the confirmed design.

**New Schedule lens, "Daily Meal Plan"**, deliberately separate from "Meal Plan": a carb-level picker, "Generate My Day"/"Regenerate," the day's own green/yellow/red rating with plain-language warnings when something couldn't be fully satisfied, each pick shown with its own real carb grams, a real nutrient-coverage list, and "Add This Day to Schedule," which reuses the exact same `MealPlanSlot`/`addMealPlanDayToSchedule` machinery the 6-Week Meal Plan already built (`dailyMealPlanToMealPlanDay`, a real converter, not a second scheduling path).

**Named honestly, not silently assumed away:** the same lunch-side recipe could, in principle, also get picked as dinner's own side on the same day (only the lunch/dinner MAIN is excluded from re-selection, not its side) -- a minor, accepted limitation, not a correctness bug. This ships behind no real tier gate, same as everything else in this app today (no paid-tier enforcement exists anywhere yet). `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every new/touched file (`schedule.tsx`'s own pre-existing unescaped-entity errors elsewhere in the file, confirmed via `git diff` to sit outside every line this pass touched, left alone per this project's own standing precedent for that file). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.10; no Digest content or database changes, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION`/`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- this is a genuinely new, multi-part feature (live nutrient computation, a new generator algorithm, a new UI lens, real scheduling) worth a real walkthrough before trusting it fully.
