**Most recent (2026-08-25): "Meals You Can Eat" now respects a declared diet preference, not just the condition.** Direct request: "if I select vegan from Profile, and I select that my condition is Hashimoto's, and then I go into Digest > Hashimoto's Disease > Meals You Can Eat, I should only see recipes that comply with both vegan and Hashimoto's. The same applies for every other dietary preference and condition selected from profile." Confirmed real: "Meals You Can Eat" already silently applied a declared healing stage, but never a declared diet preference at all, despite `RecipeCard.dietTags` already existing for every one of the 300 curated recipes and Profile's "Diet Preferences" card already collecting exactly this.

New `recipeMatchesDietPreference`/`recipeMatchesAllDietPreferences` (`purple-digest.tsx`), applied inside `mealsYouCanEatForCondition` before its existing safe/yellow/red sort. A recipe passes only if it satisfies every declared preference at once (someone both vegan and dairy-free needs both). The one real subtlety: the base tier (Vegan/Vegetarian/Omnivore) is the single place in the `RecipeDietTag` vocabulary with a genuine implied-looser-tag relationship (a recipe carries exactly one of the three, vegan implying vegetarian-safe), so a plain equality check would have wrongly hidden a Vegan-tagged recipe from someone who declared Vegetarian, and would have wrongly hidden everything but Omnivore-tagged recipes from someone who declared Omnivore despite an omnivore diner having no real restriction on this axis at all. `BASE_DIET_TIER_RANK` encodes the real ordering (Vegan stricter than Vegetarian stricter than Omnivore) so a recipe qualifies whenever its own tag is at least as restrictive as the declared preference; every other tag (Mediterranean, Gluten-Free, Paleo, AIP, and so on) is computed independently with no implied looser tag, where a direct match is already correct.

`dietPreferences` (Profile's state, already read into this screen for the separate Recipes-lens filter default) is threaded through `groupEntriesForLens` → `groupConditionEntries` → `mealsYouCanEatForCondition`, reaching all three real call sites (the main drilled-in render, and both category-scoped-search computations) so the topic-menu count, the drilled-in sub-shelves, and search within "Meals You Can Eat" all agree with each other rather than one silently showing a stale, wider number. The plain Recipes lens's separate "Filter by diet" picker is untouched, a different, already-working, user-controlled mechanism, not what was reported.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean (two `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings caught and fixed by adding `dietPreferences` to both affected `useMemo` dependency arrays, not silently disabled). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.9; no Digest content or database changes, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): a real, evidence-traced side-by-side diet comparison, plus subtopic sectioning extended from Basic Health to 13 conditions' oversized topics, 18 more shelves.** Two direct follow-ups in one message: "Yes, throughout the Digest. Everything must have continuity throughout the Digest," and "build the side by side comparison of the different eating styles based on evidence and without assumptions being made. Trace everything back to published peer reviewed data."

**Part 1: subtopic sectioning extended to conditions.** The same real per-(condition, topic) count run for Basic Health found the identical "one continuous scrolling shelf" problem inside 18 separate shelves across 13 conditions, several worse than anything Basic Health had: prostateHealth's Core Science bucket alone held 30 entries in one shelf, hashimotos' Diet & Food held 26, hashimotos' Self-Advocacy & Testing held 25. A new `CONDITION_TOPIC_SUBGROUPS` lookup (`purple-digest.tsx`) hooks into `groupConditionEntries` the same way "Meals You Can Eat" already proved out for a condition page: every entry's exact id is assigned to a specific, named subgroup, '::'-joined under its topic label, reusing the exact rendering, menu-collapsing, and search-scoping logic already built and shipped, with zero new render code needed. Every one of the 18 groupings (74 subgroups, 306 entries total) was designed by reading every title in that shelf, not guessed from the topic name, and every single id was verified programmatically against the corpus before shipping: 306 expected, 306 written, zero dropped, zero duplicated, zero mismatched. A safety net (`applyConditionTopicSubgroups`'s "Other" bucket) means a future entry added to an already-subgrouped topic without an assigned subgroup still shows, rather than silently vanishing the way it would have with Basic Health's "More" mechanism.

Named honestly: most (condition, topic) pairs are small (2-10 entries) and were deliberately left as one flat shelf, the same judgment call Basic Health's smaller topics were left under. A new standing rule (below) makes this an ongoing discipline, not a one-time sweep.

**Part 2: the side-by-side diet comparison**, closing the gap found the same day when asked "where is it that all of the types of diets are talked about and compared nutritionally": 17 individual diet write-ups existed, but nothing actually compared them against each other. Two new entries in `lib/digest/popularDiets.ts`. The first, `diet-headtohead-network-metaanalysis`, surfaces the one real trial that tests multiple named diets against each other directly rather than each one alone against no intervention: Ge et al., BMJ 2020;369:m696 (PMID 32238384), a network meta-analysis of 121 randomized trials and 21,942 participants across 14 named diet programmes. Independently cross-checked against two separate sources before writing anything down: roughly 4-5kg weight loss at 6 months for low-carbohydrate and low-fat patterns alike, Atkins/DASH/Zone the strongest individually-named diets, no diet significantly improving HDL or CRP, Mediterranean alone keeping its LDL benefit past 6 months, and the authors' own direct quote that differences between named diets were "generally trivial to small." A real correction to this paper was found (BMJ 2020;370:m3095) and named directly in the entry itself even though its exact content couldn't be confirmed after multiple searches, rather than silently citing the original as if no correction existed.

The second, `diet-sidebyside-comparison`, synthesizes what the category's 17 existing entries already established into one comparison across three axes: composition (keto's three distinct clinical variants from StatPearls, Paleo's actual published macronutrient range from a 2023 cross-cultural analysis, high-protein's RDA-versus-muscle-preservation target, with diets defined by food inclusion rather than a fixed ratio reported honestly as exactly that, not forced into an invented percentage), evidence-tier comparison (reusing each diet's already-assigned tier rather than a fresh judgment call), and documented deficiency risk (the vegan-versus-vegetarian B12 gap, Paleo/AIP's shared dairy elimination, carnivore's total fiber elimination, unplanned gluten-free's documented nutritional cost). Every figure traces to a specific, checkable source, several reused directly from citations this category had already verified rather than re-derived. Both new entries lead a new "Comparing Them Side by Side" subgroup, added to the same Popular Diets & Eating Styles subtopic structure built earlier the same day.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. Every new `relatedIds` reference, and the full corpus's now-3,213 references, reconfirmed to resolve. Field-scoped self-check for the standing filler-word/dash/"own" writing rules run and confirmed clean on both new entries before shipping. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real content changed); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.8; no database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): 7 of Basic Health's biggest topics split into real subtopic sections, closing a follow-up question with a standing rule.** Direct follow-up after asking "where is it that all of the types of diets are talked about and compared nutritionally": "please separate them into their own sections... groups of information that is specific to one diet or eating style or another, rather than one continuous scrolling left to right list of them... This needs to be followed throughout the digest."

A real count (a Node script pairing every entry's own `id` with its `category` across all of `lib/digest/*.ts`, not a naive prefix grep, which overcounted since several ids share a prefix with a different category's own content) found Popular Diets & Eating Styles was far from the worst offender: **Prevention & Lifestyle by Condition** was one 38-entry flat shelf (its own description already said "organized by condition," nothing enforced it), and 6 more Basic Health topics sat at 15-20 entries each with no internal division at all: How Your Body Works: Organs & Systems (20), Food Additives (18), Fruits/Vegetables/Nuts & Seeds (18), Mental Health & Food (18), Popular Diets & Eating Styles (19, 17 `diet-` plus 2 `pbn-`), Fermented Foods (15).

All 7 given real `BasicHealthTopic.subtopics`, the same mechanism Essential Nutrients already established (2026-08-24), grouped by whatever genuinely distinguishes that topic's own content, not an arbitrary split: Prevention by the 19 tracked conditions themselves (trivial, exact, since every `prevention-`/`apphelps-` id already carries its own condition abbreviation); Organs & Systems by organ/body system (14 subtopics, plus a small "Overview & Big Picture" for the 2 entries belonging to no single organ); Popular Diets by what actually distinguishes a diet nutritionally (how much animal food, traditional whole-food patterns, elimination diets, timing versus composition, macronutrient ratio, food quality independent of macros, this app's own tracking philosophy); Food Additives by additive type (sweeteners, preservatives, emulsifiers/gums, flavor/color/dough agents, ultra-processing as a whole); Fermented Foods by bacterial genus/culture type, matching what this topic's own description already claimed but never enforced; Fruits/Vegetables/Nuts & Seeds by food type; Mental Health & Food by theme (ADHD/OCD, gut-brain mechanisms, nutrients, diet-pattern evidence, when to seek help).

Every one of the 7 new subtopic lists was verified programmatically against the real corpus before shipping, not assumed correct from the prefixes reading right on paper: a script cross-checked that every real entry under each topic's old flat prefix is captured by exactly one new subtopic, zero dropped, zero duplicated. `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file.

**Named honestly, not silently assumed clean:** the same "one continuous scrolling shelf" problem almost certainly also exists across the 19 condition pages' own topics (Hashimoto's alone carries roughly 176 entries across a handful of topics, an average well past the threshold that triggered this fix in Basic Health) -- not audited this pass, since designing a real, non-arbitrary subtopic split per condition-topic is a much larger effort than this batch, closer in scale to this project's own other "ongoing, multi-session" pushes. A new standing rule captures the discipline going forward (Standing rules, below) so this doesn't silently regress as new content is added, and so the condition-page half of it stays a visible, tracked next step rather than dropped. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.7; no Digest content or database changes (a pure display-grouping change, the underlying entries themselves are untouched). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the remaining bold-plus-drop-shadow pill in Digest fixed.** Direct report: "Everywhere that you made a small tag pill with bold and dropshadowed text in Digest, remove the bold. I see it specifically on the search returned match pills," the same "drop shadow only if not already bolded" rule the diet-tag pills were already fixed under a few hours earlier the same day. A full sweep of every `menuLabelShadow` use in this file (11 styles) plus every `fontWeight: '700'/'600'/'bold'` use (4 more) found exactly one real violation, not several: `matchTermPillTextTitle`, the filled variant a category-scoped search's own match pill switches to once a search term actually hits that entry's title (`matchTermPill`, `purple-digest.tsx`), carried its own `fontWeight: '700'` layered on top of `matchTermPillText`'s already-shadowed base style. `dietTagPillText` and `crossConditionPillText`, the app's other two real pill-text styles, were both already correctly on one side of the rule or the other (`dietTagPillText` is bold with no shadow; `crossConditionPillText` is shadowed with no bold) and needed no change. Fixed by dropping the bold from `matchTermPillTextTitle`, leaving its shadow (inherited from `matchTermPillText`) as the only emphasis. `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.6; no Digest content or database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the "Worth knowing" cross-condition leak, still real after the first fix, root-caused to a second render path the first pass never touched.** Direct, angry follow-up after the fix below shipped and was reported fixed: "Baked Salmon with Leaks and Lemon STILL HAS THE ERONEOUS REFERERENCE TO 'IF YOU HAVE' CHRONIC KIDNEY DISEASE." The earlier fix (entry immediately below) was real and correct as far as it went, `activeConditionCode` genuinely does scope `conditionNotes` correctly wherever it's passed, confirmed again by tracing this exact recipe's exact stored data by hand. The bug survived because it was only being passed on one of two `BasicHealthShelves` call sites that render a condition's own recipe cards: the ordinary drilled-in topic view (`selectedTopicGroup !== null` branch) got it, but the category-scoped **search results** branch (`categorySearchGroups`, reached the instant someone types into the search box while inside a condition's own page, including inside "Meals You Can Eat") never computed or passed `activeConditionCode`/`activeStageCode` at all. `conditionNoteAppliesTo` defaults to "show everything" when `activeConditionCode` is undefined, so any condition note attached to a recipe surfaced by search rendered unscoped, reproducing the identical symptom through a path the first fix's own testing never exercised (I verified the DIRECT topic-drilldown path, not the search path).

Fixed by hoisting one shared `activeConditionCode`/`activeStageCode` pair (derived once from `DIGEST_KEY_TO_CONDITION_CODE[lens]`, same lookup either branch already needed) and passing it to both `BasicHealthShelves` call sites instead of one; the topic-drilldown branch's own local, duplicate computation was removed in favor of the shared one. `tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing, unrelated errors aside), `eslint` clean on the touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.5; no Digest content changed, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bump; no database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- given this is the second time this exact symptom was reported after being called fixed, worth a direct, deliberate re-check against the same recipe from the same condition page, both by tapping into it directly from the shelf and by finding it through search, before trusting this closed.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the "Worth knowing" box scoped to the active condition, plus the diet-tag pills fixed and made real links.** Also: the dev server restarted (had been running since 2026-08-23, two days stale across multiple sessions of fixes -- "I don't see a difference on my screen" was a real symptom of that, not a code bug). Two direct reports.

**(1) "If there is a warning, it absolutely must ONLY be for that conditions. There shouldn't be random warnings on Hashimoto's (or any other condition) for other conditions if they might have them, too."** Confirmed real: `recipeCard.conditionNotes`' own "Worth knowing if you have..." box rendered every hand-written and computed note on a recipe at once, unconditionally, regardless of which condition's own page the recipe was opened from -- a Hashimoto's recipe would show its real Gout/RA/CVD notes too, just because nothing scoped the list. A direct audit of every real `condition:` string actually used across `recipes.ts` found the fix needed more than an exact-match filter: several hand-written entries are compound, prose-style labels ("Type 2 Diabetes / PCOS", "Inflammatory Bowel Disease / Irritable Bowel Syndrome / Celiac") rather than one clean tag, and some use a shorter form than this Digest's own canonical label ("Hashimoto's" alone, not "Hashimoto's Disease"). New `CONDITION_NOTE_KEYWORDS` (one confirmed-real substring per condition) plus `conditionNoteAppliesTo`, threaded through as a new `activeConditionCode` prop on `RecipeCardDetail`: a note that names one or more specific tracked conditions now shows only when the active condition is among them; a note that names none at all ("Pregnancy," "Anyone taking levothyroxine," "Any autoimmune condition") is a genuinely general caution, not a warning for some other condition, so it still shows everywhere, unchanged. Verified directly against real observed strings before shipping, not just trusted from the logic reading correct on paper.

**(2) The diet-tag pills under "Build This Recipe": styling, alignment, and broken linking, all in the same report.** "Drop shadowed is fine only if the font is not already bolded" -- `dietTagPillText` was carrying both `menuLabelShadow` and `fontWeight: '700'` at once; the shadow is now removed, since bold text on a solid, opaque `TAB_COLOR` fill already reads clearly without one. "Haphazardly placed there as if poured out onto the table" -- `dietTagRow` gained `alignItems: 'flex-start'` (a wrapped second row was stretching to match the tallest pill on the line above it) and `RecipeDietTagRow` itself now always re-sorts its own tags against `RECIPE_DIET_TAGS`' single canonical order before rendering, rather than trusting whatever order happened to already be stored per recipe.

"They are linked somehow but not to what they should be linked to... Each of those are whether or not they are part of a certain type of diet, so they should link to an explanation about that diet type, but should then always have a way to get back to the recipe they started from." The pills carried no `onPress` at all before this pass, despite reading as tappable -- now each one genuinely opens its own diet's real explanation. Five of the ten real `RecipeDietTag` values (Vegan, Plant-Based/Flexitarian, Mediterranean, AIP, High-Protein) already had a matching entry in this Digest's own Popular Diets & Eating Styles topic; the other five (Vegetarian, Omnivore, Gluten-Free, Dairy-Free, Paleo) had none anywhere in the whole corpus, confirmed by direct search, not assumed. Rather than five working links and five dead ends, all five were researched and written fresh this pass (`lib/digest/popularDiets.ts`), matching that topic's own citation and evidence-tiering discipline: Vegetarian and Dairy-Free reuse citations this app had already verified elsewhere (the same vegan cardiovascular meta-analysis, the federal NIDDK's own lactose-intolerance page); Omnivore, Gluten-Free, and Paleo needed fresh WebSearch verification, and one real citation trap was caught along the way, an initial lactose-prevalence paper turned out to be a retracted Lancet Gastroenterology & Hepatology study, replaced with the NIDDK's own non-retracted figure before it ever shipped.

New `DIET_TAG_ENTRY_ID` maps all ten real tags to their entry. Deliberately opens the explanation in the same `showInfoAlert` overlay this app already uses for tap-to-explain content elsewhere (`DimensionFlags`' own `onExplain`), not a full `jumpToRelated` navigation away from the recipe -- this screen's own lens/topic/search state is already intricate enough that a real navigation-history feature would have been a real, separate risk, while an overlay makes "a way to get back to the recipe" trivially, unconditionally true: closing it is being back.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file. Every new `relatedIds` entry, and the full corpus's existing ~3,200 `relatedIds` references, reconfirmed to resolve before shipping. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real content changed); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.4; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device**, though the dev-server restart specifically should already be visible.

**Most recent (2026-08-25): the raw-vs-cooked fix actually built (not just named), plus a real absolute-exclusion rule for Celiac.** Direct, insistent follow-up, quoting this document's own prior entry back: "You said 'fixing it for real would mean going through roughly 2,000 ingredient rows across 300 recipes and tagging each one against what the recipe's own instructions actually do.' Fix it." Alongside: "If they cannot eat it at all, it should not be listed in the safe zone for them at all."

**The real scope turned out much smaller than the original, honest-but-untested estimate.** Rather than 2,000 rows, a direct query found only 39 of the 210 distinct ingredients actually used across all 300 curated recipes have a real, flag-crossing difference between their Raw score and any other real prep-method score (checked against every sub-criterion, not just goitrogens) -- everything else is either raw-only in this database, has no cooked variant at all, or scores identically either way. Of those 39, only the ones genuinely used in a recipe that demonstrably cooks them needed a fix: 73 recipes use one of the 39, and every one of those 73 was individually read (its own real ingredients list and its own real instructions, both already in `lib/digest/recipes.ts`) to confirm what it actually does, not assumed from its name. A kale salad that only massages the kale, a smoothie that blends spinach raw, a collard leaf used as an uncooked wrap were each correctly left alone; 64 recipes covering 70 real ingredient uses (goitrogenic vegetables actually roasted or boiled, legumes actually cooked or canned, sautéed spinach) needed a real correction.

New `RECIPE_PREP_OVERRIDES` (`compute_recipe_condition_data.js`), one entry per (recipe, ingredient) pair naming the real prep method that recipe's own instructions describe, resolved via a new `resolveWithPrepOverride` that tries the named prep method first and falls back to any other real non-Raw variant (every one of the 39 ingredients was confirmed to score identically across its own real non-Raw variants for the specific sub-criteria that matter, so which exact variant resolves doesn't change the outcome). The shared, ingredient-pair-wide resolution cache stays the default for the other ~140 recipes with no sensitive ingredient, unaffected.

**Real, confirmed impact, not assumed:** Celiac's own safe list grew from 244 to 267 (legume/lectin fixes, mostly), IBD from 273 to 298, IBS from 262 to 287, Hashimoto's yellow-tier grew from 69 to 90 while red-tier dropped from 213 to 191 (goitrogenic vegetables correctly cooking out of their red flag, legumes correctly downgrading from High Risk to Mild Risk lectins once resolved as cooked/canned). Spot-checked directly, not just trusted from the aggregate counts: `curated_ferment_sauerkraut` now correctly shows safe for Hashimoto's (fermented cabbage's real goitrogenic status resolves to "Not Assessed," not the raw-only "Goitrogenic" tier); `curated_side_lemon_garlic_broccoli` no longer flags for broccoli at all once roasted, surfacing a different, real, previously-hidden concern (olive oil's own omega-3-to-6 ratio) instead.

**The second, distinct correction: "If they cannot eat it at all, it should not be listed in the safe zone for them at all."** Correct, and a real, separate category from severity: yellow and red both still describe a real recipe genuinely under "Meals You Can Eat," just with more or less caution attached, honest for the overwhelming majority of this app's own flags (a matter of degree, timing, or individual trial). Celiac disease's own relationship to gluten is categorically different, established medical consensus, not a matter of degree: any amount of gluten triggers continued autoimmune gut damage, with no dose or preparation that makes it safe. New `ABSOLUTE_EXCLUSIONS`, deliberately a short, named list (currently exactly one entry: `celiac` + `Gluten: High Risk`), checked against every other condition's own real, already-published Digest research before writing it -- Hashimoto's own gluten/dairy flags, for comparison, are explicitly framed throughout this app's own content as a real, individually-reintroducible elimination-diet trial, not a permanent ban, so gluten stays a real caution there, not an exclusion. A recipe matching an absolute exclusion is now skipped entirely for that one condition: no `safeForConditions` entry, no `conditionCautions` entry, genuinely invisible under that condition's own "Meals You Can Eat," the same as a condition with no real coverage at all. Confirmed directly: 31 gluten-containing recipes are now fully absent from Celiac's own "Meals You Can Eat" (was 31 red-tier recipes a few hours earlier the same day); Rheumatoid Arthritis and Psoriasis, which also flag gluten, correctly keep their own 31 recipes in the red tier rather than excluding them, matching the elimination-diet-trial framing that's actually true for those two.

**A real bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after:** `apply_recipe_condition_cautions.js`'s own first version only ever wrote the `conditionCautions` field, passing the existing `safeForConditions` line through unchanged on the assumption it never needed rewriting once first applied. That assumption broke the moment this same pass started correcting `safeForConditions` itself (the prep-method fixes, the new exclusions) -- silently leaving `recipes.ts` internally inconsistent, several dozen recipes newly safe in the computed data but stuck showing neither safe nor cautioned in the shipped file, invisible under "Meals You Can Eat" for exactly the conditions this fix was supposed to add them back to. Caught by a full, direct cross-check of all 300 recipes' own `safeForConditions` arrays in `recipes.ts` against the freshly computed JSON before considering this done (not assumed correct from a passing `tsc`), fixed by making the script rewrite that line from fresh data too, and reconfirmed idempotent afterward (zero diff on a repeat run).

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two scripts' own pre-existing `__dirname`/unused-var pattern, unchanged). `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real Digest content changed); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.3; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): "Meals You Can Eat" now separates genuinely clean recipes from real, severity-graded cautions, instead of showing all 300 as equally eatable.** Direct, insistent report: "All of the conditions list all 300 meals saying they can eat all of them. That cannot be... maybe it could be IF THERE ARE THINGS SUCH AS COOKING THEM A CERTAIN WAY THAT MAKES THEM SAFE. And they aren't separated into stages of when, during their healing process, is it ok for them to start eating them." Correct, and a real design flaw in the fix shipped a few hours earlier the same day: treating every flagged recipe as one undifferentiated "caution" made a serious, well-documented, never-safe-in-any-amount concern (Gluten: High Risk for Celiac) read exactly the same as a mild, portion-aware one (Sodium: Moderate) -- both just showed up in "Meals You Can Eat" with a caption.

**A real, honest data limitation investigated and named directly, not silently worked around.** The "cooking a certain way makes it safe" idea is genuinely correct for some flags (goitrogens specifically -- cooking substantially reduces the concern, already stated in the caution text itself), but this app's own curated-recipe data has no structured way to know whether a given ingredient is used raw or cooked in a specific recipe: `curated_recipe_ingredients` stores only category and base_name, and the real, already-shipped ingredient-resolution function this app uses everywhere (`resolveCuratedRecipeIngredient` in `lib/db.ts`, confirmed by reading it directly, not guessed) always tries the 'Raw' prep-method row first, regardless of what a recipe's own written instructions actually do with that ingredient. This is a genuine, pre-existing, app-wide limitation (affects nutrition display and the Food-builder import too, not just this feature), not something a quick patch can honestly fix without either adding real prep-method tracking to ~2,000+ ingredient rows across 300 recipes or guessing, which this app's own standing evidence-honesty discipline rules out. Named directly rather than glossed over.

**The fix that IS honestly buildable: real severity, not just a caption.** `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js` now records a real `severity` ('yellow' | 'red') alongside every computed caution -- 'red' when at least one flagged hit for that condition is genuinely serious (this app's own RED_TIERS), 'yellow' when every hit that fired was the milder tier. `RecipeCard.conditionCautions` is now `Record<string, {severity, note}>`, not a plain string (`lib/digest/types.ts`). A new, separate `apply_recipe_condition_cautions.js` (rewritten for the nested shape, re-verified idempotent) writes it in.

"Meals You Can Eat" now shows three real, visually distinct tiers per condition: genuinely clean recipes lead, then yellow-severity ones, then red-severity ones last -- confirmed directly, not assumed: Hashimoto's is 18 safe / 69 yellow / 213 red (not "300 you can eat"); Celiac is 244 safe / 25 yellow / 31 red, and every one of those 31 red recipes is confirmed to actually contain gluten, exactly the trigger that should never read as casually eatable. Ten narrow-criteria conditions (CKD, Fatty Liver, Gout, Migraine, PCOS, Type 1/2 Diabetes, and 3 more) genuinely do stay at or near 300/300 safe, an honest reflection of this app's own whole-food recipe library against those conditions' own narrower real criteria sets, not something to force differently.

Each severity is now visible in two real places, not just buried in caption text: a color dot on the shelf card itself (`colors.statusGood`/`statusYellow`/`danger`, the same already-established, contrast-verified palette `DimensionFlags.tsx` uses for the identical concept, deliberately a different color family from the existing evidence-tier dot, which measures a completely different thing) and a distinctly colored, distinctly labeled caution box in the recipe's own detail view ("Worth knowing" for yellow, "Approach with caution" for red, reusing the same statusYellowBg/statusRedBg dark-theme-safe fill DimensionFlags already established). A real contrast bug caught before shipping, not after: the first attempt used `colors.statusYellow` directly as label text on `statusYellowBg`, measuring a genuinely illegible 1.59:1 (that token was only ever verified as a border color elsewhere, never as text) -- corrected to `colors.statusYellowStandalone` (5.67:1) after actually computing the contrast math rather than assuming the pairing was safe.

The prior same-day stage-awareness (a declared healing stage sorting a stage-flagged recipe behind others, and preferring the richer stage-specific note) is preserved, now applied within each of the yellow/red tiers independently via a shared `orderCautionTier` helper. A genuine literal "these foods unlock as you progress through stages" grouping was considered and deliberately not built: the underlying stage-advisory data doesn't actually support that story (Hashimoto's Digging and Gut Repair stages both flag the same triggers, gluten/dairy, just with different framing, removal versus reintroduction, not a strict "becomes safe later" sequence) -- forcing that structure would have meant inventing a progression this app's own real data doesn't show, against its own standing citation/evidence discipline. Named directly as a real, considered non-fix rather than silently skipped.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two scripts' own pre-existing `__dirname`/unused-var pattern, unchanged). `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (real Digest content changed this time); `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.2; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-25): "Meals You Can Eat" split into real sub-groups by Food builder, plus a spacing bug from the prior day's "Putting It Together" removal fixed.** Two direct reports in one message.

**(1) A spacing bug from 2026-08-24's own "Putting It Together" heading removal.** "You got rid of the Putting it together and then moved the next thing up to the spot where it was and it is now touching the one above it instead of having the same distance as the others have from each other." Root cause, confirmed by reading the actual styles rather than re-guessed: the removed heading had been the only thing providing visual separation between a category's own top-level `DigestTopicMenu` and its closing "tying together" card below it -- `styles.shelfSection` (the wrapper that card reuses) only ever carries a `marginBottom` (correct for `BasicHealthShelves`' own multiple shelves, which only ever need space AFTER each other), never a `marginTop`, so once the heading's own spacing was gone, the card sat flush against the menu's last row. Fixed with a new, narrowly scoped `tyingTogetherAfterMenu` style (`marginTop: 18, marginBottom: 18`, matching the same 18px this screen already uses everywhere else to separate one section from its neighbor) applied only at this one call site -- deliberately not by adding `marginTop` to `shelfSection` itself, which would have doubled the already-correct gap between two ordinary shelves inside `BasicHealthShelves` elsewhere.

**(2) "The meals you can eat should be separated into groups specific to what they are, such as related to the Food builders that they would open into."** Confirmed real: "Meals You Can Eat" was one flat, unbroken shelf of up to 300 recipes per condition, with no way to jump straight to, say, just the sides. Fixed by reusing the exact same classification the plain Recipes category already uses for its own topic menu (`classifyRecipesTopic`/`RECIPES_TOPIC_ORDER`: Sides, Salads & Bowls, Smoothies, Fermentation, Beverages, Snacks, Baked Goods, Soups, Sauces, Handhelds, Desserts), so a recipe's own sub-group here always matches the exact Food builder it would actually open into. `groupConditionEntries` now emits one real `'::'`-joined sub-topic per non-empty builder type under the "Meals You Can Eat" label (`Meals You Can Eat::Sides`, and so on) instead of one flat bucket, the same real nesting convention Essential Nutrients' own 22 subtopics already established -- `shelfHeadingLabel`'s existing `::`-stripping logic needed zero changes to display each one correctly. Within each sub-shelf, the same clean-first-then-cautioned priority order from the two prior sessions' own work is preserved exactly, since entries are bucketed in the order they already arrive in, never independently re-sorted.

Picking "Meals You Can Eat" from a condition's own top-level topic menu now shows every one of its own non-empty sub-shelves stacked together at once (Sides, Salads & Bowls, and so on, each independently horizontally scrollable), not a further click-through subtopic menu the way Essential Nutrients requires -- a deliberate, scoped choice: at most 12 sub-groups here (fewer than Essential Nutrients' 22, the case that specifically needed an extra menu step to stay fast), and every shelf is already virtualized (the 2026-08-23 `FlatList` fix), so the same performance concern that drove Essential Nutrients' own extra step doesn't apply here at the same severity. The top-level topic menu itself still shows exactly one "Meals You Can Eat" row with a real, correctly summed total count, via a new, generic `collapseTopicsForMenu` helper (mirroring `basicHealthMenuGroups`' own established collapsing technique, order-preserving rather than alphabetized, matching this generic path's own "keep each category's curated order" rule).

**Two real regressions caught and fixed before shipping, not after:** the two category-scoped search useMemos (`categorySearchScopeEntries`, `categorySearchGroups`) both used to resolve "Meals You Can Eat" via an exact label match, which would have silently found nothing once the label became several `'::'`-joined sub-topics instead of one flat string, one falling back to searching the WHOLE category instead of just this one topic, the other returning zero results outright. Both fixed the same way `BasicHealthShelves`' own drill-in filter was, matching on the label's own top-level segment (`label.split('::')[0]`) rather than requiring an exact match, combining every matching sub-topic's own entries.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the one touched file. No Digest content changed, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bump; `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.26.1 (day segment 26, a new calendar day). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): "Meals You Can Eat" fixed from a hard gate into a real advisory list, across all 19 conditions.** Direct report: "I looked at meals you can eat in Hashimoto's and it literally only has a bunch of fermented drinks listed... they are far from being meals... What they can eat is exactly that, everything they can eat, at the levels of healing that they need to start from and achieve along the way." Confirmed by direct count before writing any code: Hashimoto's own "safe" list was 18 of 300 recipes, every single one a fermented drink (yogurt, kombucha, kefir, kvass, and so on), zero actual meals.

**Root cause.** The feature's original build (shipped a few hours earlier the same day) used `safeForConditions` as a hard include/exclude gate: a recipe was shown only if ZERO of its ingredients tripped ANY flag relevant to that condition. Combined with a wide criteria net (Hashimoto's owns 25 real relevant sub-criteria, the most of any condition), more ingredients meant more chances to trip one single flag somewhere, so only near-single-ingredient recipes could ever pass. This also directly contradicted this app's own standing healing-stage rule, already established for individual foods (`lib/foodStageReordering.ts`): "advisory and reordering only, never gating." A direct count across the other 18 conditions confirmed the same underlying defect at different severities: Psoriasis/Rheumatoid Arthritis (81/300), Cardiovascular Disease (84/300), Lupus/MS/Sjögren's (107/300 each) were all meaningfully thin; 10 of the 19 conditions already sat near 300/300 and weren't practically affected.

**The fix.** `safeForConditions` (a recipe with zero relevant flags) is kept, but no longer excludes anything -- `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js` now also computes a new `conditionCautions: {conditionCode: string}` per recipe: for every condition where a recipe IS flagged, a real, computed one-sentence caution naming the single most severe flagged ingredient (red before yellow) and what the flag means, built from the same tier vocabulary `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts` already uses everywhere else in this app (e.g. "Whole-Grain Wheat Flour: rated High Risk for gluten. A significant, well-documented concern."). A first version made the flagged ingredient's own name the grammatical subject of a verb and broke on subject-verb agreement for any plural ingredient ("Flax seeds skews..."); fixed by restructuring to a colon-plus-explanation form that needs no agreement at all.

"Meals You Can Eat" now shows every recipe with real per-condition data for that condition, genuinely clean ones first, flagged ones after, each carrying its own specific caution once opened from that condition's own page -- never excluded, matching the app's own advisory-only rule instead of contradicting it. New `RecipeCard.conditionCautions?: Record<string, string>` (`lib/digest/types.ts`). A new, deliberately separate `scripts/apply_recipe_condition_cautions.js` writes it into `recipes.ts` (kept apart from the original `apply_recipe_condition_data.js`, whose own `conditionNotes`-appending logic is already known not to be idempotent, per the Migraine entry below -- this new script only ever fully overwrites its own field, verified idempotent by running it three times and confirming zero diff after the first application). The caution itself is threaded through as a new `activeConditionCode` prop (`BasicHealthShelves` -> `DigestCard` -> `RecipeCardDetail`), rendered in its own distinct "A note for this condition" box, deliberately kept separate from the always-visible "Worth knowing if you have..." box (`conditionNotes`) rather than folded into it -- stuffing a mechanically generated caution for all 19 conditions into that box on every recipe would have buried the real, hand-written notes already there.

**Same-day direct follow-up: "Now factor in their declared healing stage too."** Closes the named gap above. For the 6 conditions with a real healing-stage model, "Meals You Can Eat" now uses the person's own Profile-declared stage two ways: within the flagged/cautioned group, a recipe with a real stage-specific advisory hit for their CURRENT stage now sorts behind ones that don't (so "worth a look right now" leads over "a concern for later"), and the recipe's own detail view prefers the richer, stage-specific note (already computed back when the feature first shipped, `stageAdvisoryNotes`) over the plain per-condition caution when one exists for that exact stage. No declared stage, or a condition with no real staging model, leaves everything exactly as the same-day fix above already left it.

**A real, higher-impact bug found and fixed along the way, not assumed correct from a passing type-check:** the stage labels the original build hand-typed into its own `STAGED_CONDITIONS` list (`compute_recipe_condition_data.js`) had drifted from the real, canonical stage labels Profile's own stage picker actually shows (`lib/conditionStages.ts` and each condition's own `*_STAGE_INFO`/`*_PHASE_INFO`): Hashimoto's "Digging (Removing Triggers)"/"Gut Repair (Reintroduction)" should have been "Stage 2: Digging"/"Stage 3: Gut Repair", Gout's "Acute Flare"/"Intercritical (Between Flares)" should have carried the same "Stage N:" prefix, and IBS's "Elimination Phase" should have been "Phase 1: Elimination." Caught only because building the new stage-aware lookup against the REAL canonical labels (not the drifted ones) surfaced a silent zero-match. This wasn't just an internal lookup problem: the mismatched label text was already being shown directly to users in every recipe's own "Worth knowing if you have..." box since the feature first shipped a few hours earlier the same day, so this fix also corrects real, already-visible copy, not only the new sort/caution logic. Fixed at the source (the compute script's own labels, so any future regeneration stays correct) and, separately, with a precise, verified string replacement directly against the already-applied text in `recipes.ts` (350 lines changed, exactly matching the combined count of the two affected labels, confirmed via `git diff --stat` before trusting it) -- deliberately not by re-running the original `apply_recipe_condition_data.js`, whose own `conditionNotes`-appending logic is already known not to be idempotent. Gout's own mismatch had zero practical impact to fix in `recipes.ts` itself: this app's own curated recipes never trip Gout's narrow Additives/Processing-only stage trigger at all (0 real stage notes generated for Gout, confirmed directly), so there was nothing already written to correct, only the script's own labels for going forward.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file (the two new script files carry the same pre-existing `__dirname`/`no-undef` pattern every other `scripts/*.js` file already has, and one pre-existing unused-variable warning in `compute_recipe_condition_data.js`, confirmed via `git show` to predate this session's own edits, both left alone per standing precedent). `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump needed. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.27. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): diet preference moved into Profile, and wired to default both places it already mattered.** Direct request: "the type of diet a person is trying to follow or is interested in trying should be in the Profile." New "Diet Preferences" card, same conditionGrid/conditionPill layout Conditions & Check-In already established, listing the same `RecipeDietTag` vocabulary already computed per-recipe (Vegan, Vegetarian, Omnivore, Plant-Based/Flexitarian, Mediterranean, Gluten-Free, Dairy-Free, Paleo, AIP, High-Protein), so a person's own stated preference lines up against real, existing data rather than a second, disconnected list. Backed by a new `diet_preferences` table (`lib/db.ts`), same shape as `curious_about_conditions`. A new shared `RECIPE_DIET_TAGS` export (`lib/digest/types.ts`) is the one ordered source of truth for the vocabulary now; Recipes' own "Filter by diet" picker (`purple-digest.tsx`) was refactored to derive its options from it instead of a second hand-maintained copy.

Wired into both places a diet preference already had a UI home: Recipes' "Filter by diet" picker now defaults to the person's own single stated preference on a fresh arrival at the Recipes lens (left at "All Diets" when none or more than one is set, since picking one of several to lead with would be a guess, not a real default); Schedule's 6-Week Meal Plan "Diet track" picker now defaults to Vegan or Vegetarian the same way (Vegan winning if both are set, matching `RecipeDietTag`'s own "vegan implies vegetarian-safe" convention), guarded by a plain ref so a later background refetch can never silently overwrite a manual pick made earlier in the same visit. Both stay fully overridable in the moment; this only changes the starting point.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file (a handful of pre-existing, unrelated unescaped-apostrophe/quote errors in `profile.tsx`/`schedule.tsx`, confirmed via `git diff` to sit outside every line this batch touched, left alone per this project's own standing precedent for that exact file). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.25; no Digest content changed, so no `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bump. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): a corpus-wide sweep for genuinely truncated, unfinished sentences across the Digest, 6 real instances found and fixed.** Direct report, quoting the exact broken text: "The research elsewhere is almost entirely about whether a given food helps or hurts a specific condition, assuming the food itself is." -- a sentence that just stops mid-thought. A scripted sweep of every `summary`/`teaser`/`note`/`relevanceNote`/`stageNote`/`mechanism`/`problem` field across all 61 `lib/digest/*.ts` files found 5 more genuine cutoffs beyond the one reported (`choosingQualityProducts.ts`, `foodAdditives.ts`, `problemFoods.ts`, `selfAdvocacy.ts`, `lifestyleEnvironment.ts`, one inside `foodIndustryHistory.ts`'s own AI-opinion-synthesis entry). Each fixed by reading the full entry to reconstruct what the sentence was actually building toward, not deleted or patched with a placeholder.

**Same-day direct correction to the sweep above: the "grammatically legal" bar the first pass used was itself wrong.** The first pass's own report used "the mechanism celiac disease was the original research population for." as an example of a sentence it correctly left alone, grammatically complete via preposition-stranding. Direct pushback, quoting that exact sentence back: "is not a complete sentence. For me it has no beginning or end... if it doesn't make sense when they read it, then there is no point to writing the app at all." Correct: formal grammatical legality was never the actual bar. The real bar is whether an average reader parses a sentence cleanly on one pass without mentally rearranging it, and that sentence buries its own antecedent a full clause before the stranded "for," forcing exactly that kind of backtracking even though a strict parse defends it.

Re-reading the same candidate list under the corrected standard found 10 more real problems the first pass's grammar-only check had waved through, across 8 files (`celiac.ts`, `bodyFatBiology.ts`, `bodySystems.ts`, `essentialNutrients.ts`, `gutMicrobiome.ts`, `ibs.ts`, `cardiovascularDisease.ts`, `foodIndustryHistory.ts`): the reported celiac sentence itself, rewritten to state its own antecedent before the reference to it rather than stranding a preposition behind a whole intervening clause; two more of the same long-distance-stranding shape (`bodyFatBiology.ts`, `bodySystems.ts`); one elided verb that read as incomplete ("...who actually needs to." fixed to name what: "...needs to supplement in the first place"); one ellipsis needing a real predicate ("...the connection is." to "...the connection holds up"); and, caught in the same close-reading pass though not part of the original report, a garbled word ("cefor instance" to "cereal, for instance"), two "a/an" agreement errors ("A honest complication" to "An honest"), one subject-verb agreement error ("practices there is" to "there are"), and one stray comma splitting an adjective from its noun. The same sweep, run a second time across `app/**/*.tsx` and `components/*.tsx` as directly requested ("Run the same sweep across the rest of the app's screen text"), came back genuinely clean: every flagged candidate was confirmed on full reading to be a natural, complete sentence, or a scanner artifact from JSX text split across sibling `{...}` expressions, not real broken text. A real scanner bug was caught and fixed along the way: the first script's naive quote-matching was snagging on apostrophes inside `//` comments and consuming to the next stray quote mark, producing 73 garbage hits; a corrected version that strips comments while respecting real string-literal boundaries brought that down to 10 candidates, all confirmed legitimate. Standing writing-style memory updated with this corrected standard (`feedback_writing_style.md`). `tsc`/`eslint` clean across all 8 touched files. **Not yet confirmed on-device** (pure text changes, same low visual risk as the first pass).

A real, hard-won detection lesson: a naive "flag any sentence ending on a word that can't end a sentence" heuristic produced 783 false-positive-dominated hits on the first pass, since this app's own prose style leans heavily on legitimate preposition-stranding relative clauses ("the mechanism celiac disease was the original research population for.") and elliptical short sentences completing via the prior one ("Sometimes it is."). The one signal that actually held up: a sentence ending on a bare linking verb (is/was/are/were) or article/possessive determiner with literally nothing after it -- no legitimate stranding explanation exists for those, unlike a preposition. A second check confirmed zero fields anywhere in the corpus are missing terminal punctuation entirely. Scope named honestly: this sweep covered `lib/digest/*.ts` specifically, not the rest of the app's UI copy.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped, `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.23; no database changes. The standing writing-style memory updated with this as a sixth distinct tic, including the detection-heuristic lesson for any future sweep. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): the Migraine gap closed -- all 19 tracked conditions now have real "Meals You Can Eat" coverage.** Direct follow-up: "Yes, build that migraine relevance mapping," approving the concrete next step named at the end of the AIP/migraine research shipped a few hours earlier the same day.

New `scripts/add_migraine_condition_relevance.js` adds two real rows to the live database's own `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` table, reusing the already-owned Additives and Processing sub-criteria (the same two conditions like IBD/Gout/CVD already reuse under their own condition-specific framing, an established real pattern, not a new one). Grounded directly in the same day's own migraine research, not re-researched: Additives cites the 2025 elimination-diet RCT (PMID 41473187, measured drops in IL-6/TNF-alpha/CGRP), Processing cites the 2024 gut-to-brain mechanism review (PMID 39064664, intestinal permeability reaching the trigeminovascular system). Deliberately scoped narrow rather than forced: the histamine/DAO mechanism also researched the same day was NOT mapped to any existing sub-criterion, since none of this database's real sub-criteria actually measure histamine content (`Fermentability` is a real but unrelated FODMAP measure; `Microbiome Effects` scores fermented foods positively for prebiotic value, the opposite direction a histamine caution would need) -- forcing either would misapply a criterion built for a different purpose, against this app's own standing "don't force a citation that doesn't really support the claim" discipline.

**A real bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after:** re-running `apply_recipe_condition_data.js` (the same script that originally wrote `safeForConditions` into every recipe) against the already-populated `recipes.ts` turned out not to be idempotent -- it blindly inserts a fresh line and re-appends stage notes every time rather than updating in place, producing genuine duplicates on a second run (caught immediately via a direct line-count check, not assumed clean). Reverted and fixed with a new, narrower, genuinely idempotent script (`scripts/add_migraine_to_recipe_condition_lists.js`) that parses each recipe's own existing `safeForConditions` array and adds `'migraine'` only if truly missing, re-sorting rather than re-inserting -- verified idempotent by running it twice and confirming the second run changed nothing.

Migraine's own real result, computed the same way every other condition already was: all 300 recipes, since this app's whole-food-only curated-recipe corpus never actually trips an Additives/Processing "High Risk" flag at all (the same honest, structural outcome already accepted for CKD/Fatty Liver/Gout/PCOS/Type 1/Type 2 Diabetes, whose own narrow criteria sets show the identical trivial 300-of-300 result) -- not a sign the mapping is wrong, a real reflection of this app's own already-whole-food recipe library. Every stale "18 of 19 conditions" / "Migraine excluded" comment across `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js`, `lib/digest/types.ts`, and `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx` updated to match.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched file. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped (the live database itself changed for the first time this session), `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped, `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.22. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): AIP diet and migraine researched, 2 new Digest entries.** Direct request: "I think migraines probably could be related to the AIP diet in reduction of inflamation as it would pertain to the brain. Research that and any study about that which could be used in this app and provide real solid data." The honest finding, stated directly rather than glossed over: no published trial has tested the Autoimmune Protocol itself on migraine, a real, named gap in the current literature (which explicitly lists migraine as a future AIP research direction, not a settled one).

What real evidence does exist, covered in two new entries in `lib/digest/migraine.ts`: (1) a 2025 randomized, sham-controlled trial (98 adults, PMID 41473187) testing the same underlying mechanism AIP is built on, food elimination to calm systemic inflammation, found statistically significant drops in IL-6, TNF-alpha, and CGRP itself (the neuropeptide behind an actual migraine attack, already covered in this category's own trigeminovascular entry), plus fewer migraine days and lower MIDAS severity scores; a separate 2024 review (PMID 39064664) names the real pathway connecting gut-level food elimination to brain-level effect, increased intestinal permeability letting inflammatory molecules into systemic circulation, where they directly stimulate the trigeminovascular system. (2) A distinct, real mechanism AIP's own elimination list also touches without being designed around it: 87% of migraine patients show measurable DAO enzyme deficiency versus 44% of controls (PMID 28624934, p<0.0001), impairing the body's ability to break down dietary histamine, one of the foods AIP eliminates (fermented foods, aged cheese, cured meats) being independently histamine-rich; a separate randomized trial of DAO enzyme supplementation (PMID 30017233) found a real reduction in headache burden, confirming the pathway is causal, not just correlational.

Both entries tiered `moderate` (real, controlled-trial-level evidence for the underlying mechanisms, not direct trial evidence for AIP itself, an honest distinction named directly in both entries' own text). Cross-linked to the existing `diet-aip` Basic Health entry, `migraine-trigeminovascular-cgrp-mechanism`, `migraine-food-triggers-honest-nuance`, and `migraine-fermented-drinks`, each of those updated with a reverse link too. A real writing-style self-check caught and fixed 6 instances of overused "real"/"honest" filler and 3 instances of the redundant "X's own Y" pattern in the first draft before shipping, confirmed clean by direct grep, not assumed from a passing `tsc`.

**A real, concrete follow-up this research opens up, named directly rather than acted on unprompted:** Migraine currently has zero real condition-scoped food-scoring data in the reference database, which is why it was excluded from the "Meals You Can Eat" feature shipped earlier the same day. The Additives/Processing sub-criteria already exist and are already reused by other conditions via `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` -- a real, defensible relevance mapping for Migraine (Additives/Processing, matching the histamine/inflammatory-trigger mechanism just researched) could extend that same feature to a 19th condition. Not built in this pass; a real, scoped next step if wanted.

`tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on both touched files. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.21, `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped; no database changes. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): recipes real-linked to conditions, and to healing stage where a real stage model exists, via a new "Meals You Can Eat" topic on 18 of the 19 condition pages.** Direct request: "there needs to be an association between the recipes and the conditions somehow, so that the user can look through their specific condition that will then show them meals they can eat, depending on the stage of their conditions." Scoped via two direct clarifying questions: real safety-based association for all 19 conditions (staged where a real stage model already exists), surfaced as a new topic inside each condition's own Digest page.

**The computation.** New `scripts/compute_recipe_condition_data.js` reuses this app's own already-built, already-verified scoring machinery, faithfully replicated against the live database rather than re-derived: the exact same `getFoodScoresForCondition`/`getFoodScores` queries (`lib/db.ts`) and the same tier-classification rules (`lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts`), plus a faithful port of all 6 real per-condition stage-advisory files (`lib/healingStageAdvisory.ts`, `lib/ibsPhaseAdvisory.ts`, `lib/celiacStageAdvisory.ts`, `lib/ibdStageAdvisory.ts`, `lib/ckdStageAdvisory.ts`, `lib/goutStageAdvisory.ts`). Every recipe's own ingredients are resolved to real `(food_id, source)` rows the same way `getCuratedRecipe` already does, then scored.

Two real, distinct outputs per recipe: `recipeCard.safeForConditions` (condition codes with zero significant flagged ingredients, computed for 18 of the 19 tracked conditions -- Migraine has zero real condition-specific scoring data in this database, confirmed by direct query, so it's honestly excluded rather than trivially marked safe for everything), and real, computed stage-advisory entries appended to each recipe's own existing `conditionNotes` array (never replacing the hand-written ones already there) for the 6 conditions with a real staging model.

**Two real bugs caught and fixed before shipping.** First: "Selenium & Zn synergy" and "Iron Presence" tiers hit roughly 50% and 5.5% of all 22,022 foods respectively, a known near-universal background signal this app's own recipes.ts header comment already documents excluding from hand-written cautions -- excluded here the same way. Second, more structural: a first run using this app's own standard yellow-or-red flagged-tier threshold returned only 18 of 300 recipes safe for Hashimoto's specifically, a real artifact of Hashimoto's owning 25 real sub-criteria against 1-7 for most other conditions (far more chances to trip one moderate flag somewhere across a whole recipe, not a real difference in food quality). A red-only threshold was tried and rejected in turn: it fixed Hashimoto's number but made 13 of the other 17 covered conditions trivially show all 300 recipes as "safe" (their own narrow 1-2 relevant sub-criteria never reach red tier anywhere in this database). Kept yellow-or-red, matching this app's own standard `isFlaggedTier` semantics everywhere else: Hashimoto's own smaller, more selective list is the honest consequence of checking a wider, more rigorous net of real criteria, not a number to inflate.

A third issue caught by direct inspection of the generated output, not assumed clean from a passing `tsc`: the first draft's own generated text used a curly apostrophe, an em dash, and (copied verbatim from the real source advisory files) the banned " -- " double-hyphen pattern and filler "real," all violating this app's own standing writing-style rules. Fixed in the generator itself; the source advisory files still carry these same violations, a real, separate cleanup this pass found but didn't fix, named directly rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover.

**The UI.** A new synthetic "Meals You Can Eat" topic, built directly from `RecipeCard.safeForConditions` and inserted into `groupConditionEntries`' own topic list (right after the reordering, now leading every condition's own topic order where it exists) -- reuses the exact same `DigestCard`/`RecipeCardDetail` rendering every Recipes-category entry already uses, not a new component. New `DIGEST_KEY_TO_CONDITION_CODE` (`lib/conditionCodeMap.ts`) bridges a condition's own camelCase Digest key back to the real snake_case condition code `safeForConditions` stores, derived from the existing reverse map rather than hand-duplicated. A real perf trap caught before shipping: `getEntriesForCategory('recipes')` does a full filter over the entire Digest corpus, and calling it fresh inside `groupConditionEntries` would have re-run on every render of a condition's own page (the exact class of bug already found and fixed for `basicHealthAllGroups`/`categorySearchGroups` on 2026-08-23) -- fixed with a module-level cache, the same "compute once per app session" pattern `lib/db.ts`'s own `getSafeFoodIds` already established. A second real bug caught before shipping: tapping a recipe card inside this new topic to expand it would have tried to scroll to the wrong shelf, since `classifyConditionTopic` (used by the tap-to-scroll mechanism) had no way to recognize a Recipes-category entry showing up on a condition's own page -- fixed with a direct `entry.category === 'recipes'` check at the top of that classifier.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file. `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.20, `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump needed. **Not yet confirmed on-device.** Named, not started: category-scoped search on a condition's own page doesn't index its "Meals You Can Eat" recipes (a separate, deeper change to the search pool, out of this pass's own scope); the pre-existing " -- "/"real" filler violations in the 6 stage-advisory source files themselves.

**Most recent (2026-08-24): a real, app-wide button design system, plus two Glossary/Recipes bugs fixed in the same request.** Direct report: "there needs to be some sort of continuity between app buttons, or buttons that follow the color of the ground color chosen in the Profile. They should look like buttons, not like pills. They should have some depth, and not be an annoyance being there but definitely noticable," scoped via two direct clarifying questions to every button in the whole app, colored from Profile's own 5-option Ground Theme rather than each tab's own identity color.

New `GroundFamily.buttonColor` (each ground theme's own `background` lightened by a fixed 0.55 fraction, `lighten()`'s existing remaining-headroom mix) plus `colors.buttonColor`/`colors.textOnButton` in `constants/colors.ts`, both real, WCAG-verified: `textOnButton` (`#0F2E2B`, deliberately its own token rather than reusing `textOnPrimary` directly, so a future retune of one can't silently drag the other) clears 4.5:1 against every one of the 5 themes' own `buttonColor` (4.72:1 Burgundy, the tightest, to 6.68:1 Teal), and each `buttonColor` still reads distinctly against its own ground `background` (4.41-5.03:1). `keySurface` was tried first and rejected outright: only 3.75-4.92:1 against `textOnPrimary`, failing Purple and Burgundy. New shared `BUTTON_SHADOW` export (`shadowOpacity: 0.3`, `shadowRadius: 4`, `elevation: 4`) is the one real depth recipe every migrated button now carries; nothing had a shadow of its own before this.

Deliberately a new, additional token, not a repurposing of `colors.primary`: that color already carries checkbox/pill/link duty well beyond buttons (documented in its own comment), and changing its value would have rippled into all of that when only buttons were reported.

**Migrated: `components/PopoverSelect.tsx`'s own closed field** (the single shared trigger every picker across the whole app already reuses — the highest-leverage fix in this batch, one file cascading everywhere), all 13 Food-builder-family files (`SideBuilder`, `SaladBuilder`, `SoupBuilder`, `SaucesBuilder`, `SnackBuilder`, `SmoothieBuilder`, `HandheldsBuilder`, `BakedGoodsBuilder`, `BeverageBuilder`, `DessertBuilder`, `FermentationBuilder`, `MealBuilder`, `StepsEditor`), and 11 more app screens (`purple-digest.tsx`'s own `backToHomeText` back-link, `connect.tsx`, `connections.tsx`, `fermentation-tracker.tsx`, `food-product-detail.tsx`, `import-shared.tsx`, `scan-product.tsx`, `garden.tsx`, `index.tsx`/Home, `insights.tsx`, `log.tsx`, `schedule.tsx`) — every file in the codebase that defines its own `primaryButton` style, confirmed by a full-project grep before considering this batch done. Each screen previously drew its button fill from a different, inconsistent source (`tabColor`, `colors.primary`, `colors.accent`, or a translucent `inputBackground(tabColor)` tint) with no shared color or shadow at all; all now share one `colors.buttonColor`/`colors.textOnButton`/`BUTTON_SHADOW` recipe.

**Deliberately left alone, not missed:** real toggle/selection chips (a condition picker's pills, `FermentationBuilder`'s own strain multi-select, Basic-Health-style mode tabs) stay on their own tab/context color — a different, legitimate UI pattern (pick-one-of-several) from a CTA button, not what was reported. `FermentationBuilder.tsx`'s own strain-pill line was caught mid-migration (a shared literal string match briefly swept it in too) and reverted by hand before shipping, confirmed by a direct post-edit grep, not assumed correct from the script's own success message.

**Two Digest bugs fixed in the same request, direct report:** "When I select Glossary while in the recipe area, the filter by diet should no longer be there. And again, the secondary smaller header that says Glossary should also not be there." The diet-filter row (shipped earlier the same day) was gated only on `lens === 'recipes'`, never checked whether Glossary was open on top of it; now also requires `!glossaryOpen`. Glossary's own shelf never passed `hideTopLevelLabel` to `BasicHealthShelves`, the exact same class of bug the "Sides" duplicate-header fix caught earlier the same day, just never applied to this one remaining case — fixed the same way, `hideTopLevelLabel="Glossary"`.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched file (a handful of pre-existing unescaped-apostrophe/quote errors in `index.tsx`/`schedule.tsx`, confirmed via `git diff` to sit outside every line this batch touched, left alone per this project's own standing precedent for that exact file). `APP_VERSION` bumped to 1.0.25.19, `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped. **Not yet confirmed on-device** — worth a real look given how much of "some depth... noticeable" is a visual judgment a type-checker can't verify.

**Named, not started this batch:** Profile's own buttons (`app/profile.tsx` has no shared `primaryButton` style at all, several one-off `backgroundColor: colors.primary` fills instead) and any modal/alert/action-sheet buttons elsewhere weren't part of the `primaryButton`-pattern survey this batch was scoped to. A real, tracked next step, not silently dropped.

**Earlier the same day (2026-08-24): every one of the 300 curated recipes tagged with real, computed diet compatibility, plus a real filter to browse by it, and two smaller UI-consistency fixes shipped in the same request.** Direct three-part request: "For all recipes, they need to be grouped so they can be identified at being omnivore, vegetarian, or vegan. Also... identified for if they fit into the mediterranean, or the AIP, or any of the other various diet plans... Putting it Together header isn't necessary in any place where you have it listed as just the header all by itself... Go through and check that all lines are 2 pixels thick."

**Diet tagging.** New `scripts/compute_recipe_diet_tags.js` computes a real `RecipeDietTag[]` for all 300 curated recipes directly from their own `curated_recipe_ingredients` rows in the live database, never eyeballed. Exactly one base tier (Vegan/Vegetarian/Omnivore, vegan implying vegetarian-safe so only the strictest applies) plus independent tags wherever a real, defensible rule matches: Plant-Based/Flexitarian (vegan/vegetarian, or omnivore with only poultry/fish/shellfish, no red meat), Mediterranean (olive oil present, no red meat), Gluten-Free (no wheat/rye/spelt/seitan/wheat-soy-sauce ingredient), Dairy-Free, Paleo (no grain/legume/dairy/refined sugar/commercial mayo, honey and maple syrup allowed as real Paleo-compatible sweeteners), AIP (Paleo's own rules plus no egg/nuts-seeds/nightshades), and High-Protein (meat, a real protein legume, egg, or a real high-protein dairy food). Keto deliberately left untagged: no reliable per-recipe carb-gram data exists to back a real 30g/day threshold (`scripts/compute_recipe_data.js` only ever covered the original 47 recipes, not all 300), named honestly rather than inventing a number, matching this app's own standing evidence-honesty rule.

One real classification bug caught and fixed before shipping: wheat flour sits under this database's own `PantryStaples` category, not `Grain`, so the first pass of the Paleo/AIP rule missed it entirely and mistagged the whole-wheat bread recipe as Paleo/AIP-compliant. Fixed by reusing the already-built `GLUTEN_INGREDIENTS` set (which already names every real grain-derived item across every category it actually appears in) inside the Paleo check too, re-verified directly against the bread recipe's own tags after the fix, not just trusted from the rule reading correct on paper. `scripts/apply_recipe_diet_tags.js` writes the computed tags into `lib/digest/recipes.ts` as each entry's own `recipeCard.dietTags`; new `RecipeDietTag`/`dietTags?: RecipeDietTag[]` added to `RecipeCard` in `lib/digest/types.ts`.

**UI.** A new `RecipeDietTagRow` badges every recipe's own tags at the top of its detail view (`app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`). A new "Filter by diet" `PopoverSelect` on the Recipes lens is the real grouping mechanism the request asked for: it narrows the screen's own `entries` value directly, so the topic menu, a drilled-in shelf (Sides, Salads, and so on), and category-scoped search results all narrow together automatically, no separate wiring needed in any of them.

**The other two parts of the same request.** The standalone "Putting It Together" heading that used to sit alone above every category's own closing synthesis card is removed outright (the card's own title, "...Pulled Together," "Putting It Together: ...", already says what it is). A precise, brace-depth-scoped audit (not a loose text-proximity search, which produced false positives on a first attempt) found every real remaining `borderWidth: 1` on a card whose border color is set to a tab-identity color, the standing 2px convention this project settled on 2026-08-23: Digest's own `headerCard`, plus `recipeNutritionBox`/`recipeConditionBox` across all 11 Food builders. Fixed everywhere found; genuinely distinct, deliberately-thin controls (buttons/pills/chips/inputs, Schedule's own intentionally dashed-border `myMedsResearchCard`, and unrelated special-purpose elements like camera scan frames and slider thumbs) were checked and correctly left alone.

`tsc` clean project-wide (`app-example/`'s own pre-existing, unrelated template errors aside), `eslint` clean on every touched app file (the two new one-off scripts carry the same pre-existing `__dirname`/`no-undef` pattern every other `scripts/*.js` file in this project already has, not a new problem). `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped; no database changes, so no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump needed. `APP_VERSION` stays at 1.0.25.16, already bumped once for this same three-part request when the header/border-width fixes shipped earlier the same day. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Most recent (2026-08-24): chrononutrition research content shipped, phase 3 of 3 of the same larger request (chrononutrition reorg → diet tracks → research content), all three phases now complete.** Direct follow-up, after the vegan/vegetarian tracks below shipped: "Keep going straight into the research content now."

**New `lib/digest/chrononutrition.ts`, a Basic Health topic with 9 entries**, covering the actual science: the master clock (SCN) versus peripheral clocks in the liver/gut/pancreas and why food, not light, synchronizes the peripheral ones; the diurnal insulin-sensitivity rhythm behind the "dawn phenomenon"; early time-restricted eating's real RCT evidence (Sutton 2018); an honest complication where a real T2D trial found glucose control improved but insulin sensitivity itself didn't; the NutriNet-Santé cohort linking late eating to cardiovascular risk; the gut microbiome's own diurnal rhythm (Thaiss 2014); shift work as a real-world natural experiment in circadian disruption. Two claims got a direct, honest correction rather than being repeated as settled fact, per the source conversation's own framing: the popular claim that eating late "blocks autophagy" turns out genuinely mixed in human research (a 2024 study found no acute change from one meal, a 2025 study found a longer-term intervention may increase autophagic flux, not the same thing); and the specific "cortisol awakening response" mechanism has a real, recent complication (newer research found no evidence of an increased rate of cortisol secretion specifically in the hour after waking), separate from the broader morning-peak cortisol rhythm, which remains solid.

**19 new condition-specific entries, one per tracked condition**, each independently researched and cited, not template-filled: Hashimoto's and Graves' (circadian clock gene disruption in autoimmune thyroiditis, BMAL1/PER2 reduced in affected thyroid tissue); Rheumatoid Arthritis (the CAPRA-1 trial, real chronotherapy, timing a prednisone dose to the overnight IL-6 peak measurably shortened morning stiffness); Type 1 Diabetes (the dawn phenomenon's real clinical magnitude, 58-66% higher insulin need); Type 2 Diabetes (a genuine TRE nuance, cross-linked to an existing entry rather than duplicated, see below); Celiac and IBD (clock gene disruption in inflamed gut tissue, an active, not-yet-settled research direction); Multiple Sclerosis and Lupus and Sjögren's (melatonin/BMAL1/salivary-gland clock genes, each a real but still-mechanistic finding, tiered accordingly); PCOS (a real 6-month RCT where time-restricted eating lowered a marker of excess androgen that calorie restriction alone didn't); Chronic Kidney Disease (nocturnal blood-pressure non-dipping, a real circadian-biology finding distinct from meal timing, named honestly as such); Fatty Liver Disease (the liver's own clock and why irregular eating tracks with MASLD risk); IBS (night shift work linked to a real jump in IBS-like symptoms); Migraine (a striking real finding, 110 of 168 migraine susceptibility genes are circadian clock genes); Cardiovascular Disease (the same NutriNet-Santé cohort, condition-specific framing); Gout (the real mechanism behind gout's well-known nocturnal flare pattern, NLRP3 inflammasome circadian control); Prostate Health (nocturnal polyuria's real vasopressin/melatonin mechanism); Psoriasis (skin's own circadian clock and the F3 gene's role in IL-17 signaling).

**A real duplicate caught and fixed before shipping, not after:** a first draft of the Type 2 Diabetes entry collided, both by id and by content, with an existing entry (`type2-time-restricted-eating`) already covering the same 8-RCT meta-analysis ground more thoroughly. Caught by a full-corpus duplicate-id check, not by chance -- the new entry was deleted outright rather than kept alongside a near-duplicate, and the existing entry was cross-linked to the new Basic Health entries instead. A second near-miss: the new circadian-biology entry's first draft substantially overlapped an existing Sleep & Health entry on the same master-clock/light mechanism; rewritten to focus specifically on the non-overlapping angle (food as the peripheral-clock zeitgeber) and cross-linked both directions rather than left duplicative.

Every citation individually verified via WebSearch, matching this app's own standing discipline. Every one of the 20 new entries' `relatedIds` cross-checked to resolve, and a full-corpus sweep confirmed all ~1,031 `relatedIds` references across every existing Digest entry still resolve too, not just the new ones.
