**Also 2026-08-23: a spacing report on the Digest's own fixed search subheader, applying to every category's search bar at once since they all share one implementation.** Direct report: "There is a shadowy bar that extends under the search field in the Digest areas and it needs to move closer to the search field by about 10 pixels, and the bottom edge of that subheader needs to move up to the bottom edge of the shadowy bar, and then the page can scroll under it." Two edits in `purple-digest.tsx`, both to the one shared `fixedHeader`/`DigestSearchInput`/`EdgeShadow` structure every category's own search bar already reuses (there's only one implementation, not one per category): `searchInput`'s own `marginBottom` (14 to 4) closes the gap between the field and the `EdgeShadow` bar below it by the requested 10px; `fixedHeader`'s own `paddingBottom` (4 to 0) removes the plain, un-shaded strip that used to sit below the shadow, so the subheader's own bottom edge now lands exactly at the shadow's own bottom edge and scrolled content starts appearing right where the shadow itself ends. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, one file touched. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: Food's own resting-screen "Desktop," a new product direction, piloted on one tab first.** Direct idea: "I think I know how I want to use each tab's screen before any lens is selected. It will be an area where their things that they create through their use of that Tab's lenses. They are, in a way, several Desktops. This would include their Favorites... accessed by using a menu system just like the Digest uses." Confirmed via three quick scoping questions before building: Food only as a pilot (not all seven non-Home, non-Digest tabs at once); the Desktop layers on top of the existing shared photo background rather than replacing or hiding it; the menu is one entry per builder plus Favorites, matching Digest's own topic-menu shape exactly.

Turned out to need far less new plumbing than expected: Food already had this exact data fully computed for a small, easy-to-miss corner-icon popup (`MyItemsHub`, "My Foods" and its own "Saved & Favorites" submenu, both built 2026-08-01/17), so this is a presentation change built on already-working data, not a new feature from scratch. `GatedTabContent.tsx` gained an optional `restingContent` prop (harmless everywhere else, `undefined` on the other 6 screens using it), rendered in the same branch that used to show nothing at all before a lens is picked. New `components/TabDesktopMenu.tsx` is Digest's own `DigestTopicMenu` visual pattern (colors.surface card, tab-colored border, label plus count) pulled out into a reusable component rather than copied a second time, so any tab building its own Desktop later reads the same shape. Food's own Desktop drills into "Saved & Favorites" in place (Digest's own tap-to-drill-in, breadcrumb-back pattern) rather than opening the old popup's Modal, reusing the exact same `myFoodsCategories`/`savedAndFavoritesCategories` arrays via one `.map()` so the other three top-level rows (My Food Products, My Whole Foods, System Meals) can never drift out of sync between the popup and the new Desktop. Counts refresh via `useFocusEffect` (covers both the first mount and returning from elsewhere), and the Desktop always resets to its own top level once a lens is actually picked, so returning to rest never leaves someone stranded mid-drill-down.

The original `MyItemsHub` corner-icon popup is deliberately left untouched, not merged or removed: it still works as a quick "My Foods" shortcut reachable even from inside an open builder, something the Desktop itself can't do (it only shows at rest). An overlap worth naming, not yet resolved: Digest already has its own "My Kitchen" and "My Favorites" lenses covering closely related ground (the same saved/favorited builder records, shown as richer Digest-style cards with schedule/share actions, reached from the Digest tab rather than Food's own). This Desktop deliberately reuses Food's own plainer `food-items.tsx` list path (matching what the popup already does) rather than routing into that richer Digest experience, the lower-risk, already-proven choice, not a considered decision that Digest's own version should stay separate long-term. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, four files touched (`components/TabDesktopMenu.tsx` new, `components/GatedTabContent.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, plus this document). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: a version numbering system, direct request.** "I would like to have a way to be sure I am looking at the correct version that definitely includes the latest updates." New `constants/version.ts` exports a single `APP_VERSION` string, `1.0.X` format (still pre-1.0-launch in scope, so major/minor stay pinned at `1.0`), kept in sync by hand with `app.json`/`package.json`'s own `version` field at every bump going forward. Starting value **1.0.24**, not arbitrary: the count of distinct calendar days with committed work between this app's first commit (2026-07-25) and this feature's own build date, a checkable number rather than a guess (the raw git commit count, 680, is mostly fine-grained auto-checkpoints and would have read as noise, not a version history). New `components/VersionLabel.tsx`, a small (`fontSize: 9`), muted, always-on-screen `v1.0.24` label, mounted once globally in `app/_layout.tsx` alongside `AppKeyboard` so it shows on every screen regardless of which tab or stack screen is active, not just ones with a hub button of their own. Positioned at the hub-button cluster's own true bottom-left corner (`SECONDARY_HUB_CARD_LEFT_MARGIN`, the same left inset LensHub/ScopeHub/MyItemsHub already use) and just under the buttons' own bottom edge (reads `FLOATING_BUTTON_BOTTOM_OFFSET` directly, the same shared value every hub button's own position already uses, so it can't drift out of sync with a future adjustment to that offset). Carries the same `textShadow` legibility treatment every other small label in this app now uses against a photo background, the same lesson from this same day's own header-legibility saga. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, three files touched (`constants/version.ts` new, `components/VersionLabel.tsx` new, `app/_layout.tsx` one import plus one mount line). **Confirmed on-device same day.**

**Same-day follow-up, direct request:** confirmed working and liked on-device, with one addition: "it should also then have versions from this day, so another .number should be added... there will be many updates throughout a day." Version format is now `1.0.DAY.UPDATE`, current value **1.0.24.1**: `DAY` (24) stays the count-of-distinct-days value from the original build, incrementing by exactly 1 the first time a version bump happens on a new calendar day; `UPDATE` resets to 1 whenever `DAY` increments and increments by 1 for every further checkpoint shipped that same day. `app.json`/`package.json` updated to match. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, three files touched (`constants/version.ts`, `app.json`, `package.json`), no component changes needed since `VersionLabel.tsx` already reads `APP_VERSION` directly. **Confirmed on-device**: "Checked it on my phone, it shows correctly."

**Same-day second follow-up, two direct requests.** (1) "Move the app version down by about 10 pixels": `VersionLabel.tsx` gained a named `MOVE_DOWN_ADJUSTMENT` constant, kept separate from the existing `GAP_BELOW_BUTTON` so this specific ask stays legible in the math rather than silently redefining what that constant means. (2) The `UPDATE` segment's own increment rule, refined and now standing (added to this document's own Standing rules section, and to `constants/version.ts`'s own comment): increments once per distinct request the user provides that day, not once per file changed or checkpoint shipped, "there may be many changes that happen at once, but each time I provide something for you to do or to change, there needs to be an increment for that day." This request is itself the first one bumped under the new rule: **1.0.24.2**. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, four files touched (`components/VersionLabel.tsx`, `constants/version.ts`, `app.json`, `package.json`) plus this document. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: direct correction to both halves of the prior entry, plus Basic Health's plain-browsing view rebuilt as a topic menu.** Two overcorrections from the header-legibility chip fix reverted: `cardTitle` and `shelfCardTitle` (individual entry titles inside an already-opaque topic card) never needed their own solid background chip, only `shelfCardSelected`'s own near-transparent override was the actual bug there, already fixed at its source; both reverted to shadow-only, their prior state. Separately, `shelfHeading` (Basic Health's group labels) was never meant to get a plain black overlay either: direct instruction was to match the same look every other card on this screen already uses, `colors.surface` fill with a `TAB_COLOR` border, not a one-off dark chip. Fixed to match. Bigger change: even after the FlatList virtualization fix, Basic Health's plain-browsing view still took too long, because mounting all ~21 groups' shelves at once (479 entries total) was itself the cost, not just how each shelf rendered internally. Direct instruction: move toward a menu-driven system for selecting a topic rather than showing everything at once, while keeping the existing shelf-row-plus-detail-panel display exactly as it already works once someone has drilled into a specific group. Basic Health's plain-browsing landing view (search is untouched, it already narrows results directly) now shows a plain tappable list of its ~21 topic names first (`BasicHealthTopicMenu`), and picking one renders that single group through the same, unmodified `BasicHealthShelves` component every other category still uses in full; a back link returns to the menu. `jumpToRelated` (Related-entry chips, cross-category links) now drills into the right Basic Health group automatically before scrolling, so cross-links into Basic Health still land correctly. No other category's own browsing view was touched. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Same-day follow-up:** Essential Nutrients' own 22 individual nutrient/hormone leaf groups (Magnesium, Vitamin D, and so on) were each showing as their own separate row in the new topic menu, direct request to fold them back under one shared "Essential Nutrients" row instead. New `basicHealthMenuGroups` (a `useMemo` over `basicHealthGroups`) collapses every leaf group's label to the part before its first `::` and sums entry counts under that one top-level key, matching `BASIC_HEALTH_TOPICS`' own original 2-level structure (only Essential Nutrients has subtopics; every other topic already resolves to one row, unchanged). Picking "Essential Nutrients" from the menu now renders all 22 of its own subtopic shelves together through the same, unmodified multi-shelf `BasicHealthShelves` view every condition category already uses, restoring the "look at them all together" browsing experience for that one cluster specifically rather than one nutrient at a time. `jumpToRelated`'s own Basic Health drill-in updated to match (drills into the top-level topic, not one leaf). `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Same-day second follow-up:** the topic menu is now alphabetized by display label (`basicHealthMenuGroups`' own sort), not `BASIC_HEALTH_TOPICS`' declared order, "More" sorting in place with everything else rather than pinned last; Essential Nutrients' own drilled-in subtopic shelves sort the same way. Separately, direct instruction to carry the same `colors.surface`/`TAB_COLOR`-border "topic header" card look (already on `shelfHeading`, so already consistent across every drilled-in sub-topic, Basic Health's own and every condition's) through to the screen's navigation links too: `backToHomeText`, shared by "‹ Back to Digest," "‹ Clear search," and Basic Health's own drill-in back link, now carries that same treatment (was plain colored text on purpose, a since-superseded choice). That drill-in link is also renamed "‹ Back to Basic Health" (was "‹ All Basic Health Topics"), matching "‹ Back to Digest"'s own naming pattern. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Same-day third follow-up:** drilling into a Basic Health subgroup still showed the generic "Basic Health" title and its own "Food, vitamins, minerals..." description above the shelves, direct report that this reads as if still on the topic-wide overview rather than one specific subgroup. That header now switches: on the top-level menu it's unchanged ("Basic Health" plus its description, exactly where "all of the groups listed within Basic Health are listed"), but once a subgroup is picked, the title becomes that subgroup's own name (`basicHealthDrilldownLabel`) with no description at all, no per-subgroup blurb invented, the shelves and their own entries carry that weight the same way every other category's topic shelves already do. Separately, each of Essential Nutrients' own 22 drilled-in shelves stopped repeating "Essential Nutrients ›" before every heading ("Essential Nutrients › Magnesium" simplified to plain "Magnesium") via a new `hideTopLevelLabel` prop on `BasicHealthShelves`, set only by Basic Health's own drilled-in view; search results and every condition category's own topic shelves are unaffected, they never pass it. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Same-day fourth follow-up, three more direct reports.** (1) The empty drilled-in header from the prior fix went too far the other way: direct report that Essential Nutrients' own header said nothing about what the subgroup covers or how it connects to basic health generally. `BasicHealthTopic` gained a required `description` field, one specific line per topic (23 of them, plus `BASIC_HEALTH_MORE_TOPIC_DESCRIPTION` for the dynamic "More" catch-all), shown in place of Basic Health's own shared blurb once a subgroup is picked, not a repeat of it. (2) A flat 1px bottom border on Digest's own fixed (non-scrolling) header strip, sitting roughly mid-screen over the photo background, read as "everything begins to slide behind" it while scrolling, direct report, the same class of bug the footer's own divider line was fixed for on 2026-08-21 but never applied here. Replaced with `EdgeShadow direction="down"`, the same soft shaded-edge treatment `ScreenHeader`'s own bottom edge already uses, not a new one invented. (3) `backToHomeText` ("‹ Back to Digest," "‹ Clear search," "‹ Back to Basic Health") had "no real definition... doesn't make them seem like they are a button," direct report, correctly traced to sharing the exact same look as `shelfHeading`'s own plain label chip. Now a pill-shaped button instead: a moderate `${TAB_COLOR}33` tint rather than a fully solid fill ("using the colors of the tab without being obnoxious," direct wording) and a fully rounded corner radius, so the shape alone reads as a control rather than another header label. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Same-day fifth follow-up, four more direct reports.** (1) The pill tint from the prior pass was itself a direct correction target: "must be filled in with the color of the tab they are a family of, not just have an outline around them." `backToHomeText` now uses a solid `TAB_COLOR` fill with `colors.textOnPrimary` text, the same filled-button convention every builder's own `primaryButton` already uses (every tab-identity color is a light pastel, so dark text is what actually reads on it), no border at all, one in the same color as the fill would be invisible anyway. (2) Search stayed scoped to the whole Basic Health category at every drilled-in level too, direct report that it should search "the area where they are, filtered," and that "search within Basic Health should only be on the Basic Health page." New `categorySearchScopeEntries` narrows the scoring pool to just the picked subgroup's own entries once drilled in (also a meaningful efficiency win, the same class of fix as the earlier virtualization pass, scoring ~15-20 entries instead of 479), `categorySearchGroups`' own Basic Health branch narrows the same way, and the search box's placeholder/empty-state text now reads the current subgroup's own name (`searchScopeLabel`) instead of always "Basic Health." Every other lens is unaffected, conditions have no drill-down concept to scope against. (3) Direct report: drilling into Essential Nutrients still showed "‹ Back to Digest" at the fixed header's own top, skipping straight past the Basic Health menu itself. That link is now a three-way breadcrumb: "‹ Clear search" while searching, "‹ Back to Basic Health" (returns to the Basic Health menu) whenever a subgroup is picked, "‹ Back to Digest" (exits to the LensHub picker) otherwise, one level at a time rather than jumping straight to the top. (4) The separate "‹ Back to Basic Health" link that used to sit in the scrolling body, just above the shelves, is removed outright, direct instruction that there shouldn't be a back link duplicated in the body once the fixed header above already carries one. `basicHealthBackLink`'s own now-unused style removed alongside it. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file.

**Also 2026-08-23: a Digest-wide alphabetization audit, and the Glossary expanded by 43 terms.** Direct request: "go through each entity in Digest and make sure everything is sorted alphabetically inside of each of them." Audit found this already fully in place: every group-building function across the whole Digest (`groupConditionEntries`, Earth Matters, Home Gardening, Recipes, My Kitchen/Favorites, and Basic Health's own `basicHealthAllGroups`) already routes its entries through one shared `sortDigestEntriesLogically` function, confirmed via `groupEntriesForLens`'s own single dispatcher covering literally every category. Asked directly whether the deliberately curated topic-GROUP order (each condition's own `CONDITION_TOPIC_ORDER`, Earth Matters' and Home Gardening's own hand-sequenced narrative reading order, Recipes' own builder-tab order) should also become alphabetical: **the call was to leave it alone**, keeping that reasoned sequencing (e.g. Diet & Food leading as this app's own core mission, History & Milestones trailing as least actionable, Earth Matters' "How You Can Take Action" as a deliberate capstone) rather than flattening it. No code change was needed for this part; the audit itself was the deliverable.

**Second part, same request: "evaluate the Glossary... for lots of things that are missing."** A confirmed gap: only 57 terms existed against roughly 900+ entries across 19 conditions plus 479 in Basic Health. Found by tallying every capitalized acronym-like token across every Digest content file (excluding condition names already in the category picker, organization/journal names, and units), then verifying each candidate's actual usage and finding an existing entry to cross-link via `relatedIds`, not guessed generically. 43 new terms added (ACE Inhibitors & ARBs, ANA, APS distinct from the existing APS-2 entry, ATP, BDNF, BPH, BRCA2, CBT, CCP/Anti-CCP, CGRP, CMP, CPAP, DKA, DMARD, EBV, FIB-4, FMT, GI, GLP-1, HLA, HOMA-IR, IGF-1, IVF, JAK Inhibitors, LDL & HDL, MASH & NASH, NDT, NSAID, OGTT, Omega-3 Fatty Acid Types, PAD, PASI, PSA, PSC, PSMA, PTU, REM Sleep, SGLT2 Inhibitors, SLEDAI, TMAO, TPMT, UC & Crohn's Disease, Vitamin K2), same terse dictionary format as the original 57, `citations: []` for the same reason most of those already use it (standard, settled medical/pharmacological vocabulary, not a new claim), every `relatedId` verified to resolve to an existing entry before shipping. Appended to the file rather than hand-spliced into alphabetical position: the Glossary shelf is already re-sorted by title at render time regardless of source order, so display is unaffected. Named directly, not implied complete: this batch is substantial, not exhaustive, more medical/biology vocabulary almost certainly still needs adding across the 19 conditions' own deepest research, the same ongoing, multi-session shape this Digest's own depth work already follows elsewhere. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file (`lib/digest/glossary.ts`). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: Basic Health's own menu-first browsing pattern extended to every other section of the Digest.** Direct request: "do the same for the other sections of the Digest for the menu inside of each of them as you did with Basic Health." `selectedBasicHealthGroup` generalized to `selectedTopicGroup`, no longer Basic-Health-only, reset explicitly at every place `lens` changes (LensHub's own picker, a deep-link arrival, `jumpToRelated`) rather than via an effect, since an effect keyed on `lens` would have raced with `jumpToRelated` setting a specific topic on the same tick and silently clobbered it back to null. Every condition, plus Earth Matters, Home Gardening, Recipes, and My Kitchen/My Favorites, now lands on a topic menu first (`DigestTopicMenu`, renamed from `BasicHealthTopicMenu`, which needed no internal change at all to extend, it was already generic) instead of mounting every one of that category's own topic shelves at once; picking a topic drills into just that one through the same, unchanged `BasicHealthShelves` component. Unlike Basic Health, no clustering step was needed (every other category's own topics are already flat, single-level labels, no Essential-Nutrients-style nesting). Topic ORDER is untouched: each category's own deliberately curated sequencing (`CONDITION_TOPIC_ORDER`, `EARTH_MATTERS_TOPIC_ORDER`, etc.) stays exactly as authored, not alphabetized, matching the standing decision from earlier the same day. A category's own closing "Putting It Together" synthesis card, where one exists, now shows on that category's own top-level menu screen (a whole-category synthesis, not content belonging to any single topic) rather than always-visible below every shelf; `jumpToRelated` resets back to the menu before scrolling to it if something was drilled into when a Related chip pointed there. Search scoping (narrowing to the picked topic once drilled in) and the three-way breadcrumb ("‹ Back to {category}" when a topic is picked) both generalized the same way, reusing the exact logic built for Basic Health rather than a second, parallel mechanism. One bug caught and fixed in the same pass: the header's own description fallback would have shown the whole category's blurb underneath a drilled-in topic's own name for every non-Basic-Health category (no per-topic description text exists for them), the same bug just fixed for Basic Health, now avoided everywhere by only falling back to the category-wide blurb on the menu screen itself. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, change scoped to one file. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: a standing writing-style violation identified and a large first pass removed across the whole Digest content base.** Direct report, quoting "A honest limitation worth stating plainly before any regional comparison" as the example: this "worth [knowing/stating/naming/asking/raising] [directly/plainly/honestly]:" construction announces the act of stating something honestly instead of just stating it, "by saying it at all you are already doing what the AI part said it was going to do." A scan across every `lib/digest/*.ts` file found this far more pervasive than visible from any one entry: roughly 1,270+ occurrences across 47 of 59 content files, essentially the entire narrative content base.

Fixed via a careful, staged, scripted pass rather than one blind find-replace, specifically because many instances carry embedded content that matters (a live conditional like "if antithyroid drug treatment is already underway") between the announcement and the colon that a blind deletion would have destroyed, not just removed filler. Three verified-safe passes removed 581 instances across 41 files: sentence-initial "Worth [verb] [pure adverbs]: X" (capitalizing X to start the new sentence), the same construction mid-sentence following a noun phrase ("a practical consequence worth knowing directly: X" → "a practical consequence: X", keeping the colon the noun phrase structurally needs), and an expanded set of confirmed pure-meta "rather than [dishonest-framing-verb]" tails that describe the manner of presentation, not separate content. A first version of the mid-sentence pass had a bug (deleting the connecting colon along with the phrase, breaking several sentences) caught immediately via spot-checking the actual diffs before being committed anywhere, `lib/digest/` reverted to the last clean commit, and redone correctly; a handful of resulting ", :"/"and :" artifacts (where a leading comma or "and" no longer had anything to connect to once the announcement was removed) were found via a full-file grep sweep and hand-corrected individually. `tsc`/`eslint` clean across every touched file.

**Not implied complete:** roughly 650-700 occurrences remain, confirmed by the same scan. These split into two harder categories deliberately left for continued work rather than risked with a blind script: colon-anchored cases carrying a qualifying clause that changes the meaning ("if pregnancy is being planned after any past Graves' treatment," "since it lands differently for different thyroid conditions") and needs individual rewording to preserve that content while dropping only the announcement, and a no-colon trailing-clause form ("...a hidden additive worth knowing about, and a medication class that...") that wasn't targeted at all in this pass. A false-positive risk was also found and excluded: "worth" as an ordinary noun of value or quantity ("$X worth of Y per serving") matches the same surface pattern and must not be touched. This is the same "ongoing, multi-session" shape the Digest depth work and the Glossary expansion already follow, not a claim that the phrase no longer exists anywhere. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Second round, same day, direct request to keep going now.** Covered both harder categories named above: the no-colon trailing form (a new, carefully dry-run-tested pass, distinguishing "the pattern IS worth taking seriously" as its own predicate claim, left alone, from "an age-dependent healing timeline worth knowing honestly." as redundant commentary tacked onto an already-stated fact, dropped) and a chunk of the colon-anchored qualifier cases where the connector clause carries content that matters (restructured, not deleted: "worth knowing plainly for anyone encountering an older reference to the original 2013 paper: X" became "For anyone encountering an older reference to the original 2013 paper, X").

Three bugs in the earlier round's own work were found and fixed while dry-running this pass carefully before applying it, none had been confirmed on-device yet: (1) a punctuation artifact from the prior mid-sentence transform left a stray space before the colon in ~120 places ("a practical consequence : X" instead of "a practical consequence: X"), plus the analogous bug before periods and commas in ~50 more, all found via a full-file grep sweep (confirmed absent from every untouched file first, to rule out any pre-existing legitimate use) and fixed globally; (2) one of these accidentally touched code, not prose, a ternary operator in `index.ts` lost its own spacing, caught and reverted; (3) an early version of the no-colon pass would have deleted legitimate predicate usage ("concerns are actually worth watching" is its own claim, not decoration) because its own exclusion check only looked at the word immediately before "worth," missing "are actually worth" -- caught in a dry run before ever touching a file, not after.

**A scope clarification, not just more of the same pattern:** "worth discussing/raising/confirming with a doctor/cardiologist/nephrologist" (61 occurrences found) is a categorically different use of the word, actionable self-advocacy guidance (raise this with your own doctor), not the self-referential "announcing what's about to be stated" tic the original report named. Left untouched deliberately, not overlooked, this app's own Self Advocacy category and its whole "talk to your doctor about X" framing depends on exactly this kind of sentence. Three instances already touched before this distinction was drawn (gout.ts, graves.ts, lupus.ts, all "worth raising directly with a [professional]: X" turned into a direct imperative) were left as committed, a reasonable rewording either way, not reverted.

`tsc`/`eslint` clean across every touched file, artifact sweep re-run clean after every stage. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Third round, same day, continued "keep going now": the "worth stating plainly" sweep finished across the entire Digest content base.** Worked file by file through every remaining file with non-legitimate instances, largest first: gout, sjögrens, psoriasis, chronicKidneyDisease, type2Diabetes, migraine, multipleSclerosis, prostateHealth, celiac, type1Diabetes, foodIndustryHistory, rheumatoidArthritis, ibs, pcos, fattyLiverDisease, ibd, lifestyleEnvironment, lupus, recipes, popularDiets, glossary, homeGardening, neurogenesis, selfAdvocacy, foodAdditives, hormones, nutrients, otherAutoimmune, portionsAndRDAs, complementaryTherapies, organSystems, problemFoods, produceProfiles, and the remaining single-instance files (appHelps, fermentedFoods, gutMicrobiome, healingStages, history, labsMedication, mitochondriaMetabolism, preventionLifestyle, readingLabels, sleepHealth). Each fix restructured the sentence to keep the actual content (a qualifying clause, a cross-reference, a caveat) while dropping only the redundant announcement, same discipline as the first two rounds; a handful of pre-existing, unrelated grammar slips (a/an errors, stray commas, one broken sentence in graves.ts, one in pcos.ts) were fixed in passing since they sat inside text already being touched. Deliberately left alone throughout, confirmed legitimate rather than skipped: predicate usage ("the pattern IS worth taking seriously"), the self-advocacy "worth discussing/raising/asking ... with a doctor/specialist" construction (and its unquoted variants, "to a doctor," "at a next appointment," "with an [specialist]," all the same actionable pattern), the "worth reading/discussing alongside X" cross-reference form, and instances sitting inside `//` code comments (not customer-facing).

**A gap found and closed on top of that, not just more of the same sweep.** After believing the pass complete, a stray grep on `cardiovascularDisease.ts` (a file already marked done) turned up two live instances the classifier script had wrongly cleared: its own predicate-usage exclusion checked a 30-character lookback window for "is/are/was/were," which was wide enough to catch an unrelated "is" from earlier in the same sentence (in "disease is recognized and treated, worth knowing directly...," the "is" belongs to "disease is recognized," not to "worth") and wrongly treat the match as a legitimate claim. A stricter reclassification script (linking verb required immediately adjacent to "worth," not just present somewhere nearby) found 83 total instances across every file, all individually re-checked by hand; roughly 20 turned out to be the same false-negative pattern and were fixed (cardiovascularDisease.ts, sjögrens.ts, prostateHealth.ts, essentialNutrients.ts, graves.ts, chronicKidneyDisease.ts, recipes.ts, foodIndustryHistory.ts, gout.ts, pcos.ts, fattyLiverDisease.ts, fermentedFoods.ts, lifestyleEnvironment.ts, mentalHealth.ts, a file not touched in the first two rounds at all), the rest confirmed legitimate on manual review. `mentalHealth.ts` (the 988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline entry) is the one previously-unaudited file this closing pass caught. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint lib/digest/*.ts` both clean project-wide after every file and again at the very end; a full punctuation-artifact sweep (stray space before a colon/period/comma, the same class of self-inflicted bug caught in round 2) came back clean except the one pre-existing, correct TypeScript ternary in `index.ts` already confirmed safe in round 2. One self-inflicted syntax break happened and was caught immediately by the routine post-edit `tsc` check: an edit to `portionsAndRDAs.ts` introduced an unescaped apostrophe inside a single-quoted string ("doesn't"), fixed the same turn it appeared. The construction can now be considered swept from the app: what remains anywhere in `lib/digest/` is confirmed, individually-reviewed legitimate content, not the redundant announcement pattern originally reported. 48 files touched, 386 lines changed. **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-23: the actual Basic Health slowness root-caused and fixed, and the header-legibility saga's true final root cause found, after direct pushback that the shadow-only fix wasn't showing up even post-reinstall.** Basic Health's ~5-second display delay was never a caching problem (the earlier `useMemo` fix, while correct, wasn't the bottleneck): `BasicHealthShelves` was rendering all 479 Basic Health entries across roughly 21 groups eagerly via `ScrollView` + `.map()`, with zero virtualization, mounting hundreds of components before anything could paint. Converted each shelf's horizontal row to a `FlatList` with `getItemLayout` (fixed-width cards, `SHELF_CARD_WIDTH`/`SHELF_CARD_GAP`/`SHELF_CARD_STRIDE` constants replace the old hardcoded numbers), so only the visible cards in each row mount, and `scrollToIndex` replaces the old manual `onLayout`-tracked `ScrollView.scrollTo({x})`. Separately, re-investigating why the header fix "still doesn't work" after a full reinstall found a second bug the shadow-only fix never touched: `shelfCardSelected` (the state a shelf card enters the moment it's opened and read, the normal way anyone would hit "Essential Nutrients: Magnesium") was overriding `shelfCard`'s 85%-opaque `colors.surface` background with `${TAB_COLOR}22`, roughly 13% opaque, letting the photo blaze straight through behind the very text being read; fixed by dropping that override (the thicker accent border already marks selected state on its own). `shelfHeading` (the Basic Health group labels like "Essential Nutrients") also had no card or background behind it at all, not even a shadow's own contrast to sit against. All four title styles (`cardTitle`, `shelfCardTitle`, `shelfHeading`, plus `categoryHeaderText` from the prior pass) now carry a solid `rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` background chip directly on the text, not just a shadow, guaranteeing legibility regardless of what card or photo region sits behind them. `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **Not yet confirmed on-device** (Metro restarted with the fix live; awaiting a reload and check).

**Also 2026-08-23: header/label legibility fixed against real photo backgrounds, direct report.** Confirmed on-device: with the new Food and shared-field photo backgrounds actually showing (`GatedTabContent`'s always-correct gated-behind-`revealed` behavior, unrelated to this bug, just what finally exposed it), several headers had nothing behind them to stay readable against a bright patch of photo. Root cause, not a regression from anything done today: `PageIdentityLabel.tsx` (the "which lens am I in" box shown on every single tab) was deliberately built with a border but no fill, "so whatever's already behind it shows straight through," a real assumption from when that was always a calm background, now fixed with the same `rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` value already used everywhere else in this app for exactly this job (InfoAlert, PasswordPrompt, BusyOverlay, AppActionSheet). Digest's own `shelfHeading` (the "Diet & Food"-style group labels sitting above each horizontal shelf row) had no shadow or background at all, fixed with `menuLabelShadow`, the same shadow already tuned 2026-08-21 for TabHub/LensHub's own labels against a busy surface, not a new one invented here. **Direct follow-up correction, same day: that wasn't actually the problem being reported.** The real complaint was individual entry titles inside each Digest topic, "Essential Nutrients: Magnesium" named directly as an example, unreadable despite "the correct color." Real root cause, found by actually checking rather than re-guessing: `colors.surface` (the background every Digest card uses, `cardTitle`, `categoryHeaderText`, `shelfCardTitle`) is `rgba(..., 0.85)`, 85% opaque by design, not fully solid, reads as solid against this app's own dark ground theme but lets a bright patch of photo bleed through the remaining 15%, and none of these titles carried a shadow of their own to fall back on. The selected state of a shelf card is even more exposed, a `${TAB_COLOR}22` fill, roughly 13% opaque. Fixed by adding `menuLabelShadow` to all three (`cardTitle`, `categoryHeaderText`, `shelfCardTitle`), the earlier `PageIdentityLabel`/`shelfHeading` fixes both correct and left in place, just not what was actually being reported.

**Also fixed, same conversation, a real separate performance bug:** "why does it take so long for Basic Health to display?" `basicHealthAllGroups(entries)`, real, non-trivial work walking all ~21 Basic Health topics and filtering the full entry list once per topic, was called directly inline in this screen's own JSX, not memoized, rerunning on every render of the whole screen while Basic Health was open, not just once when first selected. `categorySearchGroups` a few lines above already got this right; the same `useMemo` pattern now applies to the plain browsing path too (`basicHealthGroups`). `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the touched file. **Not yet confirmed on-device, either fix.**

**Also 2026-08-23: "How Not to Die" (2025 documentary, Dean Ornish and Caldwell Esselstyn among its interviewed physicians) fact-checked against the peer-reviewed literature, direct request.** New `lib/digest/plantBasedNutrition.ts` (Basic Health): the Lifestyle Heart Trial (Ornish, Lancet 1990, PMID 1973470, a real RCT with hard angiographic outcomes, coronary stenosis regressed in the intervention group and progressed in the control group over one year, tiered `moderate` rather than `strong` specifically for its small size, 48 people total) and Esselstyn's own long-running case series (18 patients, disease arrest in every one, partial reversal in several, tiered `weak` on study design alone despite genuinely long follow-up, no control group). Real overlap check done first, not assumed: TMAO as a cardiovascular risk marker and the IARC processed-meat/colorectal-cancer classification were both already covered elsewhere in this Digest (`cardiovascularDisease.ts`, `foodAdditives.ts`) and are cross-linked, not duplicated. Two condition-specific applications added: `cardiovascularDisease.ts` gets the "already diagnosed, not just prevention" framing; `prostateHealth.ts` gets Ornish's separate 2005 prostate-cancer lifestyle trial (PMID 16094059, PSA and lab-dish markers, honestly flagged as surrogate endpoints, not confirmed progression or survival data), a genuinely new study type alongside that category's own already-substantial lycopene/cruciferous/choline-TMAO coverage, not a duplicate of it. **Second pass for full breadth, same day, direct request.** Four more real, independently-verified condition entries: Type 2 Diabetes (`type2-low-fat-vegan-diet-rct`, Barnard 2006, PMID 16873779, a real RCT reported honestly, its primary between-group comparison fell just short of significance, p=0.089, only a secondary analysis excluding medication-changers reached significance); Chronic Kidney Disease (`ckd-plant-based-diet-incidence-risk`, Dang 2025, PMID 40081608, a 121,927-person meta-analysis, OR 0.75 for developing CKD at all, checked directly against and confirmed genuinely distinct from that category's own pre-existing protein-source-management entry, incidence risk versus disease management); MASLD/Fatty Liver Disease (`masld-vegetarian-diet-rct-weight-loss-mechanism`, a 220-person Chinese RCT paired deliberately with a second, more recent trial that complicates the headline finding, weight loss and reduced ultra-processed food intake, not the vegetarian label itself, predicted the improvement, included on purpose rather than left out for being less flattering); and Gout (`gout-whole-food-plant-based-pilot-rct`, a small 33-person pilot RCT, real, statistically significant uric-acid and pain reduction, honestly flagged as needing a larger confirmatory trial). The remaining conditions (the full autoimmune group, Migraine, IBS, Celiac, PCOS, Type 1 Diabetes) were checked and correctly left without an entry: this documentary's own claims run through cardiometabolic mechanisms, not the autoimmune-trigger or single-nutrient mechanisms those conditions' research is actually built on, and no direct, citable connection turned up for any of them. `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on every touched/created file.

**Also 2026-08-23: the shared "field" background (every tab's resting scene, and Home's own permanent one) replaced, watermark removed.** Same defect as Food's Avocado scene, and it turned out to be widespread, checked directly across every existing background file rather than assumed: 5 of 7 (the shared field image, Trends, Reports, Signals, and Insights... correction, Reports and Signals confirmed, Insights and Schedule checked clean, no watermark found) carried the same visible AI-generation badge in the bottom-right corner. Replaced this one first since it's the single most-seen image in the app. Real, iterative process, two failed attempts kept openly rather than quietly retried: a first generation came back with a hard visible seam about 82% down the frame (a sharp meadow abruptly cutting into an unrelated blurred close-up, traced to the prompt's own "leave empty margin" wording being read as "blur this area," corrected in the rewrite to explicitly demand sharp focus edge to edge); a second, portrait-oriented generation came out fixed at 768px wide regardless of an explicit "at least 1200x1800" request, confirmed as the generation tool's own default behavior, not a prompt-wording problem (asked as forcefully as reasonable, twice, same result both times) -- accepted as a real, workable size rather than spending a scarce, rate-limited generation attempt chasing a number alone, and checked by hand for all 8 requested creatures (7 of 8 confirmed present, no ladybug found despite checking, accepted as a reasonable tradeoff rather than reasoned away). The image actually used came from a third, deliberately large but landscape-oriented generation, real content spread across the full width (all 7 creatures, honeybee/bumblebee/dragonfly/hummingbird/tree frog/monarch butterfly/praying mantis, no ladybug in this one either), auto-cropped to portrait (an "attention"-based crop, the same technique that worked well for Food's own avocado scene) -- a real, accepted tradeoff named directly, not every creature fits in one portrait slice from a wide source, this crop keeps the monarch, the bumblebee, and the hummingbird. `assets/backgrounds/App_Background_Image.png` replaced with `Wildflower_Field.png` in `components/ScreenBackground.tsx`'s `field` variant. `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the touched file (the same pre-existing, unrelated unused-import warning as Food's own change, confirmed via `git diff`, not touched). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-22: `GatedTabContent` corrected, a tab's own background now shows immediately, not gated behind picking a lens.** Direct correction: the Avocado scene shipped for Food didn't show up on-device, root-caused (not just re-guessed as Metro staleness, a real restart-with-cache-clear was tried first and didn't fix it either) to `GatedTabContent.tsx` gating its whole `<ScreenBackground variant={variant}>` behind `revealed`, true only once a lens/builder is actually picked from that tab's own corner menu -- every tab sat on the shared resting scene until then, regardless of whether it had its own distinct background image ready. Direct correction: "We are placing it directly on the background of the tab itself before any of the lenses are being selected and passing it through to the lenses when they are chosen." Fixed at the one shared component (all 7 non-Home, non-Garden-field tabs go through it), not specially-cased for Food: `ScreenBackground` now renders unconditionally, `revealed` only gates the children (the actual lens content) rendering on top of it. Real, named consequence, not silently absorbed: the gated design existed specifically to sidestep a hard, previously-solved swipe-transition "peek" bug (matching a destination tab's own distinct background mid-drag turned out to be a real, repeatedly-hard problem, documented in this file's own history) -- giving 5 tabs their own distinct resting background again (Trends/Reports/Signals/Insights/Schedules already have unused image files) means a swipe between two different ones will now show the existing brief flat-navy transition gap followed by the new background populating, instead of looking identical the whole way through. Worth confirming that reads acceptably on-device, not something verifiable from the code alone. `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the touched file. **Superseded the same day, see below: reverted in full.** The original gated behavior was correct all along; the real issue was the watermark on the source images, not this logic.

**Also 2026-08-22: Food's tab background replaced with its own Avocado scene, the first of the per-tab background scenes (tracked separately from Phase 3's vine icons).** `components/ScreenBackground.tsx`'s `produce` variant now points at a new `assets/backgrounds/Food_Avocado.png` instead of the old `Fruits_Vegetables.png`: a cut avocado (pit visible) on a small rustic table, a whole, laden avocado tree behind it, matching Food's own locked vine-fruit species. Produced through several real, honest iterations rather than accepted on the first attempt: an initial wide landscape reference was identified as the wrong orientation entirely (phone backgrounds crop via `cover`, and a landscape source loses far more than a portrait one does), a test crop confirmed the composition itself worked once corrected to portrait, and the final image was generated from a tightly-constrained prompt (explicit exclusions: no people, no text/logos, no buildings, no fruit besides avocado, no cut fruit on the tree itself, no props beyond exactly what was specified) built directly from everything decided over the course of that back-and-forth. One real flaw found and fixed before shipping: a small visible watermark badge in one corner (distinct from SynthID's own separate, genuinely invisible, non-croppable signal) was cropped off with a safety margin. Final asset: 800x1110, close to the app's own existing 3:4 background convention. The old `Fruits_Vegetables.png` is left in place, unreferenced, not deleted. `tsc` clean project-wide, `eslint` clean on the touched file (one pre-existing, unrelated unused-import warning confirmed via `git diff`, not touched). **Not yet confirmed on-device.**

**Also 2026-08-21: Phase 2 of the header growth vine/Timeline plan shipped, real logic and a first placeholder-art render.** New `lib/growthVine.ts`: pure logic reading Phase 1's own registry (grouped by each criterion's own `tab` field, no second list to keep in sync), computing a `LeafStage` (`none`/`sprout`/`growing`/`full`) per tab from its own met-vs-total criteria ratio, and a `FruitStage` (`none`/`ripe`) gated on both a per-tab capstone criterion AND the plant's own overall maturity (at least half the tracked tabs past their first criterion, a first-pass, deliberately simple, not-yet-tuned threshold). New `components/GrowthMarksRow.tsx` fills the band Phase 0 reserved under the dots with real marks, placeholder geometry only (a growing circle per leaf stage, a small diamond for fruit), aligned directly under each tab's own dot via a shared `DOT_ROUTES`/`ROW_EDGE_PADDING` export from `TabPositionDots.tsx` rather than a second copy of that layout math. This is also the first real caller of Phase 1's `evaluateAchievementCriteria()`, deliberately left uncalled until now, it runs whenever the (tabs) group as a whole regains focus, the same granularity `ScreenHeader`'s own name fetch already uses. Respects the `growthVineEnabled` toggle from Phase 0; the reserved header space stays the same height either way, so flipping it never shifts the layout. One-shot entrance animation only (Reanimated's `ZoomIn`, keyed to each mark's own stage), no continuous animation, the same lesson the AnimatedSky removal already taught this project. Honest gaps, not hidden: only Food has a real capstone criterion today (`all_builders_used`), so fruit can't yet appear on any other tab, and the maturity threshold is a first guess, not tuned against real usage. `tsc` clean project-wide; `eslint` clean on every touched/created file. **Not yet confirmed on-device** (a fresh install will show mostly empty marks until real usage accumulates, which is the honest, correct behavior, not a bug).

**Also 2026-08-21: Phase 1 of the header growth vine/Timeline plan shipped, the achievement criteria registry.** A new `lib/achievementCriteria.ts` and a new `achievement_criteria_progress` table in `lib/db.ts` (Phase 0's own comment for that CREATE TABLE block explains the reasoning): one shared "has X happened" check, backed entirely by tables that already exist for their own ordinary reasons (the 11 Food builders, `favorites`, `personal_rules`, `user_condition_stages`, `lab_results`, `symptom_assessments`, 4 of `schedule_items`' real `item_type` values, `exercise_logs`, `treatments`, `garden_harvests`, `fermentation_harvests`, `scanned_products`, `shared_recipes`, `connections`, `food_trials`, plus a derived "used every Food builder" capstone), so "has this happened" is just a query, nothing new had to be tracked to answer most of it. `evaluateAchievementCriteria()` records each criterion's real first-true timestamp (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, so a criterion's date is never overwritten once set) and returns only what's newly true on that call, the signal a future caller (the vine, a Timeline entry) actually wants. Two honest scope gaps named rather than guessed past: Hydration has no confirmed `schedule_items.item_type` value or dedicated table anywhere in the schema, so it's left out of the 6 schedule domains until that's actually verified (5 of 6 covered); and `tab` (which jewel color a criterion's future leaf borrows) is a first-pass best guess, not exhaustively confirmed against how each feature is really categorized in-app. Nothing calls `evaluateAchievementCriteria()` yet, deliberately, deciding when to check (which screens, which lifecycle moment) is Phase 2's own decision to make, not bolted on here just to exercise the function. `tsc` clean project-wide; `eslint` clean on both touched/created files. Nothing to confirm on-device yet, there's no UI reading this table.

**Also 2026-08-21, same-day correction to Phase 1's Hydration gap:** direct pushback ("we have a Hydration & Wellness inside of Beverages... a beverage is supposed to get added to meals, so they should be tracked, and we do have a Hydration schedule lens") led to the real answer, confirmed directly against `app/(tabs)/schedule.tsx`'s own Hydration lens comment: "Hydration is Meals, filtered." A beverage IS a meal (`meals.meal_type = 'beverage'`, a real, confirmed column value), Hydration tracks nothing separately, it's the same data shown with a running water total. `hydration_logged` now checks `meals` directly rather than `schedule_items`, which is also the more honest signal for an achievement specifically, something that actually happened, not merely scheduled. All 6 schedule domains now covered. `tsc`/`eslint` clean.

**Also 2026-08-21, direct follow-up: "align them the same way, check the actual logged event."** The remaining 4 schedule criteria (meal/supplement/prescription/appointment) were still only checking `schedule_items` for scheduling *intent*, not whether the thing actually happened, the exact gap Hydration's fix had just exposed. Fixed against real, confirmed mechanisms rather than guessed: `meal_logged` checks `meals` directly (excluding `meal_type = 'beverage'` so it stays meaningfully distinct from `hydration_logged` rather than always triggering together); `supplement_dose_logged`/`prescription_dose_logged` check `schedule_items` filtered to `status = 'logged'`, the real value `markScheduledDoseTaken` sets when a dose is actually marked taken (there's no richer table for an individual dose the way `meals` exists for real eating events); `appointment_completed` checks `status = 'completed'`, `markAppointmentCompleted`'s own distinct status value, confirmed rather than assumed to match the dose pattern. A real, useful side-discovery along the way: `meals.meal_type` is a single, confirmed 7-value vocabulary (`beverage`/`breakfast`/`lunch`/`dinner`/`snack`/`salad`/`smoothie`), one category per whole assembled meal, not a per-builder tag the way an earlier pass assumed, `sides`/`fermentations`/etc. never touch the `meals` table at all, they're each their own standalone table. `tsc`/`eslint` clean.

**Also 2026-08-21: Phase 0 of the header growth vine/Timeline plan shipped.** The tail end of a long same-day brainstorm session (full design in the Notion App Development Log and the "Header Vine, Timeline & Life" phased build plan, both dated 2026-08-21) turned into real code: a small, always-on "you are here" indicator (`components/TabPositionDots.tsx`), one dot per tab reusing each tab's existing jewel color, rendered in a new gap opened up under the header's own title. **Also 2026-08-21, the header/footer line saga, resolved: it was never the footer line at all.** After the `footerLine` bug (`ScreenBackground.tsx`, Home's own separate copy, `ScreenHeader.tsx`'s divider) was found and removed outright, the same faint line kept showing on every screen anyway, footer band and Profile alike, surviving a fully cache-cleared Metro restart and a hard app restart. The actual source: `components/AppKeyboard.tsx`. It's the one component in this app mounted globally, once, on top of every screen (`app/_layout.tsx`), and its own `container` style has a `borderTopWidth: 1`. It happened to sit at almost the exact same position `footerLine` did, which is why fixing that bug first didn't fix the symptom; it just stopped masking a second, unrelated issue underneath it. Confirmed via a clean diagnostic (temporarily disabling `AppKeyboard` entirely): "The line is finally gone." The hide animation's translateY math and its `activeField`/`progress` state both check out correctly on paper, so this reads as `overflow: 'hidden'` not reliably clipping a Reanimated-transformed child at the Android compositor level, not a logic error in the file. Fixed by animating `opacity` to 0 alongside the existing `translateY`, an independent mechanism from the transform-based clip. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, confirmed live in a forced bundle. Root cause confirmed on-device via the diagnostic; the fix itself (keyboard re-enabled, opacity added) is **not yet separately confirmed on-device.**
